API Reference

Names listed here without a Nbody6Dynamics. prefix are either exported or public; the export list in src/Nbody6Dynamics.jl says which. A public name is documented and stable but is called as Nbody6Dynamics.name.

Pipeline

Nbody6Dynamics.example_input — Function
example_input(name) -> String

Absolute path of a file shipped under the package's input_files/ directory, for a user who installed the package by URL and has no checkout to point a configuration at. name is a path below input_files/ (example_input("engine/N1k_quick.inp"), example_input("showcase/equal_pipeline.toml")); a bare file name is looked up across the sub-directories (example_input("N1k_quick.inp")), which keeps the names of the flat layout before 0.4.0 valid. Raises an ArgumentError when no such file ships, or when a bare name matches in more than one place.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.run_pipeline — Function
run_pipeline(cfg::Nbody6Config; base_dir = cfg.config_dir, run_id = "") -> Dict{Symbol,Any}

Top-level orchestrator that runs the full pipeline (or any subset) based on the config flags. This is the single entry point for config-driven workflows.

Pipeline phases (controlled by config flags)

PhaseControlled byNotes
Install/Buildinstall.enabledClone + compile Nbody6++
Simulationsimulation.run_testRun the N-body integration
Post-processpostprocess.enabledRead output data + sanity checks
Plotsvisualization.enabledGenerate static plots + GIF animations

Post-process–only mode

To run only post-processing and plotting on existing output (from this project or an external source), set in config.toml:

[simulation]
run_test = false          # skip simulation

[postprocess]
enabled   = true
data_dir  = "/path/to/output"   # point at any dir with Nbody6++ files

Then call:

cfg = load_config("config.toml")
results = run_pipeline(cfg)

If data_dir is empty but run_test = false, the most recent run in runs/ is post-processed.

run_id fixes the run directory name (<runs_dir>/<run_id>); when empty, generate_run_id derives it from simulation.run_id_prefix and the time. An explicit run_id whose run directory already holds a RUN_INFO.toml (a run that was started or finished) is refused with an ArgumentError before anything is built or written; such a run is continued with resume_pipeline.

base_dir is the project directory: every relative path of the configuration (install.install_dir, simulation.input_file, simulation.runs_dir, postprocess.data_dir, merger.config_file) resolves against it. It defaults to cfg.config_dir, the directory of the file load_config read, so julia scripts/run_setup.jl path/to/config.toml keeps its engine and its runs next to that file, never inside the package.

A simulation phase that ends on a stop request (simulation.wall_budget, or a signal) ends the pipeline there, with an empty result and no completion stamp; resume_pipeline continues it.

Returns

A Dict{Symbol,Any} with keys :snapshots, :diagnostics, :lagr, :escapers, :stellar_evo, :binary_evo, :stellar_census, :remnant (present only when corresponding data exists). Returns an empty dict if post-processing is disabled.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.resume_pipeline — Function
resume_pipeline(run_dir; base_dir = nothing) -> Dict{Symbol,Any}

Continue the pipeline of an interrupted run from the configuration frozen in run_dir: one more engine segment through resume_run, and, once the run has reached its end time, the post-processing and figure phases with the completion stamp. The call can be repeated: a run that is still short of its end time returns an empty result and waits for the next call, and a run whose pipeline has finished is left alone, so one queue job per segment needs no knowledge of how many segments the run takes.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.run_gpu_validation — Function
run_gpu_validation(; base_dir = pwd(), stages = [:suite, :gpu, :cpu, :bench],
                   bench_n = [20000, 50000, 100000], bench_threads = [4, 8],
                   bench_gpu_lists = nothing, bench_tcrit = 0.25,
                   dry_run = false, stop_on_failure = false) -> String

Run the acceptance sequence of a CUDA host and collect everything under <base_dir>/runs/gpu_validation_<machine>_<timestamp>/, <machine> being the hostname with compact CPU and GPU tags so that machines sharing a hostname stay distinguishable: HOST_INFO.toml (hardware fingerprint with the GPU query, compute capabilities, CUDA path and nvcc release, gcc, gfortran and glibc versions, the host compilers nvcc can be offered and the verdict of the host-compiler probe, Julia, package commit), one <stage>.log per stage, the benchmark CSV, the RUN_INFO.toml and telemetry.csv of every benchmark run, and VALIDATION.toml (command, status, exit code and duration per stage, the run directories the pipelines created). Stages, in order:

  • :suite — the test suite with NBODY6_GPU_TESTS=1 (CUDA build in a temporary tree, N = 1000 on one device, then on two when present);
  • :gpu — input_files/gpu/gpu_pipeline.toml: CUDA build into backend/Nbody6PPGPU-beijing-gpu and the 2 × 25 000-star merger on device 0;
  • :cpu — input_files/gpu/cpu_pipeline.toml: the AVX reference build and the same merger;
  • :bench — bench/gpu_scaling.jl over bench_n × bench_threads × bench_gpu_lists (default [[0]], plus [0, 1] when two devices are visible) for bench_tcrit N-body time units; needs both binaries;
  • :gpu_merger_600k, :gpu_single_600k, :cpu_merger_600k, :cpu_single_600k — the probes above 5×10⁵ bodies, input_files/gpu/<stage>.toml, each on the tree its binary needs; not in the default list.

The probe stages and the benchmark are launched with JULIA_NUM_THREADS set to auto unless the environment sets it, because their initial-condition generation is a threaded pair sum.

Each stage is a separate Julia process whose output is echoed and written to its log with terminal escape sequences removed; a failing stage is recorded and the later stages still run unless stop_on_failure is set. VALIDATION.toml is rewritten after every stage, so an interrupted sequence leaves a readable record.

A stage's verdict rests on its artefacts as well as its exit code: a :gpu, :cpu or probe stage that exits zero without leaving a run directory marked [pipeline] completed is recorded incomplete with the reason, because the run summary is written when the engine exits and a process killed during post-processing or plotting would otherwise pass. Every pipeline stage is launched with a run ID the driver assigns (--run-id, recorded as run_ids in VALIDATION.toml), so the directory that carries the verdict is identified by name and a concurrent run elsewhere under runs/ cannot be mistaken for it. A stage killed by one of retry_signals is run again, up to max_retries times, when it is listed in retry_stages. A retried pipeline stage starts from a clean run directory; the crashed attempt's directory is kept as runs/<run_id>.attempt<k>.signal<N> beside its log. The defaults retry the test suite once after a crash signal (SIGILL, SIGABRT, SIGBUS, SIGSEGV — a compiler crash during JIT is an upstream fault, not a verdict on the package) and nothing else: a pipeline stage is hours of work that is not repeated unasked, and SIGKILL or SIGTERM come from an operator, the out-of-memory killer or a session teardown, which a rerun would only meet again. Every retried signal is recorded with the stage.

With stop_on_failure = true the run is a gated chain: once a stage ends failed or incomplete, every later stage is recorded skipped with the reason after failed stage :<stage> and not run; and when the stage list holds a stage that needs nvcc (the suite, the CUDA build, the CUDA probes) while the host-compiler probe did not pass, every stage is skipped before anything runs, with the probe's verdict as the reason. A dry run plans the commands regardless.

With dry_run = true the host record and the planned commands are written and nothing is executed. Returns the validation directory.

Example

dir = run_gpu_validation(; stages = [:suite, :gpu])
TOML.parsefile(joinpath(dir, "VALIDATION.toml"))["results"]["suite"]["status"]
source
Nbody6Dynamics.postprocess — Function
postprocess(cfg::Nbody6Config; run_dir = "", base_dir = cfg.config_dir) -> Dict{Symbol,Any}

Run all enabled post-processing steps and return collected results.

Data directory resolution (in priority order):

  1. run_dir keyword — run_dir/output/ (from a simulation run)
  2. cfg.postprocess.data_dir — explicit external directory from config.toml, resolved against base_dir when relative
  3. Falls back to the most recent run under base_dir/<runs_dir>/ (via _find_latest_run); errors if none exists.

base_dir defaults to the configuration's config_dir (the directory of the file it was loaded from).

Besides the readers, the derived data products are computed here: the class census (:stellar_census), the integrals of motion of every snapshot (:integrals, SnapshotIntegrals, with postprocess.snapshot_integrals, including the Jacobi integral when the stdout capture echoes a linearised tidal field) and, for a merger run — one whose output holds a merger_summary.txt and at least two snapshots — the remnant diagnostics (:remnant, a RemnantDiagnostics), which reuse the integrals' bound sets. Both need an O(N²) pair sum per snapshot and run only while no snapshot holds more than postprocess.pair_sum_max_n stars. From the stdout capture the stamped event lines (:engine_events, EngineEvent) and the energy-jump localisation (:energy_events, EnergyEventAnalysis, with postprocess.energy_events) follow; with the integrals, the escapers and the counters of postprocess.events_file (:event_counters, EventCounters) the mass budget (:mass_budget, MassBudgetRow, with postprocess.mass_budget). Under a linearised tidal field (tidal_mode :linearised) the engine's DE is not an error measure and no interval is flagged.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.generate_plots — Function
generate_plots(results::Dict{Symbol,Any}, vis::VisualizationConfig;
               sim_dir::AbstractString = "", animations::Bool = true,
               pair_sum_max_n::Integer = PostprocessConfig().pair_sum_max_n,
               bound::NamedTuple = (;))

Generate all available plots (and, when animations = true, GIF animations) from post-processing results, saving into vis.output_dir. sim_dir names the directory holding the raw simulation output; when given, it is searched for merger_summary.txt to produce the merger-specific figures (inter-cluster separation, per-cluster virial ratio).

pair_sum_max_n bounds the merger figures built on O(N²) pair sums, the per-cluster virial ratio and structure, the bound-member density and velocity-dispersion profiles and the remnant figures: they are drawn only when no snapshot holds more particles than this, with a warning otherwise; the inter-cluster separation is drawn regardless. The bound-set selection behind the binary figures' hard/soft scale obeys the same limit, above which the scale is taken over every system. The default is that of PostprocessConfig, and the Nbody6Config method passes cfg.postprocess.pair_sum_max_n. bound holds the keywords max_passes and min_members of the bound-set selections (bound_set); the Nbody6Config method passes postprocess.bound_max_passes and postprocess.bound_min_members.

This is the single plot dispatcher — both the config-driven pipeline (via the Nbody6Config method) and postprocess_external route through it.

source
generate_plots(results::Dict{Symbol,Any}, cfg::Nbody6Config;
               run_dir::AbstractString = "")

Config-driven wrapper around the VisualizationConfig method.

When run_dir is provided (e.g. runs/run_XXXX/), plots are saved to run_dir/<visualization.output_dir>/ (typically runs/run_XXXX/plots/) and run_dir/output/ is searched for merger metadata. Otherwise, falls back to visualization.output_dir relative to the package root.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.load_config — Function
load_config(path::AbstractString) -> Nbody6Config

Parse a TOML configuration file and return a fully-typed Nbody6Config. Missing sections or keys fall back to defaults defined in the @kwdef structs; keys the configuration schema does not define are rejected, so a typo cannot pass silently. The parsed configuration is validated fail-fast by _validate: enumerated choices, numerical bounds, and required non-empty fields raise an ArgumentError naming the offending section.key before any pipeline phase starts. The directory of path is recorded as config_dir: relative paths in the configuration resolve against it, and it is the default base_dir of the entry points, so a project directory holding a config.toml receives its backend/ and runs/ there, wherever the package is installed.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.save_config — Function
save_config(cfg::Nbody6Config, path::AbstractString)

Serialize a config struct back to a TOML file (for frozen run snapshots). config_dir is not written: it is a property of where the file lives. An existing file is backed up, never overwritten, and the write is atomic.

source

Simulation

Nbody6Dynamics.setup_nbody6 — Function
setup_nbody6(cfg::Nbody6Config; base_dir = cfg.config_dir)

Orchestrate the full install pipeline: clone → source amendments → configure → HDF5 patch → CUDA setup → build.

All paths are resolved relative to base_dir (defaults to cfg.config_dir, the directory of the configuration file). An existing checkout is left as it is; when it is not at install.ref a warning says so.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.run_simulation — Function
run_simulation(cfg::Nbody6Config; base_dir = cfg.config_dir, run_id = "") -> String

Run the engine on simulation.input_file in a new directory runs/<run_id>/, through a generated launch script that sets the stack limit and the CUDA environment, with the configuration frozen beside the output. The stdout capture is followed live (ticker, watchdogs, wall budget) and the segment is recorded in RUN_INFO.toml.

simulation.input_file, install.install_dir and simulation.runs_dir resolve against base_dir when relative. A run directory that already holds a RUN_INFO.toml is refused with an ArgumentError: an existing run is continued by resume_run or restart_simulation, never started again over its output. Returns the path to the run directory.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.restart_simulation — Function
restart_simulation(run_dir; tcrit_extra, dump = nothing, tcrtp0 = nothing,
                   base_dir = nothing) -> String

Continue a finished or interrupted run from one of the engine's COMMON dumps for tcrit_extra further N-body time units. The chosen dump (default: the last restartable comm.[12]_<t> in output/, chosen as by resume_run; a dump written during triple, quad or chain regularization, NSUB > 0, is refused with an ArgumentError) is copied to output/comm.1, which is what KSTART = 2 reads, as a working copy that is removed when the engine exits. A restart from a dump older than the last moves what was written after it to output/discarded/segment_<k>/, as resume_run does; that is the call for continuing a run to its original end time. A restart input is written from the run's original input file; the engine runs in the same output directory with stdout and stderr appended, so out1000, lagr.7, esc.11, and the time-stamped snapshot and stellar-evolution files continue. RUN_INFO.toml gains one entry in its segments list per launch and the telemetry of each segment goes to its own CSV. The frozen config.toml of the run carries absolute paths, so the engine tree is found without a base_dir; one may still be given to override it. Returns run_dir.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.resume_run — Function
resume_run(run_dir; base_dir = nothing) -> String

Continue an interrupted run to the end time of its original input. The call is idempotent: a completed run is left as it is, and a run that was stopped at its wall budget, killed, or ended by an engine error gets one more segment, started from the last complete restart dump.

The dump is the last one the engine reported in its stdout whose file is complete, at the exact time of that report. If the interrupted segment wrote anything after that dump (it was killed, not stopped), that part is moved to output/discarded/segment_<k>/ before the launch: the tail of the stdout capture, the later records of lagr.7, global.30, esc.11 and event.35, and the later snapshots and dumps. Appended files the package cannot cut by time are listed in the new segment's record (discarded.uncut). Nothing is deleted.

A run halted by the engine's energy check, terminated by the start-up watchdog or stopped by the stall watchdog is refused: the first needs a decision on the accuracy parameters, the other two would hang again. A run whose record says running is refused while its engine is alive on this host; otherwise the record is closed from what the stdout shows.

Arguments

  • run_dir: run directory of a pipeline run (RUN_INFO.toml, config.toml, output/)
  • base_dir: directory the engine tree is resolved against; default: that of the run's frozen config.toml, which carries absolute paths

Returns

run_dir as an absolute path. Raises an ArgumentError for a run that cannot be resumed, with the reason.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.generate_run_id — Function
generate_run_id(prefix::String = "run") -> String

Generate a unique run identifier: {prefix}_YYYYMMDD_HHMMSS_{4hex}.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.export_for_paper — Function
export_for_paper(paths, dest_dir; run_dir = nothing) -> Vector{String}

Copy finished figures to a manuscript figures directory with provenance attached: each file is copied as <run_id>__<name> and a TOML sidecar <run_id>__<name>.provenance.toml records the producing run, the package and backend commits (from the run's RUNINFO.toml when `rundiris given), and the export date.dest_dir` is caller-supplied — the package never hardcodes paths outside its own tree. Existing files are backed up, never overwritten. Returns the destination paths.

source

Cluster Jobs

Nbody6Dynamics.submit_run — Function
submit_run(cfg; base_dir = cfg.config_dir, run_id = "", dry_run = false)
    -> @NamedTuple{run_dir::String, script::String, commands::Vector{String}, job_ids::Vector{String}}

Create a run and submit its chain of scheduler jobs, one engine segment per job, under the [cluster] settings of cfg (cluster.enabled must be set; the [cluster] checks of the configuration are applied again, so a configuration built in memory is held to them too).

The run directory is <runs_dir>/<run_id> (run_id generated as by run_pipeline when empty); a directory that already holds a RUN_INFO.toml is refused, since a run in progress is continued by its own chain or by resume_pipeline. The configuration is frozen with absolute paths into <run_dir>/cluster/config.toml, which the first job reads, and the job script (job_script) is written to <run_dir>/cluster/job.sbatch.

With chain = "dependency", max_segments jobs are submitted at once, each after the first with --dependency=afterok:<previous job> and --kill-on-invalid-dep=yes, so a failed segment cancels the rest of the chain. With chain = "resubmit" one job is submitted, and each job submits its successor while the run is short of its end time (cluster_segment). Every submission is appended to the job ledgers (<run_dir>/cluster/jobs.csv and <runs_dir>/cluster_jobs.csv, see read_job_ledger) as soon as it is made, so a failure part-way through the chain leaves the submissions already made on record.

With dry_run, nothing is submitted and no ledger row is written; the commands are returned with <job k> placeholders for the dependency ids.

Arguments

  • cfg: the run's configuration, with cluster.enabled = true
  • base_dir: directory the relative paths of cfg resolve against
  • run_id: name of the run directory; empty = generated
  • dry_run: render and write the run's files without submitting

Returns

A named tuple: run_dir, script (path of job.sbatch), commands (the submission command lines) and job_ids (empty for a dry run).

Example

cfg = load_config("run.toml")    # [cluster] enabled = true
sub = submit_run(cfg; dry_run = true)
foreach(println, sub.commands)
sub = submit_run(cfg)
sub.job_ids                           # e.g. ["4711"]
source
Nbody6Dynamics.cluster_segment — Function
cluster_segment(run_dir) -> Dict{Symbol,Any}

Entry of a scheduler job (scripts/run_cluster_segment.jl): run one engine segment of the run in run_dir and, with chain = "resubmit", submit the job of the next one.

The configuration is read from <run_dir>/config.toml when it exists, else from <run_dir>/cluster/config.toml. The latter is the copy frozen at submission and serves only the first job; from the first segment on, <run_dir>/config.toml holds the whole configuration, [cluster] included, and is the single source of truth for both the engine segment and the chain (chain, max_segments, sbatch, the job script). Changes between jobs, such as a different simulation.wall_budget, are therefore made in <run_dir>/config.toml.

Without a RUN_INFO.toml the pipeline is started (run_pipeline with the run id basename(run_dir)), otherwise it is continued (resume_pipeline). The job id is taken from SLURM_JOB_ID (empty outside a job); the ledgers receive a started row before the segment and an ended row with the run status and the segment count after it, or with status error and the exception's message before the exception propagates. The scheduler's job description is kept as cluster/job_<job_id>.txt when scontrol is available.

A successor <sbatch> --parsable --dependency=afterany:<job_id> cluster/job.sbatch is submitted when chain = "resubmit", the run status is none of completed, halted, watchdog, stalled, the pipeline has not finished, and the segment count is below max_segments (0 = no bound); the script is regenerated from the configuration if it is missing. A segment that ended failed or killed without progress, or the second such segment in a row, stops the chain instead (_chain_stop_reason): a warning names the run, status and segment, and the ledgers receive a chain_stopped row with the reason.

Returns

The result of the pipeline call: empty while the run is short of its end time, and once its pipeline has finished.

Example

# what the generated job script runs, within a Slurm job
results = cluster_segment("/scratch/runs/merger_run_20261013_120000_ab12")
source
Nbody6Dynamics.job_script — Function
job_script(cfg, run_dir; base_dir = cfg.config_dir) -> String

Text of the sbatch script that runs one segment of the run in run_dir (resolved against base_dir when relative) under the [cluster] settings of cfg. Nothing is written; submit_run stores the text as <run_dir>/cluster/job.sbatch.

The #SBATCH header carries the job name (cluster.job_name, else the run id), the partition, account, QOS, memory and generic resources when set, the node, task and CPU counts, --exclusive when requested, the time limit, --signal=B:TERM@<stop_margin> (the signal time clamped to 1–65535 s, the range Slurm accepts) and the output file <run_dir>/cluster/slurm_%j.out, followed by the cluster.sbatch_options verbatim. The body runs the cluster.prologue lines, sets JULIA_NUM_THREADS (cluster.julia_threads, or the job's SLURM_CPUS_PER_TASK with cluster.cpus_per_task as the fallback outside a job) and execs Julia on the package's scripts/run_cluster_segment.jl. The exec makes the Julia driver the batch shell itself, so the signal Slurm sends ahead of the time limit reaches the driver, whose exit hook stops the engine with a dump.

Arguments

  • cfg: configuration whose [cluster] and simulation.stop_margin are rendered
  • run_dir: run directory (<runs_dir>/<run_id>)
  • base_dir: directory a relative run_dir is resolved against

Returns

The script text, ending in a newline.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.read_job_ledger — Function
read_job_ledger(path) -> Vector{NamedTuple}

Rows of a job ledger written by submit_run and cluster_segment (<run_dir>/cluster/jobs.csv, or <runs_dir>/cluster_jobs.csv for every run of a runs directory), as named tuples (a Vector of NamedTuples, not a table) with the nine String fields time, run_id, event (submitted, started, ended, chain_stopped), job_id, depends_on, segment, status, host and note; empty cells read as "". An absent file gives an empty vector. Raises an ArgumentError when the header is not the nine column names in this order or a row has another number of cells.

Example

rows = read_job_ledger(joinpath(run_dir, "cluster", "jobs.csv"))
[r.job_id for r in rows if r.event == "submitted"]
source

Cost Model

Nbody6Dynamics.CostEstimate — Type
CostEstimate

Estimated cost of one engine launch, from estimate_cost.

Fields

  • n: particle number
  • span_nb: integrated span [N-body time units]
  • rate_s_per_nb: wall seconds per N-body time unit, merger factor included
  • wall_s: estimated wall time [s]
  • segments: engine segments the wall time needs under simulation.wall_budget (1 without a budget)
  • dump_bytes: restart dumps [bytes]
  • snapshot_bytes: conf.3 snapshots [bytes]
  • text_bytes: sev.83 and bev.82 output [bytes]
  • disk_bytes: the sum of the three [bytes]
  • budget_hours: the configured budget (cost.budget_hours) [h]; 0 = none
  • exceeds: whether wall_s exceeds a non-zero budget
  • basis: how the rate was obtained from the table (interpolated or extrapolated, with the merger factor when applied)
source
Nbody6Dynamics.estimate_cost — Function
estimate_cost(cfg; n, span_nb, nnbopt = 0, dtadj = 0.0, deltat = 0.0,
              merger = cfg.merger.enabled) -> CostEstimate

Wall time, segments and disk of an engine run of n bodies over span_nb N-body time units.

The rate is cost_rate on cfg.cost.table (the built-in H200 table when empty), multiplied by cfg.cost.merger_factor for a two-cluster configuration (merger); the wall time is the rate times span_nb. With a non-zero simulation.wall_budget the number of segments is the wall time over wall_budget − stop_margin, rounded up; otherwise 1.

The disk is the sum of

  • restart dumps: checkpoint_keep of them when it is non-zero, otherwise one per adjustment interval dtadj (⌊span_nb/dtadj⌋ + 1), at least one terminal dump; each of the size of _checkpoint_dump_bytes(n, nnbopt);
  • conf.3 snapshots, 40 bytes per body at each of ⌊span_nb/deltat⌋ + 1 outputs (none when deltat = 0);
  • sev.83 and bev.82 text, about 130 bytes per star at each output.

exceeds is set when cfg.cost.budget_hours > 0 and the wall time is longer than the budget.

Arguments

  • cfg: the configuration ([cost], simulation.wall_budget, simulation.stop_margin, simulation.checkpoint_keep)
  • n: particle number (an integral value)
  • span_nb: integrated span [N-body time units], > 0
  • nnbopt: target neighbour number of the dump estimate, ≥ 0
  • dtadj, deltat: adjustment and snapshot intervals [N-body time units], ≥ 0; 0 = not known
  • merger: apply cost.merger_factor

Example

e = estimate_cost(cfg; n = 500_000, span_nb = 100, nnbopt = 300, dtadj = 1.0, deltat = 10.0)
e.wall_s        # 28100.0 with the built-in table
source
estimate_cost(cfg, inp_path; span_nb = nothing, merger = cfg.merger.enabled)
    -> CostEstimate

estimate_cost for the engine input file inp_path: N and NNBOPT from the file, the span from its TCRIT unless span_nb is given (a restart passes the remaining span), and the intervals DTADJ and DELTAT from the file when present. Raises an ArgumentError when N or NNBOPT cannot be read, an error when the span is not given and the file has no TCRIT.

Example

estimate_cost(cfg, "input_files/engine/N1k_quick.inp")
source
Nbody6Dynamics.cost_rate — Function
cost_rate(n; table = _COST_TABLE_H200) -> Float64

Wall seconds per N-body time unit of an engine run with n bodies: log–log linear interpolation between the two table rows that bracket n; outside the table, extrapolation with the exponent of the nearest segment.

Arguments

  • n: particle number, > 0
  • table: rows (N, s_per_nb) with strictly increasing N and positive rates, at least two; tuples, or the two-element vectors of cost.table. The default is the built-in H200 table (_COST_TABLE_H200)

Raises an ArgumentError for a table with fewer than two rows, unordered N or non-positive entries.

Example

cost_rate(5e5)                                     # 281.0
cost_rate(2e5; table = [[1e5, 60.0], [1e6, 1000.0]])
source

External Post-Processing

Nbody6Dynamics.scan_output — Function
scan_output(dir::AbstractString) -> OutputScan

Scan dir for Nbody6++ output files and report what is available.

Detects:

  • conf.3 snapshots: files matching conf.3_* or a bare conf.3
  • HDF5 snapshots: files matching *.h5part or *.hdf5
  • Diagnostics/stdout: out1000, out1, stdout, output.log
  • Lagrangian radii: lagr.7
  • Escapers: esc.11
  • Stellar evolution: files matching sev.83_*
  • Regularised binaries: files matching bev.82_*

The returned OutputScan contains full paths and an available dict summarising which data categories were found.

Example

scan = scan_output("/data/cluster_sim/output")
scan.available          # Dict(:snapshots_conf3 => true, :lagr => true, ...)
println(scan)           # human-readable summary
source
Nbody6Dynamics.postprocess_external — Function
postprocess_external(dir::AbstractString;
                     output_dir::AbstractString = "",   # default: <dir>/../plots
                     format::AbstractString = "pdf",
                     dpi::Int = 300,
                     column::AbstractString = "single",
                     units::AbstractString = "physical",
                     make_plots::Bool = true,
                     make_animations::Bool = true) -> Dict{Symbol,Any}

Post-process Nbody6++ output from an arbitrary directory.

This is the config-free entry point: no Nbody6Config or config.toml required. The function scans dir for all recognised output files (using the standard Nbody6++ file names), reads whatever is available, runs sanity checks, and optionally generates plots and animations through the same generate_plots dispatcher as the config-driven pipeline.

Arguments

  • dir: directory containing Nbody6++ output files (conf.3_*, out1000, lagr.7, etc.)
  • output_dir: where to save plots (default: <dir>/../plots/)
  • format: plot format — "pdf" (vector default), "svg", "png"
  • dpi: raster resolution at final print size (png only)
  • column: "single" | "double" journal-width preset
  • units: "physical" | "nbody" axis units
  • make_plots: set false to skip plot generation (returns data only)
  • make_animations: set false to skip GIF animations (faster)

Returns

A Dict{Symbol,Any} with keys :scan, :snapshots, :diagnostics, :lagr, :escapers, :stellar_evo, :binary_evo (present only if corresponding data exists), the same keys the config-driven pipeline produces.

Example

using Nbody6Dynamics

# Point at any directory with Nbody6++ output
results = postprocess_external("/scratch/sim42/output")

# Or data-only (no plots):
results = postprocess_external("/scratch/sim42/output"; make_plots=false)

# Inspect what was found:
results[:scan]  # OutputScan with availability info
source
Nbody6Dynamics.OutputScan — Type
OutputScan

Result of scanning a directory for Nbody6++ output files. Each field is either a Vector{String} of found file paths or a String path. The available dict maps data categories to true/false.

source

Configuration Types

Nbody6Dynamics.Nbody6Config — Type
Nbody6Config

Top-level configuration aggregating all subsections.

config_dir is the directory every relative path of the configuration resolves against — install.install_dir, simulation.input_file, simulation.runs_dir, postprocess.data_dir and merger.config_file — and the default base_dir of the pipeline entry points. load_config sets it to the directory of the file it read; a configuration built in memory takes the working directory. It is not a configuration key and is not written by save_config.

The positional constructor takes the eight sections and config_dir; the cluster and cost sections and config_dir may be omitted (defaults and the working directory).

source
Nbody6Dynamics.InstallConfig — Type
InstallConfig

Configuration for the Nbody6++ source installation phase. Controls git clone, the checked-out reference (ref: commit, tag, or branch of the backend; the default is the fork tag v2026.07.16), reinstall behaviour, and clean builds.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.BuildConfig — Type
BuildConfig

Configuration for the Nbody6++ compilation phase. Controls configure flags, MPI/GPU/HDF5 toggles, CUDA path, CUDA target architectures, and parallel make.

Fields

  • cuda_arch: CUDA architectures the GPU kernels are compiled for ("sm_90" Hopper, "sm_120" consumer Blackwell, …): native code for each plus PTX for the highest. Empty = the compute capabilities of the devices visible to nvidia-smi, or the nvcc default target (PTX JIT-compiled at the first launch) when no device is visible
  • nvcc_flags: extra nvcc options appended to the GPU build's CUFLAGS (e.g. ["-allow-unsupported-compiler"] or ["-ccbin", "gcc-14"] when the host compiler is newer than the toolkit supports)
source
Nbody6Dynamics.SimulationConfig — Type
SimulationConfig

Configuration for the simulation execution phase. Controls input file, run directory, MPI ranks, backend OpenMP thread count, run ID generation, and runtime telemetry.

Fields

  • omp_threads: OpenMP threads for the backend; 0 leaves the runtime default (an inherited OMP_NUM_THREADS, else every logical CPU)
  • gpu_list: CUDA device indices the engine may use, exported as its GPU_LIST variable; empty = every visible device. At most four per process (the engine's MAX_GPU); requires build.enable_gpu
  • telemetry_interval: sampling interval of the process-tree/GPU telemetry [s]; 0 disables the sampler (exact CPU accounting stays on)
  • startup_timeout: wall-clock seconds within which the engine must report its first adjustment beyond t = 0; otherwise the run is terminated with an error. 0 disables
  • stall_timeout: wall-clock seconds after the first adjustment beyond t = 0 during which neither the stdout capture grows nor a new restart dump appears; then the engine is asked to stop, terminated if it has not stopped the same time later, and the segment is recorded as stalled. 0 disables
  • exit_grace: after the engine has printed END RUN, the wall-clock seconds its output directory may stay unchanged with the process still alive; then it is terminated and the segment is recorded as completed (the engine has been seen to finish its integration and never exit). A final COMMON dump in progress keeps the directory changing and is never interrupted. 0 disables
  • wall_budget: wall-clock budget of one engine segment [s]; 0 = none. At wall_budget − stop_margin the engine is asked to stop (a STOP file in its working directory: it writes a restart dump and exits, and the segment is recorded as stopped); an engine still running at wall_budget is terminated
  • stop_margin: wall-clock seconds the engine is given to act on a stop request, both before the wall budget and when the driver process itself is terminated (SIGTERM); must be smaller than a non-zero wall_budget
  • checkpoint_keep: number of periodic restart dumps (comm.2_<t>) kept while the engine runs; older ones are deleted as new ones appear. 0 keeps every dump; otherwise at least 2, because the newest may be incomplete when the engine dies. Dumps written at the end of a run or at a stop request (comm.1_<t>) and the dump a restart started from are never deleted
  • live_diagnostics: with monitor, print in-terminal sparklines of the virial ratio and energy error every live_interval seconds (opt-in; interactive terminals only, never in the log file)
source
Nbody6Dynamics.PostprocessConfig — Type
PostprocessConfig

Configuration for the post-processing phase. Controls which output files to read (snapshots, diagnostics, Lagrangian radii, escapers, stellar evolution, the counters of events_file) and their file patterns, the snapshot integrals (snapshot_integrals), the limits of the bound-set iteration (bound_max_passes, bound_min_members, see bound_set), the largest snapshot on which the O(N²) diagnostics run (pair_sum_max_n), the energy-jump localisation (energy_events, flagging an adjustment interval whose |DE| reaches max(energy_jump_threshold, energy_jump_sigma × 1.4826 × MAD), see energy_intervals) and the mass budget (mass_budget, see mass_budget).

The estimator settings follow. The centre keys (centre_n_stop, centre_stop_fraction, centre_n_floor, centre_shrink) set the stop rule and step of shrinking_sphere_centre. lagr_n_min is the effective particle number lagrangian_grid requires on either side of a Lagrangian radius. The profile keys (profile_nbins, profile_n_min_sigma, profile_n_min_beta, profile_n_min_density) are the shell count and membership thresholds of radial_profile. density_neighbours is the neighbour rank of the Casertano–Hut density estimate behind the core radius and core density. The segregation keys (msr_n_tracer, msr_scan, msr_n_random, msr_n_random_small, msr_n_small, msr_seed, msr_threshold) fix the tracer class, the NMST scan, the random comparison sets and seed of [`masssegregation](@ref) and the Λ_MSR level of the segregation time. The rotation keys (rotationnmin,rotationnbinsmax,rotationnnull) are the shell membership, shell cap and null draws of [rotationanalysis](@ref). The shape keys (shapenminshell,shapenminenclosed) are the membership thresholds ofshapetensor. The pair keys (pairk,pairtidalfactor) are the neighbour count and tidal criterion ofsnapshotpairs, which feeds the hardness series of [remnantdiagnostics](@ref).relaxationgammaholds the Coulomb-logarithm arguments γ ofrelaxation_times`.

The validity keys set the per-run validity record of validity_record: validity switches the output of validity.toml and validity_snapshots.csv; validity_interval_de and validity_cumulative_de are the per-interval |DE| and the cumulative |DETOT/E₀| above which the energy check flags the run; validity_mass_tolerance is the relative mass-budget residual above which an epoch is flagged; validity_settle_tcr, validity_pericentre_tcr and validity_window_tcr are the violent-relaxation window after the start, the half-width of the window about a pericentre and the window of the stationarity tests, in crossing times; validity_virial_tolerance bounds |2T/|W| − 1| of a virialised bound set, validity_rate_tolerance the change per window of a stationary series, and validity_bias_tolerance the relative calibrated bias above which an estimator is masked.

Fields

  • angular_momentum: write angular_momentum.csv (angular-momentum budget, rotation of the bound set and per initial-mass class, elapsed relaxation times)
  • mass_class_edges: edges of the initial-mass classes [M☉]; > 0, strictly increasing
  • mass_class_n_min: bound members a mass class or radial subset needs for its rotation measure, and the shell membership of the class profiles; ≥ 2
  • equipartition: write equipartition.csv (Bianchini equipartition mass of the luminous bound stars within the half-mass radius)
  • equipartition_bin_width: mass bin of the dispersion–mass relation [M☉]; > 0
  • equipartition_n_min: stars a mass bin needs to enter the fit; ≥ 2
  • merger_tree: write merger_tree.csv and progenitor_budget.csv for runs with initial clusters
  • binary_spans: write binary_spans.csv from the KS, few-body and GW lines of the stdout capture; requires parse_stdout
  • span_n_periods: largest gap between two KS episodes of a pair that joins them into one span, in orbital periods of the earlier episode; > 0
  • bound_spans: write bound_spans.csv (physically bound pairs at the snapshots and their spans)
  • bound_gamma_max: largest apocentre perturbation of a bound pair by its nearest bodies; > 0
  • bound_n_miss: consecutive snapshots a pair that stays bound may fail the perturbation test within one span; ≥ 0
  • binary_population: write binary_population.csv (binary fractions per radial shell, stellar class and census level at every snapshot)
  • population_frame: radial shells, "lagrangian" (mass fractions), "rh" (multiples of the half-mass radius), "projected_rh" (multiples of the projected half-mass radius) or "milone" (the three projected regions of Milone et al. 2012: inside the core radius, core to half-mass radius, one to two half-mass radii)
  • population_projection_axis: line of sight of the projected frames, 1, 2 or 3
  • population_r_tide_nb: tidal filter, systems beyond this radius [NB] are left out of the population, pair-type and distribution series; 0 = off; ≥ 0
  • binary_pair_types: write binary_pair_types.csv (pair-type composition, hard/soft split and median elements per shell and census level)
  • binary_distributions: write binary_distributions.csv (histograms of log a, e, q and log |E_b|/⟨m⟩σ² per snapshot and census level)
  • gw_catalogues: write compact_binary_catalogue.csv, escaped_binary_catalogue.csv and gw_merger_catalogue.csv from the event lines, the spans, coal.24 and esc.11; requires parse_stdout
  • gw_band_hz: gravitational-wave band of the residence times [Hz], two increasing positive values
  • gw_t_now_myr: observation epoch of the escaped pairs [Myr] for their present-epoch state; 0 = none; ≥ 0
  • ldc_export: write ldc_galactic_binaries.csv, the quasi-circular compact pairs of the compact-binary catalogue as an LDC galactic-binary table (write_ldc_galactic_binaries); requires gw_catalogues
  • ldc_distance_kpc: distance of every exported source [kpc]; > 0
  • ldc_e_max: largest eccentricity of an exported pair; 0 < e_max < 1
  • ldc_seed: seed of the sky position and orientation angles of the export
  • gw_observer: write gw_observer_catalogue.csv, the observer-dependent columns of the last record of every compact pair (gw_observer_catalogue); requires gw_catalogues
  • gw_observer_distance_kpc: luminosity distance of every pair [kpc]; > 0
  • gw_observer_t_obs_yr: LISA observation time [yr]; one of 0.5, 1, 2, 4
  • gw_observer_seed: seed of the drawn sky positions and orientation angles
  • collision_catalogue: write collision_catalogue.csv (every collision of coll.13 and coalescence of coal.24 with the progenitor cluster of each star, the primordial-pair, mixed-origin and generation flags) when either file exists
  • tde: write tde_events.csv, the disruptions, partial disruptions and plunges at the static black hole of a [merger.mbh] run (read_tde_events), when the engine stdout carries TDE lines
  • binary_survival: write binary_survival.csv (Kaplan–Meier survival of the regularised binaries against their age, every end an event, the open ends censored) and binary_incidence.csv (Aalen–Johansen cumulative incidence per end class); requires binary_spans
  • imbh: IMBH diagnostics when output/merger_ic.toml records a seeded IMBH (imbh_trajectory.csv, imbh_wandering.csv, imbh_companions.csv, imbh_events.csv); inert otherwise
  • population_q_min: mass-ratio limit of the q-restricted fraction; 0 < q_min < 1
  • population_m1_min, population_m1_max: primary-mass window of the q-restricted fraction [M☉], by default 0.47–0.76 (Milone et al. 2012, doi:10.1051/0004-6361/201016384); 0 ≤ m1min < m1max
  • population_edges: shell edges replacing the frame's default (mass fractions from 0 to 1, or multiples of r_h from 0, Inf allowed); strictly increasing; empty = the frame's default
source
Nbody6Dynamics.VisualizationConfig — Type
VisualizationConfig

Configuration for the plotting and animation phase: output format (png/pdf/svg), the printed width of an export, output directory, and the PlotStyle presentation knobs.

Figures are composed in layout units on canvases fixed by the figure type (a single panel is 900 × 600, stacked panels and auxiliary strips add height, panel grids are wider) and exported so that the canvas width becomes export_width inches on the page; text and line weights scale with it. A journal target is an explicit override: column = "single" or "double" exports at that column width instead. Either way the export enters a document at native size.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.PlotStyle — Type
PlotStyle

Stylistic plotting parameters, configurable via [visualization.style] in config.toml. These are presentation knobs only — data-validity cutoffs (e.g. BSE placeholder filtering) remain named constants in the plot code.

Fields

  • marker_budget: scatter marker size is clamp(marker_budget/N, marker_min, marker_max)
  • marker_min, marker_max: clamp bounds for the scatter marker size [layout units]
  • q_log_threshold: switch virial-ratio axes to log scale when max(Q) exceeds this
  • q_floor: clamp floor for the virial ratio on log-scale axes only
  • zoom_frac: extent-ratio threshold below which snapshot panels and animation frames are zoomed each on its own data
  • anim_fps: animation frame rate; 0 selects automatically from frame count
  • anim_target_seconds: target duration used by the automatic FPS selection
  • anim_px_per_unit: pixels per canvas unit of animation frames (a single-panel canvas is 900 × 600 units)
  • snapshot_render: particle projections as a mass-coloured scatter ("scatter"), as the projected surface mass density ("density"), or "auto": density from density_min_n particles on, where scatter markers overlap into a flat disc
  • density_min_n: particle count from which "auto" renders surface density
  • density_bins: grid cells per axis of a surface-density panel
  • density_decades: colour range of a surface-density map below its maximum [dex]
  • density_mass_frac: mass fraction whose projected extent sets the limits of a surface-density panel, so that escapers do not set the scale
source
Nbody6Dynamics.MergerPipelineConfig — Type
MergerPipelineConfig

Configuration for the merger IC generation phase within the main pipeline.

Fields

  • enabled::Bool: whether to generate merger ICs before simulation
  • config_file::String: path to the merger cluster TOML file
source
Nbody6Dynamics.ClusterConfig — Type
ClusterConfig

Batch-scheduler layer of a run ([cluster]): one queue job per engine segment, chained until the run reaches its end time. The job script is generated into <run_dir>/cluster/ at submission; nothing here is read unless enabled.

Fields

  • enabled: submit runs as scheduler jobs (submit_run) instead of running them in the calling process
  • scheduler: "slurm" (the only scheduler supported)
  • partition, account, qos: the corresponding sbatch options; empty = not passed
  • job_name: --job-name; empty = the run id
  • time_limit: --time, in Slurm's time format (minutes, minutes:seconds, hours:minutes:seconds, days-hours, days-hours:minutes, days-hours:minutes:seconds)
  • partition_limit: the partition's maximum time limit, same format; when nonempty, time_limit must not exceed it
  • nodes, ntasks, cpus_per_task: the corresponding sbatch options (each ≥ 1)
  • mem: --mem, e.g. "64G"; empty = the scheduler's default
  • gres: --gres, e.g. "gpu:1"; empty = none
  • exclusive: pass --exclusive
  • sbatch_options: further #SBATCH options, verbatim without the #SBATCH prefix (e.g. ["--hint=nomultithread"])
  • prologue: shell lines run in the job before Julia starts (module loads, depot paths)
  • julia: the Julia executable the job runs; empty = the one that submits
  • julia_threads: JULIA_NUM_THREADS of the job; 0 = cpus_per_task
  • chain: "resubmit" (each job submits its successor while the run is short of its end time) or "dependency" (max_segments jobs submitted at once, each --dependency=afterok on the one before)
  • max_segments: number of segments the chain may run; 0 = until the run completes (resubmit only); dependency requires ≥ 1
  • overhead: wall-clock seconds of the job outside the engine's wall budget (start-up, initial-condition generation, post-processing); wall_budget + stop_margin + overhead must fit time_limit
  • sbatch: the submission executable
source
Nbody6Dynamics.CostConfig — Type
CostConfig

Cost model of a run ([cost]): the wall time, the number of segments and the disk of an engine launch are estimated from a table of seconds per N-body time unit against particle number (estimate_cost), logged, written into the segment record, and compared with a budget.

Fields

  • enabled: estimate at every launch
  • table: rows [N, s_per_nb] (≥ 2 rows, strictly increasing N, positive rates) replacing the built-in table measured on one H200 NVL device; empty = the built-in table
  • merger_factor: factor applied to the rate of a two-cluster (merger) configuration, whose time unit is set by the whole configuration; > 0
  • budget_hours: wall-time budget of one run [h]; ≥ 0, 0 = no check
  • on_exceed: "warn" or "refuse" when the estimate exceeds the budget
source

Data Types

Nbody6Dynamics.Snapshot — Type
Snapshot(header, name, mass, pos, vel, rho, phi[, npairs = 0, pair_name = Int32[]])

Stars of a single conf.3 snapshot. Positions and velocities are stored column-major: pos[:, i] gives star i. The engine's centre-of-mass entries of regularised pairs are not part of the snapshot; the components of the npairs regularised pairs are the first 2npairs stars, pair k being stars 2k − 1 and 2k (see pair_indices). pair_name[k] is the name of pair k's centre-of-mass entry: negative for a hierarchical merger, whose stars 2k − 1 and 2k are an inner component and the outer body rather than a pair; empty when unknown. rho and phi are empty when the file does not carry them.

Throws DimensionMismatch when the arrays disagree in length and ArgumentError when 2npairs exceeds the number of stars.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.SnapshotHeader — Type
SnapshotHeader

Header metadata from a conf.3 snapshot file. ntot is the engine's NTOT, the number of entries in the file: the N stars followed by one centre-of-mass entry per regularised pair (NTOT = N + NPAIRS). The params vector stores the full AS(1:NK) Fortran array (NK = 20 by convention). Slot map (accessors exist for the starred entries):

ASQuantityASQuantity
1*TTOT, time [NB] (time_nb)11*TSCALE, NB time → Myr
2NPAIRS, KS pair count12*VSTAR, NB velocity → km/s
3*RBAR, NB length → pc13*RC, core radius [NB]
4*ZMBAR, NB mass → M☉ (total-mass)14NC, core member count
5RTIDE, tidal radius [NB]15VC, core velocity dispersion
6TIDAL(4), tidal-field coefficient16RHOM, mean core density
7–9RDENS(1:3), density centre [NB]17CMAX
10TTOT/TCR, time in crossing times18*RSCALE, half-mass radius [NB]
19RSMIN
20DMIN1
source
Nbody6Dynamics.DiagnosticsData — Type
DiagnosticsData

Collection of parsed diagnostics from simulation stdout: the ADJUST records, the PHYSICAL SCALING values, the engine options kz (the 50 values of the OPTIONS echo; empty when the capture lacks it) and the linearised tidal field tidal (TIDAL(1:4) of the TIDAL PARAMETERS echo, N-body units; zeros when absent, i.e. no external field of KZ(14) ≤ 2). The two-argument constructor leaves kz empty and tidal zero. nbin0 is NBIN0 of the &INDATA echo (NBIN0 = … on the line that starts with ALPHAS), nzero N of the &ININPUT echo (the value row under N NFIX NCRIT), each -1 when absent; the two-, four- and five-argument constructors set the missing ones to -1.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.AdjustRecord — Type
AdjustRecord

Single ADJUST output line from the simulation log, recording the relative energy error de_rel (ΔE / max(T, |E|)), the interval energy change de_abs (engine DELTA, NB), the engine running sum of ΔE detot (DETOT, NB), and the energy terms the line prints after them (N-body units; NaN when a line lacks them): ekin (EKIN, the kinetic energy of the centre-of-mass bodies), pot (POT, the positive potential energy, W = −POT), etide (ETIDE), and the engine's correction channels ebin (EBIN), emerge (EMERGE), esub (ESUB), ecoll (ECOLL), emdot (EMDOT), ecdot (ECDOT), whose increments between consecutive lines locate the energy changes the engine has accounted for. The ten-argument constructor leaves the energy terms NaN. nmerge is the NMERGE count of the TIME[NB] line (stable hierarchies held in the engine's merger table), 0 when not printed; the ten- and nineteen-argument constructors set it to 0.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.EngineEvent — Type
EngineEvent

One stamped event line of the engine log, from read_engine_events. interval is the index of the ADJUST record whose line follows the event in the log — the adjustment interval the event falls in, (t_{k−1}, t_k], assigned by position because the engine prints every line of an interval between the two ADJUST lines that bound it — and line the line number in the capture. time_nb is the stamp of the line (NaN for the lines without one: NEW CHAIN). The remaining fields hold what the kind carries, with 0, KSTAR_ABSENT and NaN where it does not:

kindname1,2,3kstar1,2,3countn_totalenergy_nbmass_solardmass_solarvelocity_kms
:ks_rectification, :ks_new, :ks_new_compact, :ks_new_mdot, :ks_end_mdotNM1, NM2, NMSKW1, KW2, KWS——E_bM1 + M2——
:remnant_formationNAMEK* before, KW after———M1 afterM0 − M1—
:kick_*NAMEK0, K, K*(ICM)———MN afterM0 − MNVK
:collisionNM1, NM2, NMSKW1, KW2, KWSNSYS—E_bM3 (product)——
:merged_starNM1, NM2K(I1), K(I2), K*(INEW)———M(INEW)DM lost—
:coalescenceN1, N2KW1, KW2, SIQCOLL—E_bM12S (product)DM lost—
:escape——NESCN aftertotal E (BE(3))M* total after——
:supernova_no_remnantNMK* before, 15———M1——
:chain_startNAMEC(1:3)—NCH—ENERGY———
:chain_endNI3, NI4, NCM———————

Masses in M☉, energies in N-body units, velocities in km s⁻¹.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.ENGINE_EVENT_KINDS — Constant
ENGINE_EVENT_KINDS

The kinds of EngineEvent, in the order of the engine writers: :ks_rectification (BEG KSRECT), :ks_new (NEW KSREG), :ks_new_compact (NS/BH BINARY), :ks_new_mdot (MDNEW KSREG), :ks_end_mdot (MDEND KSREG), :remnant_formation (WD/NS/BH FORMATION), :kick_full, :kick_zero, :kick_low (… VELOCITY KICK), :collision (… COLLISION), :merged_star (NEW STAR), :coalescence (…COAL:), :escape (ESCAPE T,NESC), :supernova_no_remnant (MDOT WARNING! SN KW=15), :chain_start (NEW CHAIN), :chain_end (CHTERM:).

source
Nbody6Dynamics.KSTAR_ABSENT — Constant

Stellar type value of an EngineEvent field the line does not carry.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.EventCounters — Type
EventCounters

One row of event.35, the engine's counters at an output time (events.f): time_myr; the interaction counters ndiss, ntide, nsync, ncoll, ncoal, ndd, ncirc, nroche, nro, nce, nhyp, nhypc, nkick; the energy terms ebin, emerge, ecoll, emdot, ecdot, ekick, esesc, ebesc, emesc, degrav, ebind (N-body units) and mmax (the most massive body, M☉); the stellar-evolution counters nmdot, nrg, nhe, nrs, nnh, nwd, nsn, nbh, nbs; the mass lost by stellar evolution per phase zmrg, zmhe, zmrs, zmnh, zmwd, zmsn and in total zmdot (M☉, cumulative); ntype, the number of stars of stellar type K* = 0 … 15.

LagrangianData

Lagrangian radii evolution parsed from lagr.7.

UnitScaling

Nbody6Dynamics.EscaperRecord — Type
EscaperRecord

A single escaper event from esc.11. The direction angles follow the escape.F convention: phi_deg is the azimuth of the escape direction measured from the x-axis in [0°, 360°]; theta_deg is the elevation from the xy-plane in [-90°, 90°].

Nbody6Dynamics.StellarRecord — Type
StellarRecord

Nbody6Dynamics.StellarEvolutionSnapshot — Type
StellarEvolutionSnapshot

All single-star data from one sev.83_* file.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.STELLAR_TYPE_LABELS — Constant
STELLAR_TYPE_LABELS

Dict{Int,String} mapping SSE/BSE stellar type codes (K*) to human-readable labels used in HR diagram legends. Follows the standard Hurley et al. (2000) convention used by this fork (global_output.F): 0/1 = low-/high-mass MS, 2 = HG, …, 13 = NS, 14 = BH, 15 = massless supernova remnant.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.BinaryRecord — Type
BinaryRecord

One KS-regularised binary from a bev.82_* file (hrplot.F, upstream v2026.07: 32 tokens per line). Only regularised pairs are listed, so counts derived from these records are lower bounds on the bound-pair population; wide pairs beyond the regularisation distance never appear.

Fields with suffix 1/2 refer to the two components: masses in M☉, radii in R☉, ages, epochs and main-sequence lifetimes in Myr; log_period_days is $\log_{10}(P/\mathrm{d})$ and log_semi_major_axis_rsun is $\log_{10}(a/R_\odot)$.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.BinaryEvolutionSnapshot — Type
BinaryEvolutionSnapshot

All regularised binaries from one bev.82* file; `timemyris the header TPHYS [Myr] andn_pairs` the header NPAIRS.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.BinaryPopulation — Type
BinaryPopulation

Time series of the regularised-binary population from a sequence of bev.82 snapshots (see binary_population).

Fields

  • time_myr: epochs [Myr]
  • n_pairs: number of regularised pairs listed at each epoch
  • n_hard, n_soft: hard/soft split by the Heggie criterion (zeros when classified == false)
  • binary_fraction: $f_\mathrm{b} = N_\mathrm{b} / (N_\mathrm{s} + N_\mathrm{b})$, pairs over systems; NaN when the stellar count was not supplied
  • classified: whether a hard/soft energy scale was available
source

Nbody6Dynamics.zmbar — Function
zmbar(h::SnapshotHeader) -> Float64

Mass scaling factor: 1 NB mass unit (the total cluster mass) = zmbar M☉. NOT the mean stellar mass — Nbody6++ redefines ZMBAR as the total-mass scale factor at startup (start.F); the mean mass is printed separately as <M> in the PHYSICAL SCALING line.

I/O Readers

Nbody6Dynamics.read_conf3 — Function
read_conf3(path::AbstractString) -> Snapshot

Read a single conf.3 snapshot from a Fortran unformatted binary file. Supports both the bulk-array format (one record for all data) and the legacy per-particle record format. The centre-of-mass entries of regularised pairs, which follow the stars in the file, are dropped (their count and names are kept as npairs and pair_name of the returned Snapshot), and so is the centre-of-mass body of an active chain regularisation (NAME = 0), whose members the file carries individually. Throws ArgumentError when the header pair count is inconsistent with the number of entries.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.read_all_conf3 — Function
read_all_conf3(dir, pattern = "conf.3_*"; threaded = Threads.nthreads() > 1) -> Vector{Snapshot}

Read every conf.3 snapshot matching pattern in dir, ordered by the numeric time suffix. With threaded = true (the default in a Julia session started with more than one thread) the files are read in chunks on Threads.@spawn tasks and assembled in file order, which matters once a run holds hundreds of snapshots of 10⁵ particles; the result is identical to the serial read. Corrupt files are skipped with a warning each.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.read_telemetry — Function
read_telemetry(path) -> Vector{TelemetrySample}

Read a telemetry CSV written by the sampler (header = the field names of TelemetrySample, matched by name in any order). Empty cells and NaN read as NaN; n_processes is rounded to an integer. Throws an ArgumentError when a field of the sample is missing from the header or a row has the wrong number of cells.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.read_run_telemetry — Function
read_run_telemetry(run_dir) -> Vector{TelemetrySample}

Every telemetry segment of a run (telemetry.csv, telemetry_2.csv, …, one per launch) concatenated in segment order, the elapsed time of each segment offset by the last elapsed time of the previous one, since restarts run one after another. Empty when the run directory holds no telemetry.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.read_diagnostics — Function
read_diagnostics(path::AbstractString) -> DiagnosticsData

Parse the simulation stdout file for ADJUST lines, supplementary RSCALE and TIME[NB] lines, and physical scaling info; the TIME[NB] lines give N, NPAIRS and NMERGE.

Each ADJUST epoch in Nbody6++ output consists of up to three lines:

  1. ADJUST: — energy, virial ratio, time
  2. RMIN = … RSCALE = … — scale radius (may appear between ADJUST and TIME[NB])
  3. TIME[NB] … N … NPAIRS … — particle counts

These are merged into a single AdjustRecord per epoch. An ADJUST line that does not parse (too few fields, an overflowed **** field) is skipped together with its supplementary lines; the count is reported in one warning per file.

The energy terms the key-value ADJUST line prints after DETOT (EKIN, POT, ETIDE, EBIN, EMERGE, ESUB, ECOLL, EMDOT, ECDOT) are kept in the record, NaN when the line lacks them. The first OPTIONS echo gives kz, the 50 option values, left empty when the capture lacks the echo or its value row does not hold 50 integers. The TIDAL PARAMETERS: echo gives tidal, the four coefficients TIDAL(1:4) in N-body units, zeros when the capture lacks it. nbin0 from the &INDATA echo, nzero (N) from the value row under the N NFIX NCRIT header of the &ININPUT echo (-1 when absent).

source
Nbody6Dynamics.extract_scaling — Function
extract_scaling(diag::DiagnosticsData) -> UnitScaling

Build a UnitScaling from parsed physical scaling data. A missing M* or T* gives NaN for that factor, with one warning, so that every quantity converted with it is NaN rather than a number in the wrong units; a missing R* or V* falls back to 1.

The mass scale is M* (ZMBAR, the NB→M☉ conversion factor for the total mass) — NOT <M>, which is the mean stellar mass. Nbody6++ prints both in the PHYSICAL SCALING line; start.F redefines ZMBAR as the mass scale factor at startup, and units.f converts masses as ZMBAR*M.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.cumulative_energy_error — Function
cumulative_energy_error(diag::DiagnosticsData)
    -> (; time_nb::Vector{Float64}, errtot::Vector{Float64}, detot::Vector{Float64})

Cumulative energy-conservation record of a run, one entry per adjustment. errtot is the running sum of the relative interval errors de_rel (the engine's ERRTOT, dimensionless); detot is the running sum of the interval energy changes in N-body energy units (the engine's DETOT), taken from the ADJUST records when every record carries it, otherwise accumulated from de_abs, and NaN throughout when neither is available. The ADJUST line prints four significant digits, so errtot agrees with the END RUN total of read_energy_totals only to that precision.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.read_energy_totals — Function
read_energy_totals(path::AbstractString)
    -> Union{Nothing,@NamedTuple{time_nb::Float64,errtot::Float64,detot::Float64}}

Totals the engine prints on its END RUN line: the final time in N-body units, ERRTOT (sum of the relative interval errors) and DETOT (sum of the interval energy changes, N-body energy units). The last END RUN line of the stdout capture is used, so a restarted run reports its final segment; nothing when the capture has no such line.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.read_engine_events — Function
read_engine_events(path::AbstractString) -> Vector{EngineEvent}

Parse the stamped event lines of the engine log (out1000) and return one EngineEvent per line, in file order. The lines are written by ksrect.f, ksinit.F, mdot.F, kick.F, cmbody.f, mix.f, coal.f, escape.F, chain.f and chterm.f; the kinds are listed in ENGINE_EVENT_KINDS and the fields each kind carries in the docstring of EngineEvent. interval is one more than the number of ADJUST: lines above the event, so a line printed between the (k−1)-th and the k-th ADJUST line has interval == k; line is the 1-based line number. Lines that do not parse (overflowed Fortran fields, glued exponents such as 1.08726-157) are skipped.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.read_event_counters — Function
read_event_counters(path::AbstractString) -> Vector{EventCounters}

Parse event.35, the engine's event counters (events.f), and return one EventCounters per output time. Each data row holds 58 tokens: the time in Myr, 13 interaction counters, 12 energy terms and the maximum mass, 9 stellar-evolution counters, 7 cumulative mass-loss terms and the 16 stellar-type counts. The TIME[Myr] header row (absent after a restart), blank lines, and rows that are short or do not parse are skipped.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.read_lagr — Function
read_lagr(path::AbstractString) -> LagrangianData

Parse a lagr.7 file (upstream lagr.f single-row format: ##/TIME header lines, then one row per epoch) and return the Lagrangian radii evolution. Raises an error on files without the expected header, rather than mis-parsing an unrecognised layout.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.read_escapers — Function
read_escapers(path::AbstractString) -> Vector{EscaperRecord}

Parse the esc.11 file and return a vector of EscaperRecord (physical units: Myr, M☉, km/s, degrees). Skips the header, blank lines, and lines with fewer than 13 tokens (the direction angles in tokens 12–13 are part of the record; real files carry 27 tokens per line).

source
Nbody6Dynamics.read_stellar_evolution — Function
read_stellar_evolution(path::AbstractString) -> StellarEvolutionSnapshot

Parse a single sev.83_* file into a StellarEvolutionSnapshot. The snapshot time is the header TPHYS [Myr]; each record additionally stores its own per-line NB time (TTOT).

Both hrplot.F layouts are accepted: 15 tokens per data line (upstream v2026.07+, ending at TM MC RCC RE) and the 11 tokens documented by the engine manual (ending at AGE EPOCH), for which ms_lifetime_myr, mass_core, radius_core and radius_envelope are filled with NaN. Lines with any other token count, or with unparseable fields (Fortran field overflow), are skipped and reported once per file as a warning. Reals are read with E or D exponents.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.read_all_stellar_evolution — Function
read_all_stellar_evolution(dir::AbstractString, pattern::AbstractString = "sev.83_*")
    -> Vector{StellarEvolutionSnapshot}

Read all sev.83* files matching pattern in dir, sorted by snapshot time. The default pattern matches what this fork's hrplot.F actually writes (`sev.83<time>, e.g.sev.830,sev.830.5`). Files that cannot be read are skipped and reported in one warning with their count and the first path.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.read_binary_evolution — Function
read_binary_evolution(path::AbstractString) -> BinaryEvolutionSnapshot

Parse a single bev.82_* file into a BinaryEvolutionSnapshot. The snapshot time is the header TPHYS [Myr]; each record additionally stores its own per-line NB time (TTOT).

Both hrplot.F layouts are accepted: 32 tokens per data line (upstream v2026.07, ending at the SSE tail TM MC RCC RE of both components) and the 24 tokens documented by the engine manual (ending at EPOCH(I1) EPOCH(I2)), for which ms_lifetime1_myr, ms_lifetime2_myr, mass_core1, mass_core2, radius_core1, radius_core2, radius_envelope1 and radius_envelope2 are filled with NaN. Lines with any other token count, or with unparseable fields (Fortran field overflow), are skipped and reported once per file as a warning. Reals are read with E or D exponents.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.read_all_binary_evolution — Function
read_all_binary_evolution(dir::AbstractString, pattern::AbstractString = "bev.82_*")
    -> Vector{BinaryEvolutionSnapshot}

Read all bev.82* files matching pattern in dir, sorted by snapshot time. The default pattern matches what hrplot.F writes (`bev.82<time>, e.g.bev.820,bev.820.5`). Files that cannot be read are skipped and reported in one warning with their count and the first path.

Binary, few-body and gravitational-wave output

Nbody6Dynamics.read_binary_events — Function
read_binary_events(path::AbstractString) -> BinaryEventLog

Read the binary, few-body and gravitational-wave event lines of an engine log (out1000) in one pass and return them by family (BinaryEventLog). Every record carries its line number, its adjustment interval (one more than the number of ADJUST: lines above it, the convention of read_engine_events), its time in N-body units and its kind. A line of a known kind that does not parse is counted in skipped and reported in one warning. The capture is held in memory while it is read (about 3 MiB per N-body time unit at 2×10⁴ bodies with KZ(10) = 2).

source

Corrections and hazards: FORMAT field 26 is the separation in R☉, not a radius, and is stored as r12_rsun. The 28- and 29-value variants carry no orientation blocks (NaN); in :ks_new_compact r_cm_nb and v_cm_nb are ‖X(cm)‖ and ‖Ẋ(cm)‖, not relative to the density centre, and DEB is the dimensionless relative change of EB (the _nb suffix of deb_nb notwithstanding). The azimuths come from ACOS and lose the sign of y. END KSREG is written for every termination (chain exchange, merger, collision, mass loss, escape), not only disruptions; for a hierarchical outer pair NM1 is the inner c.m. and M1 its total mass.

source

overflowa field was printed as asterisks (read as absent)all

Corrections and hazards. Impact periods: the engine computes the inner "period" with the total mass and the outer one with the inner c.m. mass; they are stored rescaled to the masses of their own orbits, Pin·√(Mtot/Min) and Pout·√(Min/Mtot) with Min = m₁ + m₂ (NaN when a mass is absent). For `:quadnewwritten by the regularisation branch,semiinnbis a(IPAIR) + a(JPAIR) as printed and the corrected inner period refers to that sum. The labelNEW QUAD TIMEis also written by the quadruple-merger branch ofimpact.fwith the same layout; the two are told apart only by the record that follows (END QUADof the regularisation,END MERGERof the merger). Impact fields 3–5 are indices, not names.chainendwith NCH ≤ 3 repeats member I1 as I4 and prints R24 = 10¹⁰: the fourth index, name and mass and R24 are stored absent.chtermnameswith RIJ(3-4) = 0 belongs to a three-body chain (I4 = I3): the stale fourth name and masses and the 2-4, 3-4 distances are stored absent. The binary CHAIN ESCAPE prints 15 items through 13 descriptors: the labels afterNM1/2are shifted by two values (masses underRI VI, RI, RDOT, GPERT, 2M/R underGPERT 2*M/R RB VF) and RB, VINF form a second record underTT T`; the values are stored under their true meaning and both lines are consumed. The F10.4 times have a resolution of 10⁻⁴ NB; E8.1 fields carry two digits.

source

:hierarchy_end_hitripreset2.f FORMAT 18own (F9.2)
:hierarchy_end_outerreset2.f FORMAT 48, KZ(15) > 1following END MERGE2 line (F8.2)
:hierarchy_end_merge2reset2.f FORMAT 65, KZ(15) > 1own (F8.2)

The neighbouring stamp is searched within 12 lines (NaN when none is found). The quadruple merger of impact.f prints NEW QUAD TIME and is returned as :quad_new (FewBodyEvent).

Fields (impact = the two FORMAT 20 kinds, merge2/reset2 = the FORMAT 5–18 kinds of those routines):

Corrections and hazards. Impact periods are rescaled from the swapped masses as in FewBodyEvent. END MERGER is written after the restoration: IP, NAME(ICM), KSTAR(ICM), R12, H, R34, H2 and SEPI/J belong to whatever pair now occupies the old slot, and the JX block repeats the inner component 1; only the time, ICOMP, JCOMP and the inner components' names, types, masses and radii are stored, everything else stays absent (the orbit of the restored pair is in the adjacent NEW KSREG line, the outer body in the END KSREG line of the merged pair). merge2/reset2 names are I6 and overflow at 10⁶; ZM is F6.2.

Nbody6Dynamics.TerminationEvent — Type
TerminationEvent

A termination diagnostic of the engine log.

kindwriterfields

ecc_minus_1—capture kinds: e − 1 of the incoming hyperbola
r_capture_nbNBcapture kinds: Quinlan–Shapiro capture pericentre (RPMAX)
h_after_nbNBcapture kinds: H after the passage (h_nb before it)

overflow—a field was printed as asterisks (read as absent)

Corrections and hazards. The K*S field of :gw_decay_step, :gw_ksint and :gw_tides holds the temporary KSTAR(ICM) = −25 that selects the Peters branch; kstar_cm is absent for these kinds. mdot.F prints the names and types of :gr_brake in the reverse order of the masses; they are stored in the order of the masses (name1, kstar1 belong to m1_msun). The IPAIR of :gr_brake is a local variable set only on the Roche branches of mdot.F and is stored as absent. The DTGW label of :gr_brake_roche holds DTGW(IPAIR) in NB, its TGR is dimensionless; its DELET1 is read as the change of e over DTM0 (not settled from the code). :gw_unpert_coll has no rank test in unpert.f: an MPI run writes one copy per rank, each read as its own record. The COLL lines carry neither names nor time; the merger they trigger is the following CMBODY collision (IQCOLL = −2). The capture kinds are written at the pericentre (time_nb is the pericentre time, qperi_nb the pericentre distance, d_energy_nb the radiated energy of the passage, npert the perturbers after it); a passage that changes the orbital energy by less than 10⁻⁴ is not written. The :gw_recoil VESC, VDIS, R/SEMI, EB and R use IPAIR = KVEC(I1) after KSTERM and probably refer to a terminated pair: they are stored as printed and are not reliable.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.BinaryEscapeEvent — Type
BinaryEscapeEvent

One escape line of a KS pair, a hierarchy or a quadruple ghost binary, written by ESCAPE (escape.F, called from ADJUST) and built by _parse_evolution_line. Kinds: :binary_escape (BINARY ESCAPE, a real KS pair or the outer orbit of a merged hierarchy), :hierarchy_escape (HIARCH ESCAPE, the inner binary of that hierarchy, after its BINARY ESCAPE line), :quad_escape (QUAD ESCAPE, the ghost binary of a quadruple). No escape variant beyond these three has a FORMAT in the layout specification.

fieldunitkinds and meaning
time_nbNBTTOT of the following ESCAPE T,NESC = summary (see below)
ipair—KS pair index: :binary_escape, :quad_escape
n_after—:binary_escape particle number N after the removal
ncorr—:binary_escape escaper counter of this call
name1, name2—components; :hierarchy_escape inner primary, ghost
name_cm—:binary_escape c.m. name JLIST(NCORR), < 0 for a hierarchy
kstar1, kstar2—component types (:hierarchy_escape: inner c.m. body, ghost)
kstar_cm—c.m. type; :hierarchy_escape KSTARM, inner c.m. type at merger
ls_flag—LIST(2,·), −1 for a primordial-flagged pair
m1_msun, m2_msunM☉masses; :quad_escape CM(3:4) of the ghost binary
semi_nb, eccNB, —a, e (hierarchy line: inner; binary line of a hierarchy: outer)
eb_nbNBbinding energy (:quad_escape EB1, :hierarchy_escape μ·HM)
period_ddperiod
rstar_over_peri—:binary_escape max(R)/pericentre (label R/PM)
v_nb, r_nbNB:binary_escape c.m. speed, distance from the density centre
e_cm_nbNB:binary_escape c.m. energy in the cluster field EI
radius1_rsun, radius2_rsunR☉:binary_escape stellar radii
pmin_over_pcrit—:hierarchy_escape outer pericentre over its stability limit
overflow—a field was printed as asterisks (read as absent)

Corrections. The lines print no time: time_nb is the TTOT of the next ESCAPE T,NESC = line, which closes the same ESCAPE call; the summary is looked up without being consumed, and time_nb is NaN when an ADJUST line or the end of the capture comes first. BINARY ESCAPE prints the c.m. speed under the label RI and the distance under VI; they are stored as v_nb and r_nb. Its K*S field carries +30 when the c.m. is a merged hierarchy (NAME < 0); kstar_cm holds KSTAR(c.m.) with the offset removed. On the QUAD ESCAPE branch NAME1, NAME2 are not assigned (left from a previous escaper) and KSTAR(JM) indexes the particle arrays with a merger-table index, so name1, name2 and kstar_cm are absent for :quad_escape (reading of the specification, not settled from the code). The F5.1 masses of :hierarchy_escape/:quad_escape have 0.1 M☉ resolution and F8.4 energies keep four decimals.

source

overflow—a field was printed as asterisks (read as absent)

Corrections. Field 26 of the KSREG layout, labelled RAD1,2,S[], is the separation in R☉ (r12_rsun). BINARY KICK labels the separation R12[au] but prints R(IPAIR) in N-body units (r12_nb). In MDNEW KSREG only the time, the component names and types, the masses [NB, M☉], the radii and DM, DMR are current: its e, a, EB repeat the preceding MDEND line, IPAIR, DTAU, R12, H, GAMMA and the derived period and R12[] belong to whatever pair now occupies the old index, the S name and type are those of the evolving star, and STEP, NPERT, NB(ICM), RI, VI refer to that star; all of these are stored as absent. The orbit of the re-regularised pair is in the NEW KSREG line that follows. In MDEND KSREG the type of the evolving component may already be negated (random-phase flag of a new NS/BH, mdot.F); it is stored as printed. An MDEND line not followed by MDNEW/NEW KSREG of the same names is a termination, not necessarily a disruption.

Corrections. The PD field of NEW/END CIRC is the period after the step, that of CIRCULARIZED the period before it; each is stored under its meaning. The INACTIIVE PHASE path does not assign PD (value of an earlier call, reading of the specification), so neither period is stored for it; its leading blanks are not fixed by the specification and the line is read from its first non-blank character. RI, VI of CIRCULARIZED and INACTIIVE PHASE are written with D exponents and read as such.

Corrections. The label SEP[] holds the instantaneous separation R·SU and SEMI[] the semi-major axis SEP; they are stored as r12_rsun and semi_rsun. ROCHE COAL prints TK = 0 (roche.f), stored as an absent period. RI, VI and EB are formed only on the NEW ROCHE path; in ROCHE COAL and END ROCHE they repeat an earlier value or are undefined (reading of the specification) and are stored as absent. The logical COALS is glued to the preceding E13.5 field and is read from its single column.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.read_ks_pair_listing — Function
read_ks_pair_listing(path::AbstractString) -> KSPairListing

Read one bdat.9_<t> file, the KS-pair data bank written by bindat.f at every OUTPUT (KZ(8) ≥ 2), into a KSPairListing: the fixed-width header lines (FORMAT 30, 35, 40), the label line, and one list-directed KSPairRecord per KS pair. A header block repeated inside the file (up to three non-record lines before a further label line) is skipped; only the first is parsed. Records without 23 values or with unparseable values are skipped and counted in one warning. A missing file raises an error, a malformed header line an ArgumentError; an empty file gives an empty listing with absent header values. The record hazards (hierarchy rows that mix the inner and outer orbit, ghost rows, P = 0 for a < 0 stored as NaN) are listed under KSPairRecord.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.read_all_ks_pair_listings — Function
read_all_ks_pair_listings(dir::AbstractString) -> Vector{KSPairListing}

Read every bdat.9_<t> file in dir with read_ks_pair_listing, in the order of the time <t> of the file name (the left-justified TTOT the engine appends). Files whose suffix is not a number are ignored; a missing directory gives an empty vector.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.KSPairListing — Type
KSPairListing

One bdat.9_<t> file (bindat.f, written at every OUTPUT for KZ(8) ≥ 2), from read_ks_pair_listing: the three header lines and the KS pairs.

HeaderFieldUnit
line 1, NPAIRS, MODEL, NRUNn_pairs, model, n_run—
line 1, N, NC (core members), NMERGEn_total, n_core, n_merge—

line 1, RSCALE, RTIDE, RCrscale_nb, rtide_nb, core_radius_nbNB
line 1, TPHYS (F8.1)time_myrMyr
line 1, ECRIT = −1.5(TIDAL(1)·M²)^⅓ecrit_nb (−0 without tidal field)NB
line 2, E(1:10)energy_nbNB
line 3, SBCOLL, BBCOLLsbcoll_nb, bbcoll_nbNB
line 3, ZKIN, POTkinetic_energy_nb, potential_energy_nbNB
line 3, EBIN0, EBIN, ESUB, EMERGEebin0_nb, ebin_nb, esub_nb, emerge_nbNB
