t_form_myr, t_end_myrthe sameMyr
left_censoredthe first episode was already open at the first record of the pair—
right_censoredthe span is open at the end of the log—
end_classhow the span ends, see binary_spans—

The opening record is the NEW KSREG, NS/BH BINARY or MDNEW KSREG line of the first episode, the closing record the END KSREG or MDEND KSREG line of the last; index 1 of masses and types belongs to name1 whatever the order of the names in the record. MDEND/MDNEW lines carry no c.m. distance. Absent values are NaN for reals, KSTAR_ABSENT for types and 0 for lines: the opening elements of a left-censored span, the closing elements of a span whose last episode ended without a closing record (a collision, a coalescence, an escape, a new regularisation of the pair) or is still open.

Nbody6Dynamics.binary_spans — Function
binary_spans(log::BinaryEventLog;
             listings::AbstractVector{KSPairListing} = KSPairListing[],
             collisions::AbstractVector{CollisionRecord} = CollisionRecord[],
             coalescences::AbstractVector{CoalescenceRecord} = CoalescenceRecord[],
             nzero::Integer, nbin0::Integer, tstar_myr::Real,
             t_start_nb::Real = 0.0, t_end_nb::Real = NaN, n_p::Real = 2.0)
    -> Vector{BinarySpan}

The engine-regularised binaries of a run (level E): the KS episodes of every pair of component names in the engine log, joined to BinarySpans, each with its origin and its end class.

Arguments

  • log: binary and few-body records of the engine log, from read_binary_events
  • listings: the bdat.9_<t> listings (read_all_ks_pair_listings); they date the opening of an episode whose NEW KSREG line is not in the log
  • collisions, coalescences: coll.13 and coal.24 (read_collision_log, read_coalescence_log)
  • nzero: initial number of bodies NZERO; a name ≤ 0 or above it is a c.m. body
  • nbin0: initial number of primordial binaries NBIN0
  • tstar_myr: time unit T* [Myr]
  • t_start_nb: time at which the log starts [NB]
  • t_end_nb: end given to spans still open at the end of the log [NB]; with NaN the latest time stamp of all records
  • n_p: largest gap, in orbital periods of the earlier episode, that is joined

Method

Names are compared as unordered pairs; records with a name 0 or two equal names are ignored.

  1. Episodes. A NEW KSREG, NS/BH BINARY or MDNEW KSREG line opens an episode of its pair, an END KSREG or MDEND KSREG line closes it, and a collision (coll.13), a coalescence (coal.24, ROCHE COAL) or a BINARY ESCAPE of the pair ends an open episode without a closing record. An opening simultaneous with the one that opened the episode is the same regularisation written twice (its NEW KSREG elements replace those of an NS/BH BINARY or MDNEW line); a later opening of an open episode ends it. A closing record of a closed pair is ignored within 0.01 NB of the last close, and otherwise closes a left-censored episode opened at the first listing that holds the pair after the last close, else at t_start_nb or the last close.
  2. Coverage. Chain episodes run, in log-line order, from NEW CHAIN/TRIPLE/QUAD to the first END CHAIN or CHTERM record whose members (names 1…NZERO) intersect theirs, and collect the members of every record in between that intersects them; an opening record whose members do not intersect those of the open chain ends it (the engine integrates one chain at a time), and a record without a time stamp takes that of the preceding record. A chain still open at the end runs to the latest time stamp of the log. END TRIPLE and END QUAD lines are not read. Hierarchy episodes run from NEW MERGER or NEW MERGE2 of an inner pair to the next END MERGER of the same pair; unclosed ones run to the end.
  3. Spans. Consecutive episodes of a pair are joined when the gap g between them is ≤ 0 or at most n_p periods of the earlier episode (its closing record, else its opening record), or when a chain holding both components or a hierarchy with the pair as inner binary covers the gap, and in either case no episode of another pair sharing a component overlaps the gap.
  4. Origin. :primordial for the first span of a primordial pair, (2k − 1, 2k) with 2k ≤ 2·NBIN0, formed at t_start_nb or listed at the start; :primordial_reformed for its later spans; :exchange when an EXCHANGE or CHAIN BINARY line leaves the pair bound at the formation time and a component was in a span of another pair before; :dynamical otherwise, with the sublabel :gw_capture (GR CAPTURE of the pair) or :capture (NEW CAPTURE, tidal) at a pericentre of the first episode, which the hyperbolic NEW KSREG opened, else :three_body (END CHAIN, CHTERM or CHAIN BINARY with both components as members) at the formation time. mixed marks a non-primordial origin with a component that was earlier in a span of another primordial pair.

The end class is that of the last episode, the first that applies of:

  1. :censored_run_end: open at the end of the log;
  2. :gw_merger: a collision of the pair at the close with a GR KSINT COLL or COLL DUE TO GW UNPERT trigger, at the same time or, the trigger carrying no time, within 200 log lines before the closing record;
  3. :stellar_merger: a coalescence (coal.24, ROCHE COAL) of the pair;
  4. :collision: a collision of the pair without a GW trigger;
  5. :escape_bound: a BINARY ESCAPE of the pair;
  6. :exchange: an EXCHANGE line within 0.01 NB of the close whose initial pair is this one or whose final pair holds one of its components, or a CHAIN BINARY line there holding one of its components;
  7. :sn_disruption: closed by MDEND KSREG;
  8. :flyby: closed by END KSREG unbound (positive binding energy or a < 0) and opened unbound: a hyperbolic encounter that never was a binary;
  9. :disruption: closed by END KSREG unbound after a bound opening;
  10. :unresolved: none of these.

Tolerances: records of one family are simultaneous within a relative 10⁻⁹ (E17.10 stamps); records of different files or families (F9.2 stamps) are joined within 0.01 NB.

Returns the spans sorted by (t_form_nb, name1, name2), an empty vector for an empty log. Throws ArgumentError for nzero ≤ 0, nbin0 < 0, a tstar_myr or n_p that is not finite and positive, or a non-finite t_start_nb.

Examples

dir = "runs/<run_id>/output"
log = read_binary_events(joinpath(dir, "out1000"))
spans = binary_spans(
    log;
    listings = read_all_ks_pair_listings(dir),
    collisions = read_collision_log(joinpath(dir, "coll.13")),
    coalescences = read_coalescence_log(joinpath(dir, "coal.24")),
    nzero = 20_000,
    nbin0 = 2_000,
    tstar_myr = 0.87,
)
write_binary_spans(joinpath(dir, "binary_spans.csv"), spans)
source
Nbody6Dynamics.write_binary_spans — Function
write_binary_spans(path, spans::AbstractVector{BinarySpan}) -> String

Write the spans of binary_spans as CSV, one column per field of BinarySpan in declaration order (times in N-body units and Myr, symbols as text, non-finite values as empty cells, absent stellar types as -99). An existing file is backed up, never overwritten. Returns the path.

CensusSpan

Nbody6Dynamics.binary_census — Function
binary_census(level_e::AbstractVector{BinarySpan}, level_p::AbstractVector{BoundSpan};
              nbin0::Integer) -> Vector{CensusSpan}

Union of the engine-regularised spans (binary_spans) and the physically bound spans (bound_spans) of each pair: spans of the same pair whose intervals overlap — a level-P span widened to the midpoints between its first member snapshot and the one before, and between its last member snapshot and the one after — are joined, transitively, into one CensusSpan. A pair can have several census spans (it separates and re-forms). Flyby spans of level E (:flyby, never bound) are left out. Returns the spans sorted by (t_form_nb, name1, name2); ArgumentError for nbin0 < 0.

Example

e = binary_spans(read_binary_events(log); nzero, nbin0, tstar_myr)
p = bound_spans(read_all_conf3(dir); nbin0)
census = binary_census(e, p; nbin0)
source
Nbody6Dynamics.write_binary_census — Function
write_binary_census(path, spans::AbstractVector{CensusSpan}) -> String

Write the census as CSV, one column per field of CensusSpan in declaration order (non-finite values as empty cells). An existing file is backed up, never overwritten. Returns the path.

Gravitational-Wave Observables

Nbody6Dynamics.gw_enhancement — Function
gw_enhancement(e) -> Float64

Peters–Mathews enhancement of the orbit-averaged GW luminosity of an eccentric binary over the circular one with the same semi-major axis,

F(e) = (1 + 73e²/24 + 37e⁴/96) / (1 − e²)^{7/2},

which is also the sum over harmonics Σₙ g(n, e) (Peters & Mathews 1963, DOI 10.1103/PhysRev.131.435). Throws an ArgumentError unless 0 ≤ e < 1.

Example

gw_enhancement(0.5)   # ≈ 4.88
source
Nbody6Dynamics.gw_time_factor — Function
gw_time_factor(e0) -> Float64

Ratio F(e₀) ≡ T/[Tc(1 − e₀²)^{7/2}] of the exact Peters (1964) coalescence time T to the circular time Tc of the same semi-major axis, scaled by (1 − e₀²)^{7/2}. It rises from 1 at e₀ = 0 (minimum ≈ 0.98 near e₀ = 0.5) to 768/425 as e₀ → 1, the high-eccentricity limit of Peters (1964, DOI 10.1103/PhysRev.136.B1224).

Evaluated by singularity-free quadratures of the Peters integral (adaptive Gauss–Kronrod, relative tolerance 10⁻¹², k = 121/304): the x-form (e = e₀x) for e₀ ≤ 0.5 and the τ-form (1 − e² = (1 − e₀²)e^τ) for e₀ > 0.5. Throws an ArgumentError unless 0 ≤ e0 < 1.

Example

gw_time_factor(0.9)   # ≈ 1.139561
source
Nbody6Dynamics.gw_inspiral_time — Function
gw_inspiral_time(m1, m2, a, e) -> Float64

Coalescence time [yr] of a binary of masses m1, m2 [M☉], semi-major axis a [au] and eccentricity e under orbit-averaged quadrupole radiation,

T = T_c (1 − e²)^{7/2} F(e),   T_c = 5c⁵a⁴ / (256 G³ m₁ m₂ M),

with F from gw_time_factor (Peters 1964, DOI 10.1103/PhysRev.136.B1224). No post-Newtonian correction is applied; gw_zwick_factor gives the 1PN-recalibrated factor separately. Throws an ArgumentError for non-positive masses or a, or e outside [0, 1).

Example

gw_inspiral_time(30.0, 30.0, 1.0, 0.999)   # ≈ 3.6×10³ yr
source
Nbody6Dynamics.gw_inspiral_time_mandel — Function
gw_inspiral_time_mandel(m1, m2, a, e) -> Float64

Coalescence time [yr] by the fit of Mandel (2021, DOI 10.3847/2515-5172/ac2d35),

T ≈ T_c (1 + 0.27e¹⁰ + 0.33e²⁰ + 0.2e¹⁰⁰⁰)(1 − e²)^{7/2},

accurate to better than 3 % for 0 ≤ e ≤ 0.99999 against gw_inspiral_time; units and argument checks as there.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.gw_zwick_factor — Function
gw_zwick_factor(m1, m2, a, e) -> Float64

1PN-recalibrated correction factor Qf = exp(2.5 rS/p₀) of the Peters coalescence time (Zwick et al. 2020, DOI 10.1093/mnras/staa1314), with rS = 2GM/c² the Schwarzschild radius of the total mass and p₀ = a(1 − e) the pericentre distance. Masses in M☉, a in au. Zwick et al. fitted Qf to inspirals evolved with the 3.5PN flux and state it as accurate within 1PN precision; they give no validity range, the uncertainties of the fitted coefficients overlap only for p₀ ≲ 35 rS, and the factor is meaningless near the ISCO. Identifying their initial (a₀, e₀) with the Newtonian elements of the engine is ambiguous at O(rS/p₀). The factor is therefore a sensitivity measure reported on its own; gw_inspiral_time never applies it.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.chirp_mass — Function
chirp_mass(m1, m2) -> Float64

Chirp mass 𝓜 = (m₁m₂)^{3/5}/M^{1/5} [M☉] of component masses m1, m2 [M☉], M = m₁ + m₂. Throws an ArgumentError for non-positive masses.

Example

chirp_mass(30.0, 30.0)   # ≈ 26.12
source
Nbody6Dynamics.orbital_frequency — Function
orbital_frequency(m1, m2, a) -> Float64

Keplerian orbital frequency f_orb = (2π)⁻¹ √(GM/a³) [Hz] of masses m1, m2 [M☉] at semi-major axis a [au]. Throws an ArgumentError for non-positive masses or a.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.gw_frequency — Function
gw_frequency(m1, m2, a, e; definition = :f22) -> Float64

GW frequency [Hz] of a binary of masses m1, m2 [M☉], semi-major axis a [au] and eccentricity e, under one of the definitions in use:

  • :f22: dominant quadrupole harmonic, f₂₂ = 2f_orb;
  • :wen: peak harmonic of Wen (2003, DOI 10.1086/378794), f = (√(GM)/π)(1 + e)^{1.1954}/[a(1 − e²)]^{1.5};
  • :peri: circular orbit of the pericentre radius, f = π⁻¹ √(GM/rp³), rp = a(1 − e).

All three coincide at e = 0. The definitions differ by large factors at high e, so a product that stores the frequency names the definition in its column. Throws an ArgumentError for an unknown definition or invalid orbit.

Example

gw_frequency(30.0, 30.0, 1.0, 0.999; definition = :wen)   # ≈ 12.6×10⁻³ Hz
source
Nbody6Dynamics.eccentricity_at_frequency — Function
eccentricity_at_frequency(m1, m2, a0, e0, f_ref; definition = :f22)
    -> NamedTuple{(:e, :a_au, :time_to_ref_yr, :above_at_record)}

Eccentricity of a binary recorded at (a0 [au], e0), masses m1, m2 [M☉], when its GW frequency of the given definition (see gw_frequency) has risen to f_ref [Hz] under the Peters (1964) evolution.

Along the track a(e) = c₀e^{12/19}(1 + ke²)^{870/2299}/(1 − e²), k = 121/304, with c₀ fixed by (a₀, e₀), each definition's frequency decreases monotonically with e, so the root of f(e) = f_ref on (0, e₀] is unique; it is found by bisection in ln e to a relative precision of 10⁻¹² in e, starting from e₀·10⁻¹² as the lower limit (extended by up to three further factors of 10⁻¹² when needed). For e₀ = 0 the track is circular and e stays 0.

Returns e, the semi-major axis a_au [au] there, the time time_to_ref_yr [yr] = T(a₀, e₀) − T(aref, eref) of gw_inspiral_time, and above_at_record; a binary already at or above f_ref at the record returns NaN for the three values and above_at_record = true. Throws an ArgumentError for an invalid orbit, a non-positive f_ref, an unknown definition, or a root below the extended lower limit.

Example

eccentricity_at_frequency(30.0, 30.0, 1.0, 0.999, 10.0; definition = :wen).e   # ≈ 4.42×10⁻⁴
source
Nbody6Dynamics.peters_mathews_g — Function
peters_mathews_g(n::Integer, e::Real) -> Float64

Relative GW power g(n, e) radiated in the n-th harmonic of the orbital frequency by a Keplerian binary of eccentricity e (Peters & Mathews 1963, DOI 10.1103/PhysRev.131.435, Eq. 20). With Jₖ = Jₖ(ne) the Bessel functions of the first kind,

g(n, e) = (n⁴/32) { [J_{n−2} − 2e J_{n−1} + (2/n) Jₙ + 2e J_{n+1} − J_{n+2}]²
                    + (1 − e²) [J_{n−2} − 2Jₙ + J_{n+2}]² + (4/(3n²)) Jₙ² }.

A circular orbit radiates only in n = 2, g(2, 0) = 1 and g(n, 0) = 0 for n ≠ 2, and the harmonics sum to the enhancement, Σ{n≥1} g(n, e) = [`gwenhancement](@ref)(e). Throws anArgumentErrorforn < 1ore` outside [0, 1).

Example

peters_mathews_g(2, 0.0)   # 1.0
source
Nbody6Dynamics.gw_amplitude_ldc — Function
gw_amplitude_ldc(mchirp_msun::Real, f_gw_hz::Real; distance_kpc::Real = 1.0) -> Float64

Strain amplitude of the leading (l = m = 2) mode of a circular binary of chirp mass mchirp_msun [M☉] at GW frequency f_gw_hz [Hz] and distance distance_kpc [kpc], in the convention of the LISA Data Challenge galactic binaries (LDC manual, Eqs. 30–32a and 45–46),

𝒜 = 2 (G𝓜)^{5/3} (πf)^{2/3} / (c⁴ D).

𝒜 is half the face-on amplitude h₀ = 4(G𝓜)^{5/3}(πf)^{2/3}/(c⁴D) of the quadrupole formula and carries no orientation average. The amplitude scales as 1/D: the catalogues store the distance-free product 𝒜 × D at D = 1 kpc, and the value at a distance D is the stored one divided by D in kpc. Throws an ArgumentError for a non-positive chirp mass, frequency or distance.

Example

gw_amplitude_ldc(0.5, 1e-3)   # ≈ 1.87×10⁻²² at 1 kpc
source
Nbody6Dynamics.gw_harmonic_strain — Function
gw_harmonic_strain(m1::Real, m2::Real, a_au::Real, e::Real, n::Integer;
                   distance_kpc::Real = 1.0) -> Float64

Orientation- and polarisation-averaged strain amplitude hₙ of the n-th harmonic (frequency fₙ = n forb) of a binary of masses m1, m2 [M☉], semi-major axis `aau[au] and eccentricityeat distancedistance_kpc` [kpc] (Wagg, Broekgaarden & Gültekin 2022, LEGWORK, DOI 10.3847/1538-4365/ac5c52, Eq. 27),

hₙ² = (2^{28/3}/5) (G𝓜)^{10/3} / (c⁸ D²) · g(n, e)/n² · (π f_orb)^{4/3},

with forb = (2π)⁻¹ √(GM/a³), 𝓜 the [`chirpmass](@ref) and g from [petersmathewsg](@ref). A circular binary radiates only in n = 2, where h₂ = (4/√5) [gwamplitudeldc](@ref)(𝓜, 2f_orb) = (4/5)^{1/2} h₀, the face-on amplitude h₀ = 2𝒜 times the root of the orientation average ⟨[(1 + cos²ι)/2]² + cos²ι⟩ = 4/5. The strain scales as 1/D: the catalogues store hₙ × D at D = 1 kpc, and the value at a distance D is the stored one divided by D in kpc. Throws anArgumentErrorfor non-positive masses,a_auor distance,eoutside [0, 1) orn < 1`.

Example

gw_harmonic_strain(0.6, 0.6, 0.01, 0.0, 2; distance_kpc = 8.0)
source
Nbody6Dynamics.gw_characteristic_strain — Function
gw_characteristic_strain(m1::Real, m2::Real, a_au::Real, e::Real, n::Integer;
                         distance_kpc::Real = 1.0) -> Float64

Orientation-averaged characteristic strain h{c,n} of the n-th harmonic of an inspiralling binary of masses m1, m2 [M☉], semi-major axis `aau[au] and eccentricityeat distancedistance_kpc` [kpc] (Wagg, Broekgaarden & Gültekin 2022, LEGWORK, DOI 10.3847/1538-4365/ac5c52, Eq. 26, after Barack & Cutler 2004, DOI 10.1103/PhysRevD.69.082005),

h_{c,n}² = (2^{5/3} / (3π^{4/3})) (G𝓜)^{5/3} / (c³ D²) · f_orb^{−1/3} · g(n, e) / (n F(e)),

with forb = (2π)⁻¹ √(GM/a³), g from [`petersmathewsg](@ref) and F the [gwenhancement](@ref). It equals h_{c,n}² = (fₙ²/ḟₙ) hₙ² of the [gwharmonicstrain](@ref) hₙ and the drift ḟₙ of [gwharmonicfrequencyderivative`](@ref), fₙ = n forb. The strain scales as 1/D: the catalogues store h{c,n} × D at D = 1 kpc, and the value at a distance D is the stored one divided by D in kpc. Throws an ArgumentError for non-positive masses, `aauor distance,eoutside [0, 1) orn < 1`.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.gw_harmonic_frequency_derivative — Function
gw_harmonic_frequency_derivative(m1::Real, m2::Real, a_au::Real, e::Real, n::Integer)
    -> Float64

Orbit-averaged drift ḟₙ = n ḟorb [Hz s⁻¹] of the frequency fₙ = n forb of the n-th harmonic under quadrupole radiation, for masses m1, m2 [M☉], semi-major axis a_au [au] and eccentricity e (Peters 1964, DOI 10.1103/PhysRev.136.B1224),

ḟ_orb = (48/5) π^{8/3} (G𝓜/c³)^{5/3} (2f_orb)^{11/3} F(e),

with F the gw_enhancement; for n = 2 at e = 0 it is the circular chirp ḟ = (96/5) π^{8/3} (G𝓜/c³)^{5/3} f^{11/3} of f = 2forb. Throws an ArgumentError for non-positive masses or `aau,eoutside [0, 1) orn < 1`.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.lisa_psd — Function
lisa_psd(f_hz::Real; t_obs_yr::Real = 4.0, confusion::Bool = true) -> Float64

Effective sky- and polarisation-averaged strain noise power spectral density S_n(f) [Hz⁻¹] of LISA (Robson, Cornish & Liu 2019, DOI 10.1088/1361-6382/ab1101, Eqs. 10, 11, 13 and 14):

S_n(f) = 10/(3L²) [P_OMS + 2(1 + cos²(f/f*)) P_acc/(2πf)⁴] [1 + 0.6 (f/f*)²] + S_c(f),

with L = 2.5 Gm, f* = 19.09 mHz, POMS = (1.5×10⁻¹¹ m)² [1 + (2 mHz/f)⁴] Hz⁻¹, Pacc = (3×10⁻¹⁵ m s⁻²)² [1 + (0.4 mHz/f)²] [1 + (f/8 mHz)⁴] Hz⁻¹ and the Galactic confusion noise Sc(f) = A f^{−7/3} exp(−f^α + β f sin κf) [1 + tanh γ(fk − f)], A = 9×10⁻⁴⁵, with the fit parameters of their Table 1 for the observation time t_obs_yr (0.5, 1, 2 or 4 yr). confusion = false leaves out Sc (any positive `tobsyris then accepted). The curve is the one LEGWORK uses (Wagg et al. 2022, DOI 10.3847/1538-4365/ac5c52, Eq. 64), so the SNRs of [gwsnr](@ref) follow its conventions. Throws anArgumentErrorforf_hz ≤ 0` or an observation time without a confusion fit.

Example

sqrt(lisa_psd(3.0e-3))   # ≈ 1.8×10⁻²⁰ Hz^{-1/2} with the 4-yr confusion noise
source
Nbody6Dynamics.gw_snr — Function
gw_snr(m1::Real, m2::Real, a_au::Real, e::Real; distance_kpc::Real = 1.0,
       t_obs_yr::Real = 4.0, confusion::Bool = true, n_time::Integer = 65) -> Float64

Sky-, orientation- and polarisation-averaged LISA signal-to-noise ratio of a binary of masses m1, m2 [M☉], semi-major axis a_au [au] and eccentricity e at distance distance_kpc [kpc], observed for t_obs_yr years from the given state (Wagg et al. 2022, LEGWORK, DOI 10.3847/1538-4365/ac5c52, Eq. 32):

ρ² = Σₙ ∫ h_{c,n}²/(fₙ² S_n(fₙ)) dfₙ = ∫₀^T Σₙ hₙ²(t)/S_n(fₙ(t)) dt,

the second form by h{c,n}² = (fₙ²/ḟₙ) hₙ², with hₙ the [`gwharmonicstrain`](@ref), Sn the lisa_psd (t_obs_yr, confusion) and (a, e) evolved over the observation by the Peters (1964) equations (Tsit5, relative tolerance 10⁻¹⁰), up to the time the pericentre reaches 6GM/c². The time integral uses Simpson's rule on n_time (odd) equally spaced points; for a stationary source the integrand is constant and ρ² = Σₙ hₙ² T/Sn(fₙ) (LEGWORK Eq. 35), for a circular one only n = 2 enters. Harmonics up to five times the Wen (2003) peak are summed. ρ scales as 1/D, so the value at 1 kpc divided by the distance in kpc gives the SNR at that distance. Throws an ArgumentError for non-positive masses, `aau, distance ortobsyr,eoutside [0, 1) or an evenn_time` below 3.

Example

gw_snr(0.5, 0.25, a, 0.0)   # SDSS J0651+2844 at 1 kpc (f = 2.6 mHz): ≈ 140 (Robson et al. 2019)
source

Binary Population Series

Nbody6Dynamics.BinaryPopulationRow — Type
BinaryPopulationRow

Binary fractions of one snapshot, census level, radial bin and stellar class from binary_population_series. Radii are in the N-body units of the snapshot, masses in M☉.

f_b_number$f_b = N_b / (N_s + N_b)$—

f_b_mass$f_M = M_b / (M_s + M_b)$—
f_b_number_sebinomial standard error $\sqrt{f_b (1 - f_b) / (N_s + N_b)}$—
f_b_qfraction of binaries with $q > q_\mathrm{min}$ (and, with stellar types, two main-sequence components) among the systems with single or primary mass in the window—
r_h_binaries_nb, r_h_singles_nbhalf-mass radii of the binaries and singles of the row; NaN on shellsNB

Fractions with a vanishing denominator are NaN.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.binary_population_series — Function
binary_population_series(snapshots::AbstractVector{Snapshot}; levels = (:ks, :bound),
                         radius_frame::Symbol = :lagrangian, edges = nothing,
                         centre = :shrinking_sphere, centre_kwargs::NamedTuple = (;),
                         kstar = nothing, q_min::Real = 0.5,
                         primary_window_msun::Tuple{<:Real,<:Real} = (0.47, 0.76),
                         k::Integer = 10, n_perturbers::Integer = 4,
                         gamma_max::Real = 0.1, max_untyped_fraction::Real = 0.01,
                         projection_axis::Integer = 3, r_tide_nb::Real = NaN)
    -> Vector{BinaryPopulationRow}

Binary fractions of a snapshot sequence per census level, radial bin and stellar class, one BinaryPopulationRow per snapshot × level × bin × class, in that order.

Arguments

  • snapshots: the snapshots
  • levels: census levels, any of :ks (level E) and :bound (level P)
  • radius_frame: :lagrangian (shells in enclosed mass fraction), :rh (shells in units of the half-mass radius), :projected_rh (shells in units of the projected half-mass radius) or :milone (the three projected regions of Milone et al. 2012)
  • edges: shell edges, strictly increasing from 0; mass fractions ending at 1 for :lagrangian (default [0, 0.1, 0.25, 0.5, 0.75, 0.9, 1]), multiples of rh for :rh and of Rh for :projected_rh (default [0, 0.5, 1, 2, 4, Inf]); :milone takes no edges
  • centre: :shrinking_sphere (shrinking_sphere_centre of the stars of each snapshot, with centre_kwargs), :origin, or a fixed point (length-3 vector, NB)
  • kstar: nothing (class :all only) or a vector aligned with snapshots of name → K* maps, e.g. from stellar_type_map. A star without a type counts under :all only: the engine lists neither the outer body of a merged hierarchy (a KS pair with a negative pair_name) nor the body that represents a chain subsystem in sev.83 or bev.82
  • q_min, primary_window_msun: mass-ratio threshold and inclusive primary mass window [M☉] of f_b_q; the default window is that of Milone et al. (2012, DOI 10.1051/0004-6361/201016384, §5), 0.47 < M < 0.76 M☉
  • k, n_perturbers, gamma_max: pair membership at level P, see bound_pairs
  • max_untyped_fraction: largest fraction of the massive stars of a snapshot without a stellar type; the untyped stars up to it count under :all only, more indicates mismatched epochs and raises an ArgumentError
  • projection_axis: line of sight of the projected frames, 1, 2 or 3 (x, y, z); the projected radius is the distance from the centre in the plane normal to it
  • r_tide_nb: tidal radius [NB]; when finite, the systems farther than it from the centre (three-dimensional distance) are removed before any count or radius, and the total number removed is logged once at @info level; NaN (default) or Inf keeps every system

Massless bodies are no systems: an unpaired massless star (the ghost of a merged or chain subsystem) is left out of every count, and a pair with a massless component (in particular one of summed mass 0) is not formed, its massive component, if any, counting as a single.

Method

At a level the stars of a snapshot form systems. Level :ks takes the KS pairs (stars 2k − 1 and 2k) whose centre-of-mass name is positive. A KS slot with a negative name is a merged hierarchy: star 2k − 1 is the first component of the inner binary, star 2k the outer body, and the second inner component is written among the single stars; the binary is then (2k − 1, j), j the star nearest to 2k − 1 (Euclidean distance, ties to the lower index) among the massive stars that are not KS components, counted in n_hierarchies, and the outer body 2k is a single. Level :bound takes the pairs of bound_pairs; the inner pair of a stable triple is a binary and its outer body a single. Every other star is a single. A binary has the mass m₁ + m₂ and the position of its centre of mass; its primary is the heavier component and q = m₂/m₁ ≤ 1. Masses are converted to M☉ with zmbar.

Radii are measured from the centre of each snapshot, computed once for all levels. The half-mass radius rh of a set of systems is the radius of the first system, in radius order, at which the enclosed mass reaches half the total. With :rh shell b holds the systems with ``eb rh ≤ r < e{b+1} rh$; with `:lagrangian` those whose enclosed mass fraction lies in$(eb, e_{b+1}]``. Bin 0 holds every system.

The projected frames take the projected radius R, the distance from the centre in the plane normal to projection_axis, and the projected half-mass radius Rh, defined from R as rh is from r. With :projected_rh shell b holds the systems with $e_b R_h ≤ R < e_{b+1} R_h$. :milone has the three regions of Milone et al. (2012, DOI 10.1051/0004-6361/201016384, §3.2): bin 1 $R < R_c$, bin 2 $R_c ≤ R < R_h$ and bin 3 $R_h ≤ R < 2R_h$, with edge_lo, edge_hi in units of Rh. Rc is the projected radius at which the projected number density of the systems falls to half its central value. With $n_a = \max(20, ⌈0.01 N⌉)$ systems, the central density is that inside the projected radius of the $n_a$-th system in R order, and the profile is measured in consecutive annuli of $n_a$ systems each, placed at their area-weighted mean radius $\sqrt{(R_\mathrm{in}^2 + R_\mathrm{out}^2)/2}$ (the central disc at $R_{n_a}/\sqrt{2}$); Rc is interpolated linearly in R between the last point above half the central density and the first at or below it. It is NaN, and bins 1 and 2 are empty, when the profile does not fall to half, or with fewer than ``2na`` systems.

With a finite r_tide_nb the systems beyond the tidal radius are removed first; every count, rh, Rh and R_c refer to the systems inside it.

With $N_s$ singles and $N_b$ binaries of total masses $M_s$ and $M_b$, the number fraction is $f_b = N_b/(N_s + N_b)$, with binomial standard error $\sqrt{f_b(1 - f_b)/(N_s + N_b)}$, the fraction of stars in binaries $f_⋆ = 2N_b/(N_s + 2N_b) = 2f_b/(1 + f_b)$ and the mass fraction $f_M = M_b/(M_s + M_b)$. A class row counts the singles of the class and the binaries whose primary belongs to it; its n_in_binaries and m_binaries_msun count the components of the class in any binary, so that its $f_⋆$ is the fraction of the members of the class bound in pairs, whatever their partner. f_b_q is the fraction of binaries with q > q_min among the systems whose single or primary mass lies in primary_window_msun; with stellar types a binary enters its numerator only when both components are of class :main_sequence, since a photometric binary needs a luminous main-sequence companion (without types every binary with q > q_min does). On :main_sequence rows it is the analogue of the q-restricted fractions measured photometrically in globular clusters (Milone et al. 2012, DOI 10.1051/0004-6361/201016384). Their mass window is set relative to the turn-off of each cluster, so the fixed default suits old globular clusters only; younger populations need a window of their own.

A fraction is meaningful only together with the period range of its level: level E is restricted to the pairs the integrator regularised, level P extends to every pair bound against its nearest perturbers, and observed fractions refer to their own period windows (Hurley et al. 2005, DOI 10.1111/j.1365-2966.2005.09448.x). Photometric fractions of unresolved systems, which include chance superpositions, are not computed.

Throws ArgumentError for an empty snapshots, empty or unknown levels, an unknown radius_frame, invalid edges (any edges with :milone), an unknown centre, a kstar not aligned with snapshots or leaving more than max_untyped_fraction of the massive stars of a snapshot untyped, q_min outside (0, 1), a window with lo ≥ hi or lo < 0, a projection_axis other than 1, 2 or 3, r_tide_nb ≤ 0, and for a star in two pairs.

Example

snaps = read_all_conf3(dir)
rows = binary_population_series(snaps; levels = (:ks,), radius_frame = :rh)
write_binary_population(joinpath(dir, "binary_population.csv"), rows)
source

Nbody6Dynamics.binary_pair_type_series — Function
binary_pair_type_series(snapshots::AbstractVector{Snapshot}; levels = (:ks, :bound),
                        radius_frame::Symbol = :lagrangian, edges = nothing,
                        centre = :shrinking_sphere, centre_kwargs::NamedTuple = (;),
                        kstar = nothing, k::Integer = 10, n_perturbers::Integer = 4,
                        gamma_max::Real = 0.1, max_untyped_fraction::Real = 0.01,
                        r_tide_nb::Real = NaN, projection_axis::Integer = 3)
    -> Vector{BinaryPairTypeRow}

Pair-type composition, hardness and median orbital elements of the binaries of a snapshot sequence, one BinaryPairTypeRow per snapshot × level × bin, in that order, bin 0 holding every binary.

Arguments

The keywords, the census levels, the systems, the radial frames, the centre and the tidal filter are those of binary_population_series. Without kstar every binary counts in n_untyped.

Method

Each component is classified by its stellar type K* with the ranges of STELLAR_CLASSES: main sequence (MS) K* ∈ {−1, 0, 1}, evolved 2–9, white dwarf (WD) 10–12, neutron star (NS) 13, black hole (BH) 14. A pair (k₁, k₂) takes the first matching rule: a component without a type → n_untyped; any NS → n_ns_any; two BHs → n_bh_bh; one BH with an MS or evolved star → n_bh_star; BH with WD → n_other; two WDs → n_wd_wd; MS with WD → n_ms_wd; two MS stars → n_ms_ms; MS with an evolved star → n_ms_evolved; any other pair → n_other.

The semi-major axis a, eccentricity e and binding energy $E_b = m_1 m_2/(2a)$ follow from the relative orbit of the two components in the snapshot (G = 1); pairs that are not bound have no elements. A binary is hard when $\lvert E_b \rvert > ⟨m⟩σ²$ (Heggie 1975, DOI 10.1093/mnras/173.3.729), with ⟨m⟩ the mean mass and σ the one-dimensional velocity dispersion, $σ² = \sum_i \lvert \mathbf{v}_i - \bar{\mathbf{v}} \rvert^2 / (3n)$, of the n systems of the level within their three-dimensional half-mass radius, per snapshot and level and after the tidal filter; e_hs_nb is this boundary. The mass ratio is q = m₂/m₁ ≤ 1.

Throws ArgumentError as binary_population_series.

Example

snaps = read_all_conf3(dir)
rows = binary_pair_type_series(snaps; levels = (:ks,), radius_frame = :milone,
                               kstar = kmaps)
write_binary_pair_types(joinpath(dir, "binary_pair_types.csv"), rows)
source

Nbody6Dynamics.binary_distribution_series — Function
binary_distribution_series(snapshots::AbstractVector{Snapshot}; levels = (:ks, :bound),
                           centre = :shrinking_sphere, centre_kwargs::NamedTuple = (;),
                           k::Integer = 10, n_perturbers::Integer = 4,
                           gamma_max::Real = 0.1, r_tide_nb::Real = NaN,
                           edges_log_a_au = -3.0:0.25:3.0, edges_e = 0.0:0.1:1.0,
                           edges_q = 0.0:0.1:1.0, edges_log_eb = -2.0:0.25:4.0)
    -> Vector{BinaryDistributionRow}

Histograms of the semi-major axis, eccentricity, mass ratio and binding energy of the binaries of a snapshot sequence, one BinaryDistributionRow per snapshot × level × quantity × bin, in that order, with every bin present (zeros included) and bin 0 first.

Arguments

  • snapshots, levels, centre, centre_kwargs, k, n_perturbers, gamma_max, r_tide_nb: as in binary_population_series
  • edges_log_a_au, edges_e, edges_q, edges_log_eb: histogram edges, finite and strictly increasing, of log₁₀(a/au), e, q and log₁₀($\lvert E_b \rvert$/⟨m⟩σ²)

Method

The binaries and their elements a, e and $E_b = m_1 m_2/(2a)$ are those of binary_pair_type_series; a is converted to au with rbar and 1 pc = 206264.806 au, q = m₂/m₁ ≤ 1, and $\lvert E_b \rvert$ is measured in units of the hard/soft boundary ⟨m⟩σ² of the snapshot and level (Heggie 1975, DOI 10.1093/mnras/173.3.729), so that :log_eb = 0 is the boundary. Bin b holds the values $e_b ≤ x < e_{b+1}$, the last bin also $x = e_\mathrm{last}$; values outside the edges and binaries without elements (pairs not bound in the snapshot) are counted in bin 0. The counts of a snapshot, level and quantity therefore sum to its number of binaries.

Throws ArgumentError for the arguments rejected by binary_population_series and for invalid edges.

Example

rows = binary_distribution_series(read_all_conf3(dir); levels = (:ks,))
write_binary_distributions(joinpath(dir, "binary_distributions.csv"), rows)
source

Nbody6Dynamics.MBHRow — Type
MBHRow

One record of mbh.45, from read_mbh45.

#LabelFieldUnit
1STATstate: :binary (the IMBH is a KS component) or :single—
2Time[Myr]time_myr ((TIME + TOFF)·T*)Myr
3IBHindex: the engine's array index of the IMBH at that time—
4–6X(1:3)[PC]pos_pc (absolute, not about the density centre)pc
7–9V(1:3)[km/s]vel_kms (absolute)km s⁻¹
10NBn_neighbours (NNBT: the neighbour list, plus the KS partner)—
11–13XAVE(1:3)[AU]xave_au, mass-weighted mean neighbour offsetau
14–16VAVE(1:3)[km/s]vave_kms, mass-weighted mean neighbour velocitykm s⁻¹
17DEN[M*/PC^3]density_msun_pc3M☉ pc⁻³
18RIJMAX[PC]rijmax_pcpc
19–21VSIGMA(1:3)[km^2/s^2]vsigma_km2s2km² s⁻²
22NAMEname: the body name of the IMBH (fork v2026.07.11 on; 0 when the record has no name column)—

source
Nbody6Dynamics.read_mbh45 — Function
read_mbh45(path::AbstractString) -> Vector{MBHRow}

Read mbh.45, the IMBH record written by the engine's bhplot.f every DTBH under KZ(24) = 1 or 3 (fork bhplot.f:28-40, 197-199): a label line whose first token is STAT, then one list-directed record per output, WHICH (BINARY or SINGLE) followed by 20 numbers, or 21 from fork v2026.07.11 on, the last being the body name of the IMBH (name, written as a list-directed integer or real and rounded; 0 for 20-number records), read into one MBHRow each; name tells the IMBHs of KZ(24) = 3 apart. Blank lines and label lines are skipped wherever they occur (a restarted run appends). A record with another token count or an unreadable value raises an ArgumentError naming the line number; a missing file raises an error.

Defects of the engine's writer that the reader cannot repair:

  • The separation RIJ of bhplot.f:133 and bhplot.f:157 is overwritten by the square of each coordinate component instead of summed, so it holds |Δz| alone: rijmax_pc is the largest |Δz| over the companion and the neighbours, not the largest separation, and density_msun_pc3 inherits it through its volume. The companion term of VSIGMA (bhplot.f:182) is weighted by BODY(I), I left at the last neighbour of the list loop, instead of the companion's mass. The three columns are not usable as a local density or dispersion.
  • The positions and velocities are absolute, not about the density centre; imbh_trajectory gives the offset from the stellar density centre at the snapshots.
  • Nothing is written while the IMBH sits in a chain or in a merged hierarchy (NAME(ICM) ≤ 0), so gaps in the record are expected.
  • index is the array index of the IMBH at the output, which changes as the engine reorders its particles; it is not the name.

Example

rows = read_mbh45(joinpath(run_dir, "output", "mbh.45"))
binary_fraction = count(r -> r.state === :binary, rows) / length(rows)
source

Nbody6Dynamics.imbh_trajectory — Function
imbh_trajectory(snaps::AbstractVector{Snapshot}, name::Integer; centre_kwargs...)
    -> Vector{IMBHState}

State of the IMBH of body name name at every snapshot that holds it, one IMBHState each, about the stars (every other body). The density centre is shrinking_sphere_centre of the stars with centre_kwargs (the centre keys of PostprocessConfig), the reference velocity the mass-weighted mean velocity of the stars; the half-mass radius of the stars is measured about the density centre and their core radius by core_radius from the snapshot's density column (NaN without one). A KS component (index ≤ 2 npairs) has its partner at index + 1 for odd and index − 1 for even index.

Snapshots without the IMBH (escaped, merged, or inside a chain or merged hierarchy, whose components conf.3 does not list) are skipped and counted in one @info. ArgumentError for an empty snaps.

Example

traj = imbh_trajectory(read_all_conf3(dir), imbh_name)
r_rms = sqrt(sum(s -> s.r_pc^2, traj) / length(traj))
source
Nbody6Dynamics.imbh_wandering — Function
imbh_wandering(traj::AbstractVector{IMBHState}; window_myr::Real = 0.0,
               frame::Symbol = :density_centre) -> Vector{NamedTuple}

Wandering statistics of the IMBH offsets from the stellar density centre (frame = :density_centre, the shrinking-sphere centre, x_pc) or from the stellar centre of mass (frame = :centre_of_mass, x_cm_pc, the frame of Chatterjee, Hernquist & Loeb 2002), one row per time window: the whole span for window_myr ≤ 0, else consecutive windows [t₀ + k w, t₀ + (k + 1) w) of length w = window_myr from the first time t₀, empty windows left out; the last window takes the remainder up to the last time and is kept when it holds at least three states. States with a non-finite time are ignored. Rows (frame, t_start_myr, t_end_myr, n, x2_mean_pc2, r2_mean_pc2, v2_mean_km2s2, r_rms_pc, kurtosis):

  • x2_mean_pc2: ⟨x²⟩ per coordinate, the squared components pooled over the three axes [pc²], the quantity of brownian_rms_plummer and brownian_rms_core squared;
  • r2_mean_pc2 = ⟨r²⟩ [pc²] (≈ 3⟨x²⟩ for isotropic wandering) and r_rms_pc = √⟨r²⟩;
  • v2_mean_km2s2 = ⟨|v|²⟩ relative to the stellar centre-of-mass velocity [km² s⁻²];
  • kurtosis = ⟨x⁴⟩/⟨x²⟩² pooled over the components, 3 for a Gaussian.

The moments are taken about zero (the chosen centre), not about the sample mean. Returns an empty vector for an empty trajectory. When the wandering amplitude is below the radius of the final shrinking sphere (≈ the radius enclosing n_floor stars), the density centre follows the cusp bound to the IMBH and the density-centre frame underestimates the wandering; the centre-of-mass frame is the one to compare with the Brownian predictions.

Example

w = imbh_wandering(traj; window_myr = 50.0)
ratio = [sqrt(r.x2_mean_pc2) for r in w] ./ brownian_rms_core(σ_c, ρ_c, m_eff, M_bh)
source
Nbody6Dynamics.effective_mass — Function
effective_mass(mass::AbstractVector{<:Real}) -> Float64

Effective perturber mass m̃ = ⟨m²⟩/⟨m⟩ = Σm²/Σm of a stellar population [unit of mass], the mass that drives the Brownian motion of a massive body in a field of stars with a mass spectrum (the diffusion coefficients scale with Σ n m², the dynamical friction with Σ n m). For equal masses it is the stellar mass. ArgumentError for an empty vector or a non-positive total.

Example

effective_mass([1.0, 1.0, 2.0])   # 1.5
source
Nbody6Dynamics.brownian_rms_plummer — Function
brownian_rms_plummer(a_pc, m_eff_msun, m_imbh_msun) -> Float64

Equilibrium rms offset per coordinate [pc] of a massive body M• wandering at the centre of a Plummer sphere of scale length a [pc] made of stars of effective mass m̃ (effective_mass),

⟨x²⟩ = (2/9) a² m̃ / M•

(Chatterjee, Hernquist & Loeb 2002, DOI 10.1086/339174). Valid for m̃ ≪ M• ≪ M_cluster; the offsets are those about the stellar density centre. ArgumentError unless a_pc, m_eff_msun and m_imbh_msun are positive.

Example

brownian_rms_plummer(3π / 16, 8.0e-5, 0.01)   # ≈ 0.0248
source
Nbody6Dynamics.brownian_rms_core — Function
brownian_rms_core(sigma_c_kms, rho_c_msun_pc3, m_eff_msun, m_imbh_msun) -> Float64

Equilibrium rms offset per coordinate [pc] of a massive body M• in a core of constant density ρ_c [M☉ pc⁻³] and one-dimensional velocity dispersion σ_c [km s⁻¹], where the body moves in the harmonic potential of the core,

⟨x²⟩ = σ_c² (m̃/M•) / ω₀²,   ω₀² = (4π/3) G ρ_c,

with m̃ the effective stellar mass (effective_mass) and G = 4.30091×10⁻³ pc km² s⁻² M☉⁻¹ (Lin & Tremaine 1980, DOI 10.1086/157803; Merritt 2005, DOI 10.1086/429964). The equipartition ⟨v²⟩ = σ_c² m̃/M• per coordinate and the harmonic restoring force give the offset; it holds while the offset stays inside the core. ArgumentError unless all arguments are positive.

Example

brownian_rms_core(10.0, 1.0e4, 0.5, 1000.0)
source

Nbody6Dynamics.imbh_companions — Function
imbh_companions(snaps::AbstractVector{Snapshot}, name::Integer; k::Integer = 10,
                gamma_max::Real = Inf) -> Vector{IMBHCompanion}

Most bound companion of the IMBH of body name name at every snapshot that holds it, one IMBHCompanion each. The candidates are the k nearest stars to the IMBH and, when the IMBH is a KS component, its KS partner; for each the two-body specific energy ε = ½ |v_j − v•|² − G (M• + m_j)/|x_j − x•| is formed in physical units, and the most bound candidate j is the one of most negative ε < 0, with the elements a = −G (M• + m)/(2ε) and e = √max(0, 1 − |r × v|²/(G (M• + m) a)) and the Peters coalescence time of the pair.

The most bound candidate, at separation d from the IMBH, is then tested against the other candidates p (the KS partner included when it is one) by the perturbation parameter

γ = max_p 2 m_p d³ / ((M• + m_j) r_p³),

r_p the distance of p from the centre of mass of the pair (IMBH, j), and γ = 0 without another candidate. γ is the KS perturbation measure of the engine: the tidal acceleration of the perturber over the pair's internal acceleration, up to the factor 2 of a perturber at the pair's distance. The candidate is accepted as companion when γ < gamma_max (in the pipeline PostprocessConfig.bound_gamma_max); otherwise the row has companion_name = 0 and NaN elements as for no bound candidate, with the rejected candidate's γ in gamma. A snapshot without a bound candidate gives a row with companion_name = 0 and gamma = NaN. Without the perturbation limit (gamma_max = Inf) the two-body test over the ten nearest stars acts as a cusp detector around a massive body: the stars inside the IMBH's radius of influence are two-body bound by construction.

conf.3 positions are single precision, so elements of pairs whose separation lies below ≈ 10⁻⁵ of the cluster's distance from the coordinate origin are noise (IMBH study §4.6). The companion's stellar type is not in conf.3; join by name to sev.83/bev.82 for it. ArgumentError for k < 0.

Example

comp = imbh_companions(read_all_conf3(dir), imbh_name; gamma_max = 0.1)
imbh_companion_summary(comp).fraction_bound
source
Nbody6Dynamics.imbh_companion_summary — Function
imbh_companion_summary(rows::AbstractVector{IMBHCompanion})
    -> NamedTuple{(:n_snapshots, :fraction_bound, :n_exchanges, :longest_name, :longest_span_myr)}

Summary of the companion series of imbh_companions: n_snapshots rows, fraction_bound the fraction of rows with a bound companion (NaN without rows), n_exchanges the changes of companion_name between consecutive bound rows (unbound rows in between skipped), and the longest uninterrupted run of one companion — consecutive rows with the same name, broken by an unbound row or another name — longest_name and longest_span_myr (last minus first time of the run; the earlier run on ties). Without a bound row longest_name = 0 and longest_span_myr = NaN.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.imbh_events — Function
imbh_events(name::Integer; collisions = CollisionRecord[], coalescences = CoalescenceRecord[],
            escapers = EscaperRecord[], gw_events = GWEvent[], tstar_myr::Real = NaN)
    -> Vector{NamedTuple{(:kind, :time_myr, :partner_name, :partner_kstar, :partner_mass_msun)}}

Events of the IMBH of body name name, from the engine's records:

kindsource
:tdea coll.13 record (CollisionRecord) naming the IMBH whose partner has K* ≤ 12 (a star or white dwarf)
:collisionother coll.13 records naming the IMBH
:coalescencea coal.24 record (CoalescenceRecord) naming the IMBH
:gw_mergera GWEvent of the merger kinds (:gw_ksint_coll, :gw_unpert_coll) with name1 or name2 the IMBH
:recoila :gw_recoil GWEvent naming the IMBH
:escapean esc.11 record (EscaperRecord) of the IMBH

Times [Myr]: time_nb × tstar_myr for the records timed in N-body units (NaN when tstar_myr is NaN), the record's own time_myr where it has one (esc.11, :gw_recoil). Nbody6Dynamics.IMBHPair — Type
IMBHPair

Two-body state of a pair of seeded IMBHs at one snapshot, from imbh_pairs; name1 < name2. The elements, the period and t_gw_myr are NaN for an unbound pair (ε ≥ 0).

fieldmeaningunit

Nbody6Dynamics.imbh_pairs — Function
imbh_pairs(snaps::AbstractVector{Snapshot}, names::AbstractVector{<:Integer})
    -> Vector{IMBHPair}

Two-body state of every unordered pair of distinct IMBH names (name1 < name2) at every snapshot that holds both, one IMBHPair each, in the physical units of imbh_companions: the specific energy ε = ½ |v₂ − v₁|² − G (M₁ + M₂)/|x₂ − x₁|, and for ε < 0 the elements a = −G (M₁ + M₂)/(2ε), e = √max(0, 1 − |r × v|²/(G (M₁ + M₂) a)), the Kepler period 2π √(a³/(G (M₁ + M₂))) converted from pc/(km s⁻¹) to Myr, and the Peters coalescence time. in_pair marks two IMBHs that are the components 2k − 1 and 2k (k ≤ npairs) of one KS pair.

The criterion is two-body: the stars about the pair are ignored, so a bound pair may be strongly perturbed. Repeated names count once. Rows are sorted by (name1, name2, time_myr); fewer than two distinct names give an empty vector.

Example

rows = imbh_pairs(read_all_conf3(dir), [5001, 5002])
imbh_pair_summary(rows).t_first_bound_myr
source
Nbody6Dynamics.imbh_pair_summary — Function
imbh_pair_summary(rows::AbstractVector{IMBHPair})
    -> NamedTuple{(:n_snapshots, :t_first_bound_myr, :t_last_bound_myr, :fraction_bound,
                   :a_min_au, :e_at_a_min)}

Summary of the rows of one IMBH pair from imbh_pairs: n_snapshots rows, the first and last time of a bound row (t_first_bound_myr, t_last_bound_myr), the fraction of bound rows fraction_bound, the smallest semi-major axis of a bound row a_min_au [au] and the eccentricity of that row e_at_a_min. Without a bound row the times, a_min_au and e_at_a_min are NaN; empty rows give n_snapshots = 0 and fraction_bound = 0. ArgumentError when the rows mix several pairs.

Example

rows = imbh_pairs(snaps, [5001, 5002])
s = imbh_pair_summary(rows)
s.fraction_bound, s.a_min_au
source
Nbody6Dynamics.imbh_pair_hardening — Function
imbh_pair_hardening(rows::AbstractVector{IMBHPair}; window_myr::Real = 0.0)
    -> Vector{NamedTuple}

Hardening rate of one IMBH pair from its rows of imbh_pairs, one row per time window over the bound rows (unbound rows and rows with a non-finite time are ignored): the whole span for window_myr ≤ 0, else consecutive windows [t₀ + k w, t₀ + (k + 1) w) of length w = window_myr from the first bound time t₀, the last window taking the remainder up to the last bound time, as in imbh_wandering. A window with fewer than three bound rows is left out. Rows (name1, name2, t_start_myr, t_end_myr, n, a_mean_au, s_au_per_myr, s_err_au_per_myr, k_de_dlninva, sigma_infl_kms, rho_infl_msun_pc3, h_dimensionless):

  • n the bound rows of the window and a_mean_au their mean a [au];
  • s_au_per_myr: the least-squares slope s = d(1/a)/dt of 1/a [au⁻¹] against t [Myr], the hardening rate of Quinlan (1996, DOI 10.1016/S1384-1076(96)00003-6) and Sesana & Khan (2015, DOI 10.1093/mnrasl/slv131), whose dimensionless form H = s σ/(G ρ) (hardening_rate_dimensionless) is ≈ 15–20 for a hard binary;
  • s_err_au_per_myr: its standard error √(residual variance / Σ(t − t̄)²), the residual variance with n − 2 degrees of freedom; NaN for fewer than four rows;
  • k_de_dlninva: the least-squares slope K = de/d ln(1/a) of e against ln(1/a), the eccentricity growth rate of Quinlan (1996); NaN when 1/a does not vary;
  • sigma_infl_kms, rho_infl_msun_pc3: the means over the window's rows of the dispersion and density inside the influence radius (IMBHPair), and h_dimensionless the rate H = s σ/(G ρ) at those means; NaN when the rows carry no influence quantities.

ArgumentError when the rows mix several pairs; an empty vector without three bound rows.

Example

rows = imbh_pairs(read_all_conf3(dir), [5001, 5002])
h = only(imbh_pair_hardening(rows))
h.h_dimensionless   # H at the influence radius
source
Nbody6Dynamics.hardening_rate_dimensionless — Function
hardening_rate_dimensionless(s_au_per_myr, sigma_kms, rho_msun_pc3) -> Float64

Dimensionless hardening rate H = s σ/(G ρ) of a massive binary (Quinlan 1996, DOI 10.1016/S1384-1076(96)00003-6; Sesana & Khan 2015, DOI 10.1093/mnrasl/slv131) from the rate s = d(1/a)/dt [au⁻¹ Myr⁻¹] of imbh_pair_hardening, the one-dimensional velocity dispersion σ [km s⁻¹] and the stellar density ρ [M☉ pc⁻³] about the binary. s is converted to pc⁻¹ Myr⁻¹ (× 206264.806 au pc⁻¹), σ to pc Myr⁻¹ (× 1.0227121650537077, one km s⁻¹ in pc Myr⁻¹), and G = 4.30091×10⁻³ × 1.0227121650537077² pc³ M☉⁻¹ Myr⁻². A hard binary in a fixed stellar background has H ≈ 15–20. ArgumentError unless σ and ρ are positive.

Example

hardening_rate_dimensionless(1.0e-3, 10.0, 1.0e4)   # ≈ 46.9
source

Survival Statistics

Nbody6Dynamics.EventTable — Type
EventTable

Exit times, entry times and causes of a sample of subjects, the input of the estimators of this file (kaplan_meier, nelson_aalen, aalen_johansen, logrank_test, cox_snell_residuals).

fieldmeaning
timeexit time: event or censoring
entryentry time (delayed entry, left truncation); 0 when none
cause0 for a censored exit, k ≥ 1 for an event of cause k
idlabel of the subject (the row index of its span in event_table)
cause_labelscause_labels[k] names cause k

A subject is at risk at t when entry < t ≤ time. The estimators require finite times with entry < time and causes in 0:length(cause_labels), and throw ArgumentError naming the first row that violates this.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.event_table — Function
event_table(spans::AbstractVector{BinarySpan}; time_axis::Symbol = :age, t_end::Real,
            unit::Symbol = :myr, causes = _SURVIVAL_DEFAULT_CAUSES) -> EventTable

The lifetimes of the binary spans of binary_spans as an EventTable, on one of two time axes.

Arguments

  • spans: the spans; row i of the table carries id = i (its index in spans)
  • time_axis: :age for the binary age τ = t − tform (entry 0), or :cluster for the cluster time t with delayed entry at tform
  • t_end: end of the record, the right-censoring time; it must be ≥ every span end
  • unit: :myr (fields t_form_myr, t_end_myr) or :nb (t_form_nb, t_end_nb)
  • causes: Dict{Symbol,Symbol} from the end_class of a span to a cause label; a label :censored gives cause 0. The default maps every end class to itself and :unresolved and :censored_run_end to :censored

Method

A right-censored span (open at the end of the record) exits at t_end with cause 0, whatever its end class; every other span exits at its end with the cause its end class maps to. On the age axis a left-censored span (formed before the first record, left_censored = true) has an unknown age and is left out; the count of spans dropped is logged at the Info level. On the cluster axis a left-censored span enters at 0, every other span at its formation time, so the risk set at t holds the binaries that exist at t (delayed entry; the product-limit estimate is then conditional on survival to entry). The cause_labels are the distinct labels other than :censored in order of first appearance among the rows kept.

The time axes and the treatment of escape (a censoring for the binary lifetime, a competing cause for the in-cluster lifetime: map :escape_bound to :censored in causes for the former) follow the recommended method for binary lifetimes in N-body clusters; the astronomical use of these estimators goes back to Feigelson & Nelson (1985, DOI 10.1086/163225).

Throws ArgumentError for an unknown time_axis or unit, a non-finite t_end, a span with a non-finite time, a span that ends after t_end, a span with t_end ≤ t_form (the span builders never produce one) and an end class missing from causes.

Example

spans = binary_spans(log; nzero, nbin0, tstar_myr)
age = event_table(spans; t_end = t_last_myr)
km = kaplan_meier(age)
in_cluster = aalen_johansen(event_table(spans; time_axis = :cluster, t_end = t_last_myr))
source
Nbody6Dynamics.SurvivalCurve — Type
SurvivalCurve

A product-limit survival curve from kaplan_meier or turnbull, evaluated at its jump times.

fieldmeaning
timedistinct event times t_(j) (Turnbull: right ends of the innermost intervals)
survivalŜ(t_(j))
varianceGreenwood variance of Ŝ(t_(j)); NaN when not estimated (Turnbull)
n_risknumber at risk nj at t(j)
n_eventsnumber of events dj at t(j)
n_censoredcensorings in (t(j−1), t(j)]; those after the last event time are in censor_times only
censor_timesevery censoring time, sorted (tick marks of a plot)

Evaluate with survival_at; pointwise intervals from confidence_interval.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.kaplan_meier — Function
kaplan_meier(table::EventTable) -> SurvivalCurve

Kaplan–Meier product-limit estimate of the survival function of the exit time for any cause, with the Greenwood variance, under right censoring and delayed entry.

Method

At the distinct event times t(1) < t(2) < … with nj subjects at risk (`entry < t(j) ≤ time`) and d_j events (cause ≥ 1),

Ŝ(t) = ∏_{t_(j) ≤ t} (1 − d_j/n_j),
Var Ŝ(t) = Ŝ(t)² Σ_{t_(j) ≤ t} d_j / [n_j (n_j − d_j)].

Tied events enter through dj; a censoring tied with an event time is at risk at that time. A term with nj = d_j (the last subject at risk fails) makes the sum infinite, and the variance is stored as Inf from there on. With delayed entry the estimate is conditional on survival to the earliest entry.

References: Kaplan & Meier (1958, DOI 10.1080/01621459.1958.10501452); Greenwood (1926, Rep. Public Health Med. Subj. 33); Feigelson & Nelson (1985, DOI 10.1086/163225) eqs. 8–9 for the astronomical use.

Example

km = kaplan_meier(event_table(spans; t_end = t_last_myr))
survival_at(km, 10.0)               # Ŝ(10 Myr)
lower, upper = confidence_interval(km)
source
Nbody6Dynamics.survival_at — Function
survival_at(curve::SurvivalCurve, t::Real) -> Float64

The step function Ŝ of curve at t: right-continuous, 1 before the first jump time and the last value after the last.

Example

km = kaplan_meier(table)
survival_at(km, 0.0)    # 1.0
source
Nbody6Dynamics.confidence_interval — Function
confidence_interval(curve::SurvivalCurve; level::Real = 0.95, transform::Symbol = :loglog)
    -> (lower::Vector{Float64}, upper::Vector{Float64})

Pointwise confidence limits of Ŝ(t_(j)) from the variance stored in curve, at confidence level.

Method

With z the (1 + level)/2 quantile of the standard normal distribution and σ² = Var Ŝ:

  • :loglog (default): the interval of θ = log(−log Ŝ), whose delta-method standard error is σ/(Ŝ |log Ŝ|), transformed back, exp(−exp(log(−log Ŝ) ± z σ/(Ŝ log Ŝ))); the limits lie in [0, 1] by construction. At Ŝ = 1 both limits are 1; at Ŝ = 0 they are NaN (undefined).
  • :plain: Ŝ ∓ zσ clamped to [0, 1].

A NaN variance (not estimated, turnbull) gives NaN limits. Reference: Feigelson & Nelson (1985, DOI 10.1086/163225) eqs. 11–12. ArgumentError for a level outside (0, 1) or an unknown transform.

Example

lower, upper = confidence_interval(kaplan_meier(table); level = 0.9)
source
Nbody6Dynamics.nelson_aalen — Function
nelson_aalen(table::EventTable)
    -> NamedTuple{(:time, :hazard, :variance)}

Nelson–Aalen estimate of the cumulative hazard of the exit time for any cause, under right censoring and delayed entry.

Method

At the distinct event times t(j) with nj at risk and d_j events,

Ĥ(t) = Σ_{t_(j) ≤ t} d_j/n_j,
Var Ĥ(t) = Σ_{t_(j) ≤ t} d_j (n_j − d_j)/n_j³,

the variance being the one that accounts for tied events. A cause-specific hazard follows from a table in which the other causes are recoded as censorings.

References: Nelson (1972, Technometrics 14, 945); Aalen (1978, DOI 10.1214/aos/1176344247).

Example

na = nelson_aalen(table)
na.hazard[end]      # Ĥ at the last event time

Nbody6Dynamics.aalen_johansen — Function
aalen_johansen(table::EventTable) -> CumulativeIncidence

Aalen–Johansen estimate of the cumulative incidence F_k(t) = P(T ≤ t, cause k) of every cause of the table, under right censoring and delayed entry.

Method

With nj at risk, dj events of any cause and dkj of cause k at the distinct event times t(j), and Ŝ(t(j)−) the all-cause Kaplan–Meier estimate just before t(j),

F̂_k(t) = Σ_{t_(j) ≤ t} Ŝ(t_(j)−) d_kj/n_j,

so that Σk F̂k = 1 − Ŝ. The variance is the delta-method estimate (Marubini & Valsecchi 1995, eq. 10.11; Aalen 1978)

Var F̂_k(t) = Σ_{t_(j) ≤ t} [F̂_k(t) − F̂_k(t_(j))]² d_j/[n_j (n_j − d_j)]
           + Σ_{t_(j) ≤ t} Ŝ(t_(j)−)² (n_j − d_kj) d_kj/n_j³
           − 2 Σ_{t_(j) ≤ t} [F̂_k(t) − F̂_k(t_(j))] Ŝ(t_(j)−) d_kj/n_j²,

accumulated in one pass over the event times through the recursions of the three sums in the increments F̂k(t(i)) − F̂k(t(i−1)) ≥ 0, which avoids the cancellation of the expanded squares. A term with nj = dj has a vanishing prefactor (F̂k is constant after it) and contributes 0. The complement 1 − Ŝk of the Kaplan–Meier estimate with the other causes censored overestimates F_k; it is not a cumulative incidence under competing causes.

References: Aalen & Johansen (1978, Scand. J. Stat. 5, 141); Aalen (1978, DOI 10.1214/aos/1176344247); Marubini & Valsecchi (1995, Analysing Survival Data from Clinical Trials and Observational Studies, Wiley).

Example

ci = aalen_johansen(event_table(spans; time_axis = :cluster, t_end = t_last_myr))
k = findfirst(==(:gw_merger), ci.cause_labels)
ci.incidence[end, k]        # fraction of binaries ended by a GW merger
source
Nbody6Dynamics.turnbull — Function
turnbull(lower::AbstractVector{<:Real}, upper::AbstractVector{<:Real};
         tol::Real = 1e-8, max_iter::Integer = 10_000) -> SurvivalCurve

Turnbull nonparametric maximum-likelihood estimate of the survival function from interval-censored exit times Ti ∈ (Li, U_i].

Arguments

  • lower, upper: Li and Ui; an exact time has Li = Ui (the interval (Ui − 0, Ui]), a right-censored one U_i = Inf
  • tol: the iteration stops when maxm |Δpm| < tol
  • max_iter: largest number of iterations; reaching it is logged as a warning

Method

The left ends Li and the right ends Ui are sorted together, a right end before a left end of the same value ((a, x] and (x, b] are disjoint) and the left end of an exact time before both. The innermost intervals (l, u] are the pairs of a left end immediately followed by a right end in that order; the NPMLE places its mass on them only. Every observation covers a contiguous block of innermost intervals. The masses p_m, uniform at the start, follow the self-consistency (EM) iteration

p_m ← (1/N) Σ_i α_im p_m / Σ_m' α_im' p_m',

αim = 1 when innermost interval m lies in (Li, Ui]. The curve is Ŝ(um) = 1 − Σ{m' ≤ m} pm' at the finite right ends um; the mass of an innermost interval with u = Inf (beyond the last finite end) lowers no point of it. For exact and right-censored data the estimate equals the Kaplan–Meier estimate. The variance is not estimated and is NaN; `nrisk,neventsandncensoredare 0, andcensortimes` holds the Li of the right-censored observations.

Reference: Turnbull (1976, DOI 10.1111/j.2517-6161.1976.tb01597.x).

Throws DimensionMismatch for vectors of different length and ArgumentError for a non-finite or NaN bound other than Ui = Inf, Li > Ui, tol ≤ 0 or `maxiter < 1`.

Example

# Ends known to the snapshot cadence: formation at the age 0, end in (t_j, t_{j+1}]
curve = turnbull(age_last_seen, age_first_missing)
survival_at(curve, 5.0)
source
Nbody6Dynamics.logrank_test — Function
logrank_test(table::EventTable, group::AbstractVector{<:Integer})
    -> NamedTuple{(:statistic, :p_value, :observed, :expected)}

Log-rank (Mantel–Haenszel) test of equal hazards of two samples, events of any cause, under right censoring and delayed entry.

Arguments

  • table: the pooled sample
  • group: group label of each row, 1 or 2; both must occur

Method

At every distinct event time t(j) of the pooled sample with nj at risk, n1j of them in group 1, and dj events, the hypergeometric expectation and variance of the events of group 1 are

E_1j = d_j n_1j/n_j,
V_j = d_j (n_1j/n_j)(1 − n_1j/n_j)(n_j − d_j)/(n_j − 1)   (0 for n_j = 1).

The statistic (O1 − E1)²/V with O1 = Σ d1j, E1 = Σ E1j and V = Σ Vj is χ² with one degree of freedom under the null hypothesis, so p = erfc(√(statistic/2)). observed and expected are the vectors [O1, O2] and [E1, E2]; E1 + E2 = O1 + O_2. Without variance (no events) the statistic and p are NaN.

References: Mantel (1966, Cancer Chemother. Rep. 50, 163); Peto & Peto (1972, J. R. Stat. Soc. A 135, 185); Feigelson & Nelson (1985, DOI 10.1086/163225).

Throws DimensionMismatch when group and the table differ in length and ArgumentError unless the labels are exactly {1, 2}.

Example

age = event_table(spans; t_end = t_last_myr)
group = [spans[i].origin === :primordial ? 1 : 2 for i in age.id]
logrank_test(age, group).p_value
source
Nbody6Dynamics.cox_snell_residuals — Function
cox_snell_residuals(table::EventTable, cumulative_hazard)
    -> NamedTuple{(:residual, :event)}

Cox–Snell residuals of a predicted cumulative hazard Λ.

Arguments

  • table: the observed sample
  • cumulative_hazard: a function t ↦ Λ(t), non-decreasing, returning a real

Method

ri = Λ(timei) − Λ(entryi), the integrated hazard over the time at risk of subject i, with the event indicator `causei ≥ 1`. If Λ is the true cumulative hazard, the ri are a right-censored sample of the unit exponential distribution, so the Nelson–Aalen estimate of an event table built from them (time ri, entry 0, the same indicator) lies on the identity line; its slope measures the ratio of the observed to the predicted hazard.

Reference: Cox & Snell (1968, J. R. Stat. Soc. B 30, 248).

Throws ArgumentError for a residual that is not finite or is negative (Λ decreasing).

Example

cs = cox_snell_residuals(age, τ -> τ / t_evap)
check = nelson_aalen(EventTable(; time = cs.residual, entry = zeros(length(cs.residual)),
    cause = Int.(cs.event), id = age.id, cause_labels = [:end]))
source

Nbody6Dynamics.collision_catalogue — Function
collision_catalogue(collisions::AbstractVector{CollisionRecord},
                    coalescences::AbstractVector{CoalescenceRecord},
                    cluster_ranges; tstar_myr, rbar_pc = NaN, nbin0 = 0,
                    mass_loss = nothing) -> Vector{CollisionRow}

Collision catalogue of a run: one CollisionRow per stellar collision of coll.13 and per coalescence of coal.24, each component tagged with its progenitor cluster, sorted by (time_nb, name1, name2).

Arguments

  • collisions: coll.13 records, from read_collision_log
  • coalescences: coal.24 records, from read_coalescence_log
  • cluster_ranges: the dat.10 names of each initial cluster, from parse_merger_summary; empty for a single-cluster run (every progenitor 0)
  • tstar_myr: time unit T* [Myr]
  • rbar_pc: length unit R̄ [pc], for the coal.24 separation; NaN leaves it NaN
  • nbin0: number of primordial pairs, the first 2nbin0 names of dat.10
  • mass_loss: the mass_loss records of read_binary_events, for kicked_before; nothing sets it false

Conventions

The product keeps the name of the component listed first in the row. For coll.13 this is the engine rule of mix.f and cmbody.f: the product takes the name of the heavier body (record masses, the record order on a tie), so the components are swapped when mass2_msun > mass1_msun. For coal.24 the record order is kept; coal.f gives the product the name of the progenitor with the larger initial mass (BODY0), which the record does not carry, so either name may continue. The absorbed body becomes a ghost and its name does not recur, so generation counts a coal.24 row for both of its names; it counts earlier rows in the sorted order. kicked_before uses the :binary_kick lines (MassLossEvent), which name both members of the kicked KS pair; a kick of the companion therefore flags the row as well. r_centre is in core radii for coll.13 and in N-body units for coal.24, as recorded.

Throws an ArgumentError for tstar_myr ≤ 0, nbin0 < 0 or a name that lies in two clusters of cluster_ranges.

Example

rows = collision_catalogue(read_collision_log(joinpath(dir, "coll.13")),
                           read_coalescence_log(joinpath(dir, "coal.24")),
                           parse_merger_summary(joinpath(dir, "merger_summary.txt"));
                           tstar_myr = 0.87, rbar_pc = 2.5, nbin0 = 500,
                           mass_loss = read_binary_events(log_path).mass_loss)
collision_summary(rows).n_mixed
source
Nbody6Dynamics.collision_summary — Function
collision_summary(rows::AbstractVector{CollisionRow}) -> NamedTuple

Summary of a collision catalogue from collision_catalogue:

  • n_collisions, n_coalescences: rows of coll.13 and of coal.24
  • n_mixed, n_primordial_pairs, n_kicked_before: rows with the flag set
  • m_lost_total_msun: sum of the finite m_lost_msun [M☉]
  • t_first_myr, t_last_myr: earliest and latest finite time_myr; NaN without one
  • by_progenitor_pair: Dict{Tuple{Int,Int},Int}, rows per unordered progenitor pair (min, max) (0 for an unknown progenitor)
  • by_pair_class: Dict{Symbol,Int}, rows per pair_class of the two component types
  • max_generation: highest generation; 0 for an empty catalogue

Example

s = collision_summary(rows)
s.by_progenitor_pair[(1, 2)]   # collisions between members of clusters 1 and 2

Gravitational-Wave Catalogues

Nbody6Dynamics.pair_class — Function
pair_class(kstar1::Integer, kstar2::Integer) -> Symbol

Class of a pair from the stellar types K* of its components, independent of their order: :bhbh, :bhns, :nsns, :bhwd, :nswd, :wdwd for two compact components (BH: K* = 14, NS: 13, WD: 10–12), :bh_star, :ns_star, :wd_star for a compact component with a star of K* ≤ 9, :star_star for two stars, and :unknown when either type is KSTAR_ABSENT, negative or ≥ 15.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.peters_semi_major_axis — Function
peters_semi_major_axis(a0::Real, e0::Real, e::Real) -> Float64

Semi-major axis [au] at eccentricity e on the Peters (1964, DOI 10.1103/PhysRev.136.B1224) track through the state (a0 [au], e0),

a(e) = a₀ g(e)/g(e₀),   g(e) = e^{12/19}(1 + 121e²/304)^{870/2299}/(1 − e²).

GW decay lowers e, so the track is defined for 0 ≤ e ≤ e₀; a circular state (e0 = 0) admits only e = 0 and returns a0, and e = 0 from an eccentric state returns 0 (coalescence). Throws an ArgumentError for a0 ≤ 0, e0 outside [0, 1), e < 0 or e > e0.

Example

peters_semi_major_axis(1.0, 0.9, 0.5)   # ≈ 0.163 au
source
Nbody6Dynamics.band_residence — Function
band_residence(m1, m2, a0, e0, f_lo, f_hi; definition = :f22)
    -> NamedTuple{(:t_in_yr, :t_out_yr, :above_lo, :above_hi)}

Times [yr] from the state (a0 [au], e0) of a binary of masses m1, m2 [M☉] at which its GW frequency of the given definition (see gw_frequency) enters the band [f_lo, f_hi] [Hz] and leaves it, under the Peters (1964) evolution.

The frequency rises monotonically along the track. The crossing of a frequency f is found with eccentricity_at_frequency, which returns the eccentricity ef there; with af = a(ef) from [`peterssemimajoraxis](@ref), the crossing time is T(a₀, e₀) − T(a_f, e_f) of [gwinspiraltime`](@ref). A circular state stays circular, all definitions reduce to f₂₂ and a_f = (GM/(πf)²)^{1/3}.

Returns

  • t_in_yr: time to f_lo, 0 when the state is already at or above it;
  • t_out_yr: time to f_hi, 0 when the state is already above it, the coalescence time T(a₀, e₀) when f_hi is not reached before coalescence;
  • above_lo, above_hi: the state is already at or above f_lo, f_hi.

The residence time in band is t_out_yr − t_in_yr. Throws an ArgumentError for an invalid orbit, an unknown definition, f_lo ≤ 0, f_hi ≤ 0 or f_lo ≥ f_hi.

Example

b = band_residence(30.0, 30.0, 0.1, 0.0, 1e-4, 1e-1)
b.t_out_yr - b.t_in_yr   # ≈ 4.14×10⁶ yr
source
Nbody6Dynamics.eccentricity_after — Function
eccentricity_after(m1, m2, a0, e0, Δt_yr) -> Tuple{Float64,Float64}

State (a, e) (a in au) of a binary of masses m1, m2 [M☉], recorded at (a0 [au], e0), after Δt_yr [yr] of pure GW decay along the Peters (1964) track; (NaN, NaN) when Δt_yr reaches the coalescence time T(a₀, e₀) of gw_inspiral_time.

The remaining time τ = T(a₀, e₀) − Δt equals T(a(e), e) for one e on the track, an increasing function of e: it is found by bisection on [0, e₀] to a relative tolerance of 10⁻¹⁰ in τ (at most 200 iterations), and a = peters_semi_major_axis(a₀, e₀, e). A circular state decays as a = a₀(τ/T(a₀, 0))^{1/4} with e = 0. Throws an ArgumentError for an invalid orbit or Δt_yr < 0.

Example

T = gw_inspiral_time(30.0, 30.0, 0.1, 0.9)
a, e = eccentricity_after(30.0, 30.0, 0.1, 0.9, 0.3T)   # gw_inspiral_time(30.0, 30.0, a, e) ≈ 0.7T
source

Nbody6Dynamics.compact_binary_catalogue — Function
compact_binary_catalogue(log::BinaryEventLog, spans::AbstractVector{BinarySpan};
                         tstar_myr, rbar_pc, binary_evo = BinaryEvolutionSnapshot[],
                         band_hz = (1e-4, 1e-1), f_ref_hz = (1e-2, 10.0),
                         t_hubble_myr = 13_800.0, decision_lines = false) -> Vector{CompactBinaryRow}

Compact-binary catalogue of a run: one CompactBinaryRow per record of a pair whose two components are white dwarfs, neutron stars or black holes (K* 10–14).

Arguments

  • log: event records of the engine log, from read_binary_events
  • spans: binary spans of the run, from binary_spans
  • tstar_myr, rbar_pc: time unit T* [Myr] and length unit R̄ [pc]
  • binary_evo: bev.82 snapshots (BinaryEvolutionSnapshot), optional
  • band_hz: GW band (lo, hi) of the residence times [Hz]
  • f_ref_hz: the two reference frequencies of f₂₂ [Hz]
  • t_hubble_myr: Hubble time [Myr] of merges_before_t_hubble

Sources

  • every GWEvent of an applied Peters step (:gw_decay_step, :gw_ksint, :gw_tides): the orbit before the step, its γ and TGR;
  • every KSEvent of kind :ks_new_compact (NS/BH BINARY);
  • every bev.82 record, with a = 10^{log₁₀(a/R☉)}/215.032 au.

Lengths convert as a [au] = a [NB]·R̄·206264.806 and times as t [Myr] = t [NB]·T*. Records with an absent type are dropped and counted, non-compact pairs skipped; one @info reports rows and dropped records per source. The covering span is the first BinarySpan of the same unordered pair with t_form_nb ≤ time_nb ≤ t_end_nb. Rows are sorted by (time_nb, name1, name2). Throws an ArgumentError for a non-positive tstar_myr or rbar_pc, or a band or reference pair without 0 < lo < hi.

Example

log = read_binary_events(joinpath(dir, "out1000"))
spans = binary_spans(log; nzero = 20_000, nbin0 = 2_000, tstar_myr = 0.87)
rows = compact_binary_catalogue(log, spans; tstar_myr = 0.87, rbar_pc = 2.5,
                                binary_evo = read_all_binary_evolution(dir))
write_compact_binary_catalogue(joinpath(dir, "compact_binary_catalogue.csv"), rows)
source

write_compact_binary_catalogue(path, rows::AbstractVector{CompactBinaryRow}) -> String

Write the compact-binary catalogue as CSV: a header of the field names of CompactBinaryRow in declaration order, one line per row. Floats carry 12 significant digits (NaN written as NaN), Bools true/false, Symbols their names. An existing file is backed up, never overwritten. Returns the path.

Nbody6Dynamics.escaped_binary_catalogue — Function
escaped_binary_catalogue(log::BinaryEventLog; tstar_myr, rbar_pc, vstar_kms,
                         escapers = EscaperRecord[], t_now_myr = NaN,
                         band_hz = (1e-4, 1e-1), f_ref_hz = (1e-2, 10.0),
                         t_hubble_myr = 13_800.0) -> Vector{EscapedBinaryRow}

Escaped-binary catalogue of a run: one EscapedBinaryRow per BinaryEscapeEvent whose two component types are compact (K* 10–14), with the GW evolution of the pair after it left the cluster.

Arguments

  • log: event records of the engine log, from read_binary_events
  • tstar_myr, rbar_pc, vstar_kms: time, length and velocity units T* [Myr], R̄ [pc], V* [km s⁻¹]
  • escapers: esc.11 records (EscaperRecord), for flag_kept_in_sim
  • t_now_myr: epoch of the present-epoch columns [Myr]; NaN leaves them NaN
  • band_hz, f_ref_hz, t_hubble_myr: as in compact_binary_catalogue

The escape mode looks for a :gw_recoil GWEvent of the same c.m. name or the same pair, then for a :binary_kick MassLossEvent naming a component, in [tesc − 1 NB, tesc]. Rows are sorted by time. Throws an ArgumentError for a non-positive unit or a band or reference pair without 0 < lo < hi.

rows = escaped_binary_catalogue(log; tstar_myr = 0.87, rbar_pc = 2.5, vstar_kms = 2.6,

Nbody6Dynamics.GWMergerRow — Type
GWMergerRow

One coalescence of two compact components (K* 10–14) in the GW-merger catalogue, from gw_merger_catalogue. Times in Myr unless the suffix says otherwise; NaN, 0 and :none mark unavailable values. Names, types and masses are in the order of the coalescence record. The trigger state is the orbit of the last GW decay record (GR KSTIDE, GR KSINT2, GR TIDES) of the pair at or before the merger.

fieldmeaningunit

The remnant of a merger takes the name of the progenitor with the larger initial mass (coal.f exchanges the names when BODY0(I1) < BODY0(I2)), which the coal.24 record does not carry; the absorbed body becomes a ghost and its name does not recur, so a later record that names either component of an earlier merger concerns its remnant, and generation counts both.

Nbody6Dynamics.gw_merger_catalogue — Function
gw_merger_catalogue(log::BinaryEventLog, coalescences::AbstractVector{CoalescenceRecord},
                    spans::AbstractVector{BinarySpan}; tstar_myr, rbar_pc, vstar_kms)
    -> Vector{GWMergerRow}

GW-merger catalogue of a run: one GWMergerRow per coal.24 record whose two component types are compact (K* 10–14), sorted by time.

Arguments

  • log: event records of the engine log, from read_binary_events
  • coalescences: coal.24 records, from read_coalescence_log
  • spans: binary spans of the run, from binary_spans
  • tstar_myr, rbar_pc, vstar_kms: time, length and velocity units T* [Myr], R̄ [pc], V* [km s⁻¹]

The trigger state is the latest GW decay GWEvent (:gw_decay_step, :gw_ksint, :gw_tides) of the same unordered pair with time_nb ≤ t_merge_nb; the recoil columns come from the :gw_recoil event of the pair nearest in time. The covering span is the first span of the pair with t_form_nb ≤ t_merge_nb ≤ t_end_nb + 0.01 NB, the tolerance of the F9.2 stamps. Generations count earlier rows in time order. Throws an ArgumentError for a non-positive unit.

Example

coal = read_coalescence_log(joinpath(dir, "coal.24"))
rows = gw_merger_catalogue(log, coal, spans; tstar_myr = 0.87, rbar_pc = 2.5, vstar_kms = 2.6)
write_gw_merger_catalogue(joinpath(dir, "gw_merger_catalogue.csv"), rows)

Nbody6Dynamics.write_ldc_galactic_binaries — Function
write_ldc_galactic_binaries(path, rows::AbstractVector{CompactBinaryRow};
                            distance_kpc, e_max = 0.01, t_obs_s = 4 × 365.25 × 86400,
                            cadence_s = 15.0, rng = nothing) -> String

Write the quasi-circular compact pairs of a compact-binary catalogue as a galactic-binary table of the LISA Data Challenge: the columns, their order and units follow the Sangria galactic-binary table (LDC manual, Tab. 3).

Arguments

  • rows: rows of compact_binary_catalogue
  • distance_kpc: distance of every source [kpc]; > 0
  • e_max: largest eccentricity of an exported pair; 0 < e_max < 1
  • t_obs_s, cadence_s: observation span and sampling interval [s] of the ObservationDuration and Cadence columns; > 0
  • rng: random number generator of the sky position and orientation angles, nothing for empty angle cells

Of every pair (unordered name1, name2) the last record in time_nb is taken; it is exported when its f₂₂ and both masses are finite and its eccentricity is e ≤ e_max. The LDC galactic-binary waveform is that of a circular orbit and cannot represent an eccentric source, hence the cut. The columns are EclipticLatitude [rad], EclipticLongitude [rad], Amplitude (amp_ldc22_kpc / distance_kpc, gw_amplitude_ldc), Frequency (f₂₂ [Hz]), FrequencyDerivative (the circular chirp (96/5)π^{8/3}(G𝓜/c³)^{5/3}f₂₂^{11/3} [Hz s⁻¹]), Inclination, Polarization, InitialPhase [rad], ObservationDuration and Cadence [s]. With an rng the angles are drawn isotropically per row in that order: latitude asin(2u − 1), longitude 2πu, inclination acos(2u − 1), polarisation πu and initial phase 2πu, u uniform on [0, 1). The distance is a catalogue-level assumption, not a position of the pair in the Galaxy.

A first # line records distance_kpc, e_max, the number of pairs and the number of exported rows; the header follows, then one row per exported pair ordered by (name1, name2). Floats are written in their shortest round-trip form, non-finite values as empty cells. An existing file is backed up, never overwritten. Returns the path. Throws an ArgumentError for a non-positive distance_kpc, t_obs_s or cadence_s, or e_max outside (0, 1).

Example

write_ldc_galactic_binaries("ldc_galactic_binaries.csv", rows; distance_kpc = 8.0,
                            rng = MersenneTwister(1))
source

Row type of gw_observer_catalogue.
