Nbody6Dynamics.gw_observer_catalogue — Function
gw_observer_catalogue(rows::AbstractVector{CompactBinaryRow}; distance_kpc::Real,
                      t_obs_yr::Real = 4.0, confusion::Bool = true,
                      rng::AbstractRNG) -> Vector{GWObserverRow}

The observer-dependent columns of the GW/LISA catalogue schema for the last record (in time_nb) of every compact pair of rows with a finite orbit: the pair is placed at the luminosity distance distance_kpc [kpc] (d_l_mpc) with the redshift z of the flat ΛCDM of Planck 2018 (H₀ = 67.66 km s⁻¹ Mpc⁻¹, Ωm = 0.3111, DOI 10.1051/0004-6361/201833910), an ecliptic latitude asin(2u − 1), longitude 2πu, inclination acos(2u − 1), polarisation πu and initial phase 2πu drawn from rng in that order (u uniform on [0, 1)), and observed for `tobsyryears with the [lisapsd](@ref) curve (confusionswitches the Galactic confusion noise;noisemodelis:robson2019or:robson2019noconf`).

f_gw22_obs_hz = f₂₂/(1 + z). snr_lisa is the sky- and polarisation-averaged SNR at the drawn inclination, ρ̄ [((1 + cos²ι)/2)² + cos²ι]^{1/2}/(4/5)^{1/2}, with ρ̄ the gw_snr (three time samples) of the detector-frame binary (masses and semi-major axis scaled by 1 + z, so that the frequencies are divided by it and the strain carries the redshifted chirp mass) at distance_kpc; the inclination factor is that of the quadrupole n = 2 mode, exact for circular orbits. snr_lisa is NaN for e > 0.99 or an observed f₂₂ outside 10⁻⁵–1 Hz. Rows are ordered by (name1, name2). Throws an ArgumentError for a non-positive distance_kpc or t_obs_yr, or an observation time without a confusion fit when confusion is set.

Example

obs = gw_observer_catalogue(cat.compact; distance_kpc = 8.0, rng = MersenneTwister(1))
source

Rotating King Model

Nbody6Dynamics.RotatingKingModel — Type
RotatingKingModel

Self-consistent rotating King model (Einsel & Spurzem 1999, DOI 10.1046/j.1365-8711.1999.02083.x) with distribution function f(E, J_z) = C (e^{−βE} − 1) e^{βΩ₀J_z} for E < 0, tabulated on a polar grid (r̂, μ = cos θ), as returned by solve_rotating_king. All quantities are dimensionless (G = σ = r_K = 1).

Fields

  • W0::Float64: central dimensionless potential, ψ(0) = W₀.
  • ω0::Float64: dimensionless angular velocity ω₀ = Ω₀ r_K/σ.
  • r::Vector{Float64}: uniform radial grid r̂ = r/r_K, n_r points from 0 to r̂_max.
  • μ::Vector{Float64}: Gauss–Legendre nodes in μ = cos θ on [−1, 1], ascending, n_μ points.
  • ψ::Matrix{Float64}: ψ = −β(Φ − Φ_t) at (r̂_i, μ_j), n_r × n_μ; zero on the truncation surface, negative outside it.
  • ρ::Matrix{Float64}: density ρ̂(ψ, ω₀R̂)/ρ̂(W₀, 0) at (r̂_i, μ_j), unity at the centre and zero outside the truncation surface.
  • l_max::Int: highest (even) Legendre order of the potential expansion.
  • r_t_equator::Float64: radius of the surface ψ = 0 in the equatorial plane (μ = 0).
  • r_t_pole::Float64: radius of the surface ψ = 0 on the rotation axis (μ = ±1).
  • mass::Float64: total mass M̂ = ∫ρ̂ dV̂ in units of ρ_c r_K³.
  • r_h::Float64: radius of the sphere enclosing M̂/2.
  • iterations::Int: fixed-point iterations performed.
  • residual::Float64: final max |ψ^{n+1} − ψ^n| of the undamped update.
source
Nbody6Dynamics.RotatingKingProfile — Type
RotatingKingProfile(; W0 = 6.0, omega0 = 0.6, axis = [0.0, 0.0, 1.0])

Rotating King model of Einsel & Spurzem (1999, DOI 10.1046/j.1365-8711.1999.02083.x): central potential W0, dimensionless angular velocity omega0 = Ω₀ r_K/σ ≥ 0 (0 is the King 1966 model), rotation axis axis (the angular momentum of the cluster points along it; [0, 0, 1] is prograde with respect to the Galactic rotation of KZ(14) = 1, 2, [0, 0, -1] retrograde). Solved and sampled by solve_rotating_king and sample_rotating_king.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.IMBHSpec — Type
IMBHSpec(; mass = NaN, mass_fraction = NaN, placement = "centre", offset = zeros(3),
           velocity = "rest", velocity_kms = zeros(3), response = "rigid", spin = 0.0)

Intermediate-mass black hole seeded in a cluster ([merger.clusterK.imbh]).

Fields

  • mass::Float64: IMBH mass M• [M☉]; exclusive with mass_fraction.
  • mass_fraction::Float64: M•/Mstars of the cluster's systems, `0 < massfraction ≤ 0.05; exclusive withmass`.
  • placement::String: "centre" (the stellar centre of mass) or "offset" (the centre of mass plus offset).
  • offset::Vector{Float64}: displacement from the stellar centre of mass [pc], cluster frame; used by "offset".
  • velocity::String: "rest" (the cluster's mean velocity, nudged by 10⁻⁶ σc in a random direction because the engine's start-up rejects a body at exact rest), "equipartition" (a Gaussian draw of per-component variance σc² ⟨m⟩/M• about it, Merritt 2005) or "explicit" (the mean velocity plus velocity_kms).
  • velocity_kms::Vector{Float64}: velocity relative to the cluster [km s⁻¹]; used by "explicit".
  • response::String: "rigid" (the stellar velocities unchanged) or "jeans" (rescaled in shells to the isotropic Jeans equilibrium with the added point mass).
  • spin::Float64: dimensionless spin χ, 0 ≤ χ ≤ 1; recorded only, neither route passes it to the engine.

One IMBH per cluster. The IMBHs are the last bodies of dat.10, in cluster order. The kz24 route (KZ(24) = 1) takes one IMBH per configuration and repeats its state on the engine's IMBHINIT line; the flag route (KZ(24) = 3, engine fork ≥ v2026.07.11, the package pin) takes 1 to 16 and passes their dat.10 names ([merger.nbody6] imbh_route).

source
Nbody6Dynamics.MBHSpec — Type
MBHSpec(; mass_msun, softening_pc = 0.0, rt_mode = "fixed", r_acc_pc = 0.0,
          partial = "off", beta_full = 1.0, response = "jeans")

Static massive black hole at the coordinate origin ([merger.mbh]; engine fork ≥ v2026.07.13, KZ(44) = 1 and the &INMBH namelist). The black hole is a fixed point mass acting on every body (irregular force, KS perturbations as a tidal difference); a single star whose orbit about it reaches the capture radius is disrupted at its pericentre passage, its energy booked and its mass removed (engine line TDE).

Fields

  • mass_msun: black-hole mass [M☉]; > 0
  • softening_pc: Plummer softening of its force [pc]; ≥ 0 (0: a point mass)
  • rt_mode: "fixed" (every star disrupted at r_acc_pc, the practice of Cho et al. 2026a) or "stellar" (r_t = R★ (M/m★)^{1/3} with the stellar-evolution radius; requires stellar.kz19 ≥ 3)
  • r_acc_pc: disruption radius of "fixed" [pc]; > 0 there
  • partial: "off" (full disruption when rt/q ≥ `betafull),"gamma43"or"gamma53"` (full disruption above β = 1.85 or 0.9 and partial disruption down to 0.6 or 0.5, the star losing the fraction of Guillochon & Ramirez-Ruiz 2013, DOI 10.1088/0004-637X/767/1/25)
  • beta_full: threshold of partial = "off"; > 0
  • response: "jeans" (the stellar velocities rescaled by the isotropic Jeans equation with the black hole's potential, as for a seeded IMBH) or "rigid" (velocities unchanged)

Requires orbit_mode = "single" (the cluster at the origin) and no external field (tidal.kz14 = 0).

source
Nbody6Dynamics.resolved_imbh_route — Function
resolved_imbh_route(cfg::MergerConfig) -> Symbol

Route by which the IMBHs of cfg reach the engine: :none (no cluster carries an IMBHSpec), :kz24 (KZ(24) = 1, one IMBH repeated on the IMBHINIT line) or :flag (KZ(24) = 3, engine fork ≥ v2026.07.11, the IMBH names after NIMBHS DTBH). imbh_route = "auto" resolves to :kz24 for one IMBH and to :flag for several. The configuration is assumed validated (load_merger_config).

Example

cfg = load_merger_config("merger.toml")
resolved_imbh_route(cfg) === :flag && @info "several IMBHs, KZ(24) = 3"

Nbody6Dynamics.solve_rotating_king — Function
solve_rotating_king(W0, ω0; l_max = 12, n_r = 600, n_μ = 48, r_max = 0.0,
                    relaxation = 0.4, tol = 1e-9, max_iter = 2000) -> RotatingKingModel

Self-consistent axisymmetric equilibrium of the rotating King distribution function f(E, J_z) = C (e^{−βE} − 1) e^{βΩ₀J_z} (E < 0) of Einsel & Spurzem (1999, DOI 10.1046/j.1365-8711.1999.02083.x), with central potential W0 = ψ(0) and dimensionless angular velocity ω0 = Ω₀ r_K/σ ≥ 0 (ω0 = 0 is the King 1966 model, DOI 10.1086/109930).

Method (Prendergast–Tomer iteration as in Varri & Bertin 2012, DOI 10.1051/0004-6361/201118300). In the units G = σ = r_K = 1, the potential ψ = −β(Φ − Φ_t) solves ∇̂²ψ = −9 ρ̂(ψ, R̂)/ρ̂(W₀, 0), where the local density ρ̂(ψ, k = ω₀R̂) is a 64-node Gauss–Legendre quadrature over the speed. Starting from the non-rotating King solution, each iteration evaluates the density on the grid (r̂_i, μ_j) (n_r uniform radii from 0 to r_max, n_μ Gauss–Legendre nodes in μ = cos θ), computes its potential by the Legendre Green's function for l = 0, 2, …, l_max (_rk_multipole_potential, product-trapezoid radial rules), takes the non-spherical part ψ_h^{n+1} = −[Φ̂(r̂, μ) − Φ̂₀(r̂)] (the potential less its angular average) under-relaxed, ψ_h ← (1 − α) ψ_h + α ψ_h^{n+1} with α = relaxation, and then recomputes the spherical part ψ₀(r̂) exactly by outward integration of the l = 0 equation with that ψ_h (_rk_spherical_part!; a Picard step on l = 0 diverges on the core's Jeans mode for W0 ≳ 4), until max |ψ^{n+1} − ψ^n| < tol for the whole ψ = ψ₀ + ψ_h. r_max = 0 selects 1.5 times the tidal radius of the non-rotating model.

Raises ArgumentError("solve_rotating_king: no equilibrium at ω0 = …") when the surface ψ = 0 does not close inside r_max after 50 iterations, when the density at r_max is non-zero at convergence, when the potential or the density becomes non-finite, or when max_iter is reached (critical rotation).

Returns a RotatingKingModel in model units (r_K = 1, σ = 1, ρ_c = 1); rotating_king_moments gives its global moments.

Example

m = solve_rotating_king(6.0, 0.6)
m.r_t_equator, m.r_t_pole       # equatorial and polar truncation radii [r_K]
rotating_king_moments(m).λ      # T_rot/T_kin; Einsel & Spurzem (1999) Table 1: 0.1981
source
Nbody6Dynamics.rotating_king_moments — Function
rotating_king_moments(m::RotatingKingModel)
    -> NamedTuple{(:λ, :t_rot, :e_dyn, :σ_c, :r_c, :r_t_over_r_c, :r_h_over_r_c, :r_t_over_r_K,
                   :r_h_over_r_K, :v_rot_rms_over_σ1d, :T_rot, :T_kin, :W)}

Global moments of the rotating King model m (Einsel & Spurzem 1999, DOI 10.1046/j.1365-8711.1999.02083.x), as volume integrals over the grid with dV̂ = 2π r̂² dr̂ dμ (sixth-order radial quadrature, Gauss weights in μ), in units of ρ_c r_K³ σ² for the energies:

  • T_kin = ½∫ρ̂⟨u²⟩ dV̂ (total kinetic energy, rotation included);
  • T_rot = ½∫ρ̂⟨v_φ⟩² dV̂ (ordered rotational energy);
  • W = ½∫ρ̂ Φ̂ dV̂ with the self-consistent multipole potential Φ̂ (zero at infinity);
  • λ = T_rot/T_kin; t_rot = T_rot/|W| (Ostriker & Peebles 1973, DOI 10.1086/152513);
  • e_dyn = 1 − r_t_pole/r_t_equator, the ellipticity of the surface ψ = 0;
  • σ_c, the central one-dimensional velocity dispersion in units of σ (⟨u²⟩/3 at ψ = W₀, k = 0), and r_c = r_K σ_c/σ, King's core radius formed with the central dispersion (the r_c of Einsel & Spurzem 1999 and of King 1966, Eq. 9), in units of r_K;
  • r_t_over_r_c = r_t_equator/r_c and r_h_over_r_c = r_h/r_c (the table convention of Einsel & Spurzem 1999), with r_t_over_r_K = r_t_equator and r_h_over_r_K = r_h in King radii;
  • v_rot_rms_over_σ1d = √(2T_rot/M̂) / √(2T_kin/(3M̂)).

For a converged model 2T_kin + W = 0 to the discretisation accuracy (scalar virial theorem).

Example

mom = rotating_king_moments(solve_rotating_king(6.0, 0.4))
mom.λ, mom.t_rot, mom.e_dyn
source
Nbody6Dynamics.sample_rotating_king — Function
sample_rotating_king(N, model::RotatingKingModel; rng = Random.default_rng()) -> (pos, vel)

Draw N particles from the rotating King model model (Einsel & Spurzem 1999, DOI 10.1046/j.1365-8711.1999.02083.x; see solve_rotating_king).

Positions: the radius by inverse CDF of the enclosed mass M(r̂) = ∫₀^r̂ 2π s² Σ_j w_j ρ̂(s, μ_j) ds (trapezoidal, linear interpolation on the grid); μ = cos θ from the conditional density at that radius (interpolated between the bracketing grid rows, piecewise linear in μ, exact inversion); azimuth uniform. A position at which the interpolated ψ (_rk_psi_at) is not positive, possible only in the grid cell that contains the truncation surface, is redrawn. Velocities: exact draws from the local distribution function at (ψ, k = ω₀R̂) by _rotating_king_velocity, rotated into Cartesian components with the position's azimuth.

Returns (pos, vel), each 3 × N, in model units (r_K = 1, σ = 1), centred on the model centre; the mean velocity is not subtracted. The model rotates about +z (L_z > 0, prograde).

Example

using Random
m = solve_rotating_king(6.0, 0.6)
pos, vel = sample_rotating_king(10_000, m; rng = Xoshiro(1))
source
sample_rotating_king(N, W0, ω0; rng = Random.default_rng(), kwargs...) -> (pos, vel)

Solve the rotating King model with central potential W0 and dimensionless angular velocity ω0 (solve_rotating_king, keyword arguments kwargs passed through) and draw N particles from it (sample_rotating_king(N, model)). Units as there (r_K = 1, σ = 1); reference Einsel & Spurzem (1999), DOI 10.1046/j.1365-8711.1999.02083.x.

Example

using Random
pos, vel = sample_rotating_king(10_000, 6.0, 0.6; rng = Xoshiro(1))
source
Nbody6Dynamics.rotation_moments — Function
rotation_moments(mass, pos, vel; axis = [0, 0, 1], n_bins = 20)
    -> NamedTuple{(:λ, :l_axis, :t_rot, :t_kin)}

Ordered rotation of a particle set about the unit vector â = axis/‖axis‖, measured about the centre of mass x̄ and in the frame of the mass-weighted mean velocity v̄; mass has length N, pos and vel are 3 × N.

  • l_axis = Σ m [(r − x̄) × (v − v̄)]·â / Σ m, the specific angular momentum along â [length × velocity of the input];
  • t_kin = ½ Σ m |v − v̄|², the kinetic energy;
  • t_rot = ½ Σ_b M_b ⟨v_φ⟩_b², the ordered rotational energy, with the particles sorted by the cylindrical radius R about â through x̄ and divided into n_bins bins of equal counts, M_b the bin mass and ⟨v_φ⟩_b its mass-weighted mean azimuthal velocity (positive in the sense of â); the sample estimator of T_rot = ½∫ρ⟨v_φ⟩² dV of rotating_king_moments, biased upwards by about n_bins/(3N) in λ;
  • λ = t_rot/t_kin.

Particles on the axis (R = 0) carry v_φ = 0. Throws a DimensionMismatch for inconsistent sizes and an ArgumentError for an empty set, n_bins ∉ [1, N], or an axis that is not a finite non-zero three-vector.

Example

m = solve_rotating_king(6.0, 0.6)
pos, vel = sample_rotating_king(20_000, m)
rotation_moments(ones(20_000), pos, vel).λ   # ≈ rotating_king_moments(m).λ
source

Nbody6Dynamics.BoundSpan — Type
BoundSpan

source
Nbody6Dynamics.bound_pairs — Function
bound_pairs(snap::Snapshot; k::Integer = 10, n_perturbers::Integer = 4,
            gamma_max::Real = 0.1) -> Vector{BoundPair}

The physically bound pairs of a snapshot, in its N-body units (G = 1, arrays converted to Float64), sorted by (name1, name2).

Arguments

  • snap: the snapshot; its first 2·npairs stars are the KS components
  • k: neighbour rank of the candidate search of snapshot_pairs
  • n_perturbers: nearest other bodies to a pair's centre of mass entering $γ_\mathrm{apo}$
  • gamma_max: largest accepted $γ_\mathrm{apo}$

Method

  1. Candidates are the bound pairs of snapshot_pairs, joined greedily in ascending specific orbital energy with every star used once: one level of node joining after Bate (2009a, DOI 10.1111/j.1365-2966.2008.14106.x), ordered by ε instead of separation.
  2. With $m_b = m_1 + m_2$ and $d_l$ the distances of the n_perturbers nearest other bodies from the pair's centre of mass, the apocentre perturbation is $γ_\mathrm{apo} = \sum_l 2 (m_l / m_b) [a(1 + e) / d_l]^3$. A candidate is accepted when $γ_\mathrm{apo} <$ gamma_max. Otherwise it is kept with hierarchy = true when the body $l^*$ of the largest term is bound to the pair's centre of mass, $ε_3 = \frac{1}{2}\lvert\mathbf{v}_l - \mathbf{v}_b\rvert^2 - (m_b + m_l)/d < 0$, and the triple satisfies the stability limit of Mardling & Aarseth (2001, DOI 10.1046/j.1365-8711.2001.03974.x, eq. 90) in the form $R_{p,\mathrm{out}} / a > 2.8 (1 + q)^{2/5} (1 + e_\mathrm{out})^{2/5} (1 - e_\mathrm{out})^{-1/5} (1 - 0.3\, i / 180°)$, with $q = m_l / m_b$, $e_\mathrm{out}$ and $R_{p,\mathrm{out}} = a_\mathrm{out}(1 - e_\mathrm{out})$ of the orbit of $l^*$ about the pair's centre of mass and $i$ [deg] the angle between the inner and outer orbital angular momenta (0 when one of them vanishes); it is rejected otherwise.
  3. Every KS pair of the snapshot with a positive centre-of-mass name (pair_name; every slot when the snapshot carries none) is a member whatever steps 1 and 2 give, with ks = true; such a pair not accepted in step 2 carries the elements of its relative state when bound (NaN when not), gamma_apo = NaN and hierarchy = false. A candidate that joins a component of such a pair to another star is dropped. A slot with a negative name is a merged hierarchy: star 2k − 1 is the first inner component, star 2k the outer body, the second inner component sits among the singles. Its stars are ordinary candidates, so the inner binary is found by step 1 and the outer body enters the triple test of step 2.

Throws ArgumentError for n_perturbers < 0 or gamma_max ≤ 0, and as snapshot_pairs for k < 1.

Example

pairs = bound_pairs(read_conf3(path))
count(p -> p.hierarchy, pairs)
source
Nbody6Dynamics.bound_spans — Function
bound_spans(snapshots::AbstractVector{Snapshot}; nbin0::Integer, k::Integer = 10,
            n_perturbers::Integer = 4, gamma_max::Real = 0.1,
            n_miss::Integer = 1, k_sep::Real = 20.0) -> Vector{BoundSpan}

The physically bound pairs (level P) of a snapshot sequence joined to BoundSpans, in N-body units, sorted by (t_form_hi, name1, name2).

Arguments

  • snapshots: the snapshots in increasing time
  • nbin0: initial number of primordial binaries NBIN0
  • k, n_perturbers, gamma_max: membership at a snapshot, see bound_pairs
  • n_miss: largest number of consecutive excused misses bridged within a span
  • k_sep: separation, in units of the last semi-major axis, beyond which an unbound pair counts as separated (end_unbound)

Method

The members of snapshot j are the pairs of bound_pairs, at time $t_j$ = time_nb of its header. For each pair of names, a span runs from a member snapshot over the following ones; a snapshot in which the pair is not a member is an excused miss when both components are present, their two-body energy ε is negative and neither is a component of another member pair. Up to n_miss consecutive excused misses are bridged when a member snapshot follows them; misses at the end of the sequence are not. Otherwise the span ends at its last member snapshot $j_2$, and the next membership of the pair opens a new span.

Formation and end are interval-censored: $t_\mathrm{form} ∈ (t_{j_1 - 1}, t_{j_1}]$, $t_\mathrm{end} ∈ [t_{j_2}, t_{j_2 + 1})$. A span is left-censored when $j_1$ is the first snapshot (t_form_lo = NaN) and right-censored when $j_2$ is the last (t_end_hi = NaN). end_unbound records that at $j_2 + 1$ both components are present with ε > 0 and a separation above k_sep · a_last, false for a right-censored span or a NaN a_last. A pair is primordial when its names are (2k − 1, 2k) with 2k ≤ 2·NBIN0.

Throws ArgumentError for nbin0 < 0, n_miss < 0, k_sep ≤ 0, an empty snapshots or snapshot times that do not increase strictly.

Example

spans = bound_spans(read_all_conf3(dir); nbin0 = 2_000)
write_bound_spans(joinpath(dir, "bound_spans.csv"), spans)
source
Nbody6Dynamics.write_bound_spans — Function
write_bound_spans(path, spans::AbstractVector{BoundSpan}) -> String

Write the spans of bound_spans as CSV, one column per field of BoundSpan in declaration order (N-body units, non-finite values as empty cells). An existing file is backed up, never overwritten. Returns the path.

source

Validity Record

Nbody6Dynamics.ValidityRecord — Type
ValidityRecord

The validity record of a run, from validity_record: schema_version (VALIDITY_SCHEMA_VERSION); sections, the tables header, thresholds, energy, mass, angular_momentum, bound_set, capacity, cadence, stages, calibration and masks; the epochs, sorted by t_start; and one ValiditySnapshot per snapshot integral.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.ValidityEpoch — Type
ValidityEpoch

A time interval [t_start, t_end] (N-body time) of a run in which a check of validity_record applies: kind is one of :violent_relaxation, :pericentre, :coalescence_phase (the windows that exclude snapshots from the equilibrium estimators), :energy_interval (an adjustment interval flagged by energy_intervals), :energy_threshold (an adjustment interval whose |DE| exceeds validity_interval_de), :mass_budget (a mass-budget residual above validity_mass_tolerance) or :bound_set (a bound set that did not converge); detail describes it.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.ValiditySnapshot — Type
ValiditySnapshot

The validity state of one snapshot (one entry of the snapshot integrals), from validity_record:

  • time_nb; n_bound bound stars and their effective number n_eff = (Σm)² / Σm²
  • t_cr: crossing time M^{5/2} / (−2E)^{3/2} of the bound set (G = 1; Heggie & Mathieu 1986), M its mass and E = T + W its energy
  • q_virial = 2T / |W| of the bound set
  • lagr_f_min = lagr_n_min / n_eff and lagr_f_max = 1 − lagr_n_min / n_eff, the mass-fraction range a Lagrangian radius can resolve; NaN when n_eff < 2 lagr_n_min
  • bound_converged: the bound-set iteration converged
  • in_window: the snapshot lies inside a :violent_relaxation, :pericentre or :coalescence_phase epoch
  • equilibrium_ok = !in_window && bound_converged
  • calibration: one CalibratedEstimate per entry of VALIDITY_TRACKED, in that order, at n_bound
source
Nbody6Dynamics.validity_record — Function
validity_record(results::AbstractDict{Symbol,Any}, settings::PostprocessConfig;
                calibration = load_calibration(), build_info = Dict{String,Any}(),
                run_info = Dict{String,Any}()) -> ValidityRecord

Validity record of a post-processed run from the dictionary of postprocess (any subset of :snapshots, :diagnostics, :integrals, :energy_events, :mass_budget, :energy_budget, :angular_momentum, :remnant, :progenitor_budget, :lagr; a missing entry leaves its checks unavailable or NaN) and the thresholds validity_* of settings.

The crossing time of a snapshot is the standard crossing time of Heggie & Mathieu (1986), t_cr = M^{5/2} / (−2E)^{3/2} in G = 1 units, of its bound set (crossing_time; 2√2 for M = 1, E = −1/4). Around a pericentre the time scale is the crossing time 2 r_h / σ of the denser progenitor, with σ² = 0.4 m / r_h from the relation r_h ≈ 0.4 G M / ⟨v²⟩ (Spitzer 1987).

The sections:

  • header: run id (run_info["run"]["id"]), creation time, package version, backend_commit and source_amendments of build_info, the calibration version and model ("plummer" for equal masses, "plummer_kroupa" otherwise), tidal mode, KZ(14), initial star count, snapshot count and time span
  • thresholds: the validity settings, keys without the validity_ prefix
  • energy: per-interval and cumulative energy errors of the ADJUST records, the flagged intervals, the error without the pair-energy channel, the energy-budget residuals and a verdict ("ok", "flag", "unavailable" or "not_applicable" when the engine's DE is not an error measure) with its reasons
  • mass, angular_momentum: budget residuals
  • bound_set: convergence of the bound-set iteration
  • capacity: the SIMD-library and merger-table headroom (_validity_capacity)
  • cadence: snapshot spacing against the crossing and the initial half-mass relaxation times
  • stages: end of violent relaxation, pericentres, coalescence, and the times of virialisation (2T/|W| within virial_tolerance from then on; the drift of ln r_10 per crossing time and its stationarity are reported beside it, since stellar mass loss and relaxation move the inner radii of a virialised cluster), kinematic relaxation (λR stationary) and mixing (|a₊₂| ≤ 2σ for every progenitor from then on), each "reached", `"notreached","undetermined"(or"not_applicable"` for mixing with fewer than two progenitors), also in initial half-mass relaxation times
  • calibration: bias and scatter of the VALIDITY_TRACKED estimators at the bound star count of the first snapshot
  • masks: the masking rules as applied (M6 after Küpper et al. 2010, DOI 10.1111/j.1365-2966.2009.16173.x)

The window epochs (:violent_relaxation, :pericentre, :coalescence_phase) exclude snapshots from the equilibrium estimators; the other epochs mark energy, mass and bound-set problems.

Arguments

  • results: the dictionary returned by postprocess
  • settings: the [postprocess] configuration
  • calibration: estimator calibration; nothing leaves the estimates NaN
  • build_info: the engine build record (BUILD_INFO.toml)
  • run_info: the run record (RUN_INFO.toml)

Returns

A ValidityRecord. Throws a DimensionMismatch when snapshots and integrals are both given but differ in number.

Example

results = postprocess(cfg; run_dir)
rec = validity_record(results, cfg.postprocess)
write_validity(joinpath(run_dir, "validity.toml"), rec)
source
Nbody6Dynamics.write_validity — Function
write_validity(path, rec::ValidityRecord) -> String

Write the record as TOML: the top-level schema_version, one table per section in the order header, thresholds, energy, mass, angular_momentum, bound_set, capacity, cadence, stages, calibration (with a nested [calibration.<estimator>__<quantity>] table per tracked estimator) and masks, keys sorted within a table, then the epochs as [[epochs]] with t_start, t_end, kind and detail. NaN is written as the TOML float nan. An existing file is backed up, never overwritten. Returns the path.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.write_validity_snapshots — Function
write_validity_snapshots(path, rec::ValidityRecord) -> String

Write the per-snapshot rows of the record as CSV: time_nb, n_bound, n_eff, t_cr, q_virial, lagr_f_min, lagr_f_max, bound_converged, in_window, equilibrium_ok, then for every entry <estimator>__<quantity> of VALIDITY_TRACKED the columns _bias, _scatter and _ok. Booleans as true/false, numbers with string (NaN as NaN). An existing file is backed up, never overwritten. Returns the path.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.CalibrationRow — Type
CalibrationRow

One row of the estimator calibration table estimators.csv: the static model ("plummer", "plummer_kroupa", …), the estimator and the quantity it reports, the particle number n, the known answer truth (NaN without one), the replica mean and scatter (standard deviation over the replicas), bias = mean − truth with its error bias_err, and the mean reported_error of the estimator's own error estimate (NaN when it gives none).

source
Nbody6Dynamics.CalibrationTable — Type
CalibrationTable

The estimator calibration read by load_calibration: its version (the version key of estimators.toml), the directory dir it was read from and its rows, one CalibrationRow per line of estimators.csv.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.CalibratedEstimate — Type
CalibratedEstimate

Calibrated bias and scatter of one estimator at a particle number, from calibrated:

  • bias, scatter: relative to |truth| when relative, otherwise absolute (a noise floor of a quantity whose true value is zero or unknown)
  • error_ratio: reported_error / scatter; NaN when either is NaN or the scatter is zero
  • relative: the interpolated truth is finite and nonzero
  • in_range: the particle number lies inside the calibrated grid
  • ok: isfinite(bias) && abs(bias) ≤ tolerance when relative, true otherwise
source
Nbody6Dynamics.load_calibration — Function
load_calibration(dir = _CALIBRATION_DIR) -> CalibrationTable

Read the estimator calibration of dir: the integer version of estimators.toml and every data line of estimators.csv (columns model,estimator,quantity,N,n_rep,truth,mean,scatter,bias,bias_err,reported_error,predicted_scatter; NaN literals mark unavailable values; n_rep and predicted_scatter are not kept). The default is the calibration shipped with the package.

Throws an ArgumentError when either file is missing, when version is not an integer, or for a line whose field count is not 12 (naming the line).

Example

cal = load_calibration()
calibrated(cal, "plummer", "rotation", "lambda_r", 20_000)
source
Nbody6Dynamics.calibrated — Function
calibrated(cal::CalibrationTable, model, estimator, quantity, n; tolerance = 0.05)
    -> CalibratedEstimate

Bias and scatter of an estimator at particle number n, from the calibration rows of model, estimator and quantity. With x = log10(n) and the grid xᵢ = log10(Nᵢ) of the rows, bias, scatter, reported_error and truth are interpolated linearly between the bracketing grid points; outside the grid the end row is taken and in_range is false. When the interpolated truth is finite and nonzero the bias and the scatter are divided by |truth| (relative), otherwise they stay absolute. A NaN entry of a row used by the interpolation propagates. ok holds when a relative bias is finite and at most tolerance in modulus, and always for an absolute one.

Arguments

  • cal: table of load_calibration
  • model, estimator, quantity: the triple of the rows
  • n: particle number, finite and > 0
  • tolerance: largest admissible |relative bias|, ≥ 0

Returns

A CalibratedEstimate. Throws an ArgumentError when the table has no row for the triple, or for an invalid n or tolerance.

Example

c = calibrated(load_calibration(), "plummer", "core_radius", "r_core_number", 50_000)
c.ok
source

Binary Population

Nbody6Dynamics.binary_population — Function
binary_population(bevs::Vector{BinaryEvolutionSnapshot};
                  n_stars = nothing, m_mean = nothing, sigma_kms = nothing)
    -> BinaryPopulation

Reduce a time-ordered sequence of bev.82 snapshots to pair counts, the binary fraction and the hard/soft split. n_stars (total number of stars, scalar or one value per epoch) enables the binary fraction $N_\mathrm{b} / (N_\star - N_\mathrm{b})$; m_mean [M☉] and sigma_kms [km s⁻¹] (scalars or per-epoch vectors, e.g. from hardness_scale) enable the classification.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.binary_hardness — Function
binary_hardness(bev::BinaryEvolutionSnapshot, m_mean, sigma_kms) -> Vector{Float64}

Hardness ratio $x = E_b / (⟨m⟩ σ²)$ of every pair in bev for a mean system mass m_mean [M☉] and one-dimensional dispersion sigma_kms [km s⁻¹]; a pair is hard when x > 1 (Heggie 1975).

source
Nbody6Dynamics.hardness_scale — Function
hardness_scale(snap::Snapshot, bev::BinaryEvolutionSnapshot; bound_only = true)
    -> (; m_mean, sigma_kms, n_systems)

Mean system mass ⟨m⟩ [M☉] and one-dimensional, mass-weighted velocity dispersion σ [km s⁻¹] of the systems in snap: single stars plus the centres of mass of the pairs listed in bev, matched to the snapshot by particle name. A pair with a component missing from the snapshot is not reduced and its present component counts as a single star. With bound_only the scale is taken over the self-consistently bound systems (bound_set), which keeps escapers and kicked stellar remnants — a handful of stars at tens of km s⁻¹ — from dominating the mass-weighted dispersion; n_systems counts the systems used. ⟨m⟩ σ² is the hard/soft energy scale used by binary_hardness.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.binary_scales — Function
binary_scales(bevs, snaps::Vector{Snapshot};
              pair_sum_max_n = PostprocessConfig().pair_sum_max_n)
    -> (; m_mean, sigma_kms, n_stars)

Per-epoch hard/soft energy scales and stellar counts for bevs, each taken from the snapshot in snaps closest in N-body time to the stamp of the epoch's records (the F9.1 Myr header is too coarse to tell neighbouring snapshots apart); an epoch without records is matched by its header time against the snapshots' times in Myr. The scale of a snapshot of at most pair_sum_max_n particles is taken over its bound systems (hardness_scale with bound_only), an O(N²) selection; above that limit it is taken over every system. Returns nothing when snaps is empty.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.binding_energy — Function
binding_energy(r::BinaryRecord) -> Float64

Binding energy $G m_1 m_2 / (2a)$ of a pair in M☉ (km s⁻¹)².

source
Nbody6Dynamics.semi_major_axis_pc — Function
semi_major_axis_pc(r::BinaryRecord) -> Float64

Semi-major axis in pc from the record's log10(a / R☉).

source
Nbody6Dynamics.plot_binary_population — Function
plot_binary_population(pop::BinaryPopulation, cfg::VisualizationConfig;
                       filename = "binary_population") -> String

Two stacked panels sharing the time axis: regularised pair counts (total and, when classified, hard and soft) above the binary fraction $f_\mathrm{b} = N_\mathrm{b} / (N_\mathrm{s} + N_\mathrm{b})$ and the hard fraction $N_\mathrm{hard} / N_\mathrm{pairs}$ in per cent. The net change of the pair count is annotated in the upper panel.

Nbody6Dynamics.plot_binary_orbital_elements — Function
plot_binary_orbital_elements(bev::BinaryEvolutionSnapshot, cfg::VisualizationConfig;
                             m_mean = NaN, sigma_kms = NaN,
                             filename = "binary_orbital_elements") -> String

Semi-major axis [au] against eccentricity of every regularised pair at one epoch. With a hard/soft energy scale (m_mean [M☉], sigma_kms [km s⁻¹]) the pairs are classed by the Heggie criterion, the class fractions are annotated, and the boundary semi-major axis of a pair of the mean component-mass product is marked.

Nbody6Dynamics.plot_binary_timeline — Function
plot_binary_timeline(spans::AbstractVector{BinarySpan}, cfg::VisualizationConfig;
                     filename = "binary_timeline", max_bars = 60,
                     select = :compact, t_end_myr = NaN) -> String

Swimmer plot of the engine-regularised binaries: one horizontal bar per BinarySpan from its formation to its end, rows in order of formation time, the earliest at the top, labelled by the pair names name1–name2 (the labels are dropped above 30 bars, the ticks kept). The bar colour encodes the origin (primordial blue, re-formed primordial sky blue, exchange orange, dynamical bluish green), the marker at the end of a bar the end class: a star for :gw_merger; a diamond for :stellar_merger, :collision, :disruption and :sn_disruption; a triangle for :exchange; a square for :escape_bound; none for :flyby; a circle for :unresolved and any other class. A right-pointing arrow head ends a right-censored span, an open circle starts a left-censored one.

Arguments

  • spans: the spans of binary_spans
  • max_bars: most bars drawn; beyond it the max_bars longest-lived spans are kept and their number stated in the panel
  • select: :compact (both components white dwarfs, neutron stars or black holes at formation), :primordial (primordial pairs, re-formed spans included) or :all
  • t_end_myr: right end of the time axis [Myr]; NaN takes the latest span end

The canvas grows by 12 layout units per bar beyond the single-panel height. Throws an ArgumentError for an unknown select or max_bars < 1, and an error when no span is selected.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.plot_binary_population_series — Function
plot_binary_population_series(rows::AbstractVector{BinaryPopulationRow},
                              cfg::VisualizationConfig;
                              filename = "binary_population_series",
                              level = :bound, class = :all, bins = (0, 1, 2)) -> String

Two stacked panels sharing the time axis: the number fraction of binaries $f_\mathrm{b} = N_\mathrm{b} / (N_\mathrm{s} + N_\mathrm{b})$ in per cent with its binomial band $f_\mathrm{b} \pm \sigma_{f_\mathrm{b}}$ above, the mass fraction $f_M = M_\mathrm{b} / (M_\mathrm{s} + M_\mathrm{b})$ below. One series per radial shell of bins (bin 0 holds every system, the others are labelled by their edges in the frame of the rows), coloured in Okabe–Ito order, with markers at the snapshots when there are fewer than 40. The census level (E for :ks, P for :bound) and the stellar class are stated in the upper panel.

Arguments

  • rows: the rows of binary_population_series
  • level: census level, :ks or :bound
  • class: :all or the key of a STELLAR_CLASSES entry
  • bins: the shells drawn, in legend order

Throws an error when no row matches level, class and bins.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.plot_survival_curves — Function
plot_survival_curves(curves::AbstractVector{<:NamedTuple}, cfg::VisualizationConfig;
                     filename = "binary_survival",
                     xlabel = L"\tau\;[\mathrm{Myr}]") -> String

Kaplan–Meier survival curves of binary lifetimes. Main panel: one step curve per element of curves, starting at (0, 1), with its 95 % confidence band as a stepped low-opacity band and its censorings as short vertical ticks on the curve. Strip below, sharing the time axis: the number at risk of every curve at the tick positions, one row per curve in its colour. Colours follow the Okabe–Ito order; above four curves the line style alternates between solid and dashed.

Arguments

  • curves: NamedTuples (label, time, survival, lower, upper, n_risk, censor_times); time sorted, with survival, the band limits lower, upper and the number at risk n_risk (just before each time) aligned to it, and censor_times the censored durations
  • xlabel: label of the duration axis

Throws an error for an empty curves, an ArgumentError for a curve without one of the fields or with unsorted times, and a DimensionMismatch for misaligned vectors.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.plot_gw_planes — Function
plot_gw_planes(rows::AbstractVector{CompactBinaryRow}, cfg::VisualizationConfig;
               filename = "gw_planes", last_only = true,
               band_hz = (1e-4, 1e-1)) -> String

The compact pairs in two planes side by side: semi-major axis against $1 - e$ (both logarithmic) on the left, GW frequency $f_{22}$ against eccentricity on the right. One marker per pair at its last record (by time_nb); with last_only = false every record is drawn faint and the last one bold. Colour encodes the pair class (pair_class: BH–BH vermillion, BH–NS reddish purple, NS–NS blue, BH–WD bluish green, NS–WD sky blue, WD–WD yellow, others grey), marker shape the channel (primordial circle, dynamical triangle, ejected square, other diamond).

The left panel carries the curves of constant Peters (1964, DOI 10.1103/PhysRev.136.B1224) coalescence time (gw_inspiral_time) for the median component masses of the pairs, at 10, 100 and 1000 Myr and 13.8 Gyr, labelled along the lines. The right panel shades the space-borne band band_hz (LISA) and, when inside the axis range, the ground-based band $f \ge 10$ Hz (LVK). The frequency axis spans the data with a 0.15-dex margin and is extended to the band edges when the data lie within two decades of them.

Arguments

  • rows: the rows of compact_binary_catalogue; records without a bound orbit or a GW frequency are left out
  • last_only: draw only the last record of every pair
  • band_hz: (lo, hi) of the space-borne band [Hz]

Throws an error for an empty rows or when no record has a bound orbit, and an ArgumentError for a band without 0 < lo < hi.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.plot_imbh_wandering — Function
plot_imbh_wandering(traj::AbstractVector{IMBHState}, wandering::AbstractVector{<:NamedTuple},
                    cfg::VisualizationConfig; prediction_plummer_pc = NaN,
                    prediction_core_pc = NaN, filename = "imbh_wandering") -> String

Brownian wandering of the IMBH in two stacked panels sharing the time axis. Upper panel, logarithmic: the distance of the IMBH from the stellar centre of mass (r_cm_pc, blue) and, thinner, from the stellar density centre (r_pc, orange), over the core radius $r_\mathrm{c}$ (grey, dashed) and the half-mass radius $r_\mathrm{h}$ (grey, dotted) of the stars, each labelled at its end; non-positive values are left out. Lower panel, logarithmic: the rms offset per coordinate $x_\mathrm{rms} = \sqrt{\langle x^2 \rangle}$ of every window of wandering as a marker at the window centre with a horizontal bar over the window, in the centre-of-mass frame when the rows hold it (the frame of Chatterjee, Hernquist & Loeb 2002, DOI 10.1086/339174), in the density-centre frame otherwise, coloured as the distance from that centre above. The equilibrium predictions are dashed horizontal guide lines labelled at their right end, and the rms offset pooled over all windows (weighted by the states per window) is stated in the panel.

Arguments

  • traj: the states of imbh_trajectory
  • wandering: the rows of imbh_wandering, of either frame or both
  • prediction_plummer_pc: brownian_rms_plummer [pc]; drawn when finite and positive
  • prediction_core_pc: brownian_rms_core (Lin & Tremaine 1980, DOI 10.1086/157803) [pc]; drawn when finite and positive

An empty wandering leaves the lower panel with the predictions and the note No window. Throws an error when traj holds no state with a finite time.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.plot_imbh_companions — Function
plot_imbh_companions(rows::AbstractVector{IMBHCompanion}, cfg::VisualizationConfig;
                     max_named = 6, filename = "imbh_companions") -> String

The most bound two-body companion of the IMBH per snapshot in two stacked panels sharing the time axis: the semi-major axis a [au] above (logarithmic), the eccentricity e below. One marker per snapshot with a bound companion (companion_name > 0); the colour encodes the companion's identity — the max_named most frequent companions in Okabe–Ito order of frequency, labelled Body <name>, every other one grey as Other — and the fill its regularisation: filled for the KS partner of the IMBH (in_pair), open-faced otherwise. A black star on top marks an IMRI candidate, a pair whose Peters coalescence time t_gw_myr is shorter than the time span of the rows. The upper panel states the bound fraction, the number of snapshots and the exchanges of imbh_companion_summary.

Arguments

  • rows: the rows of imbh_companions
  • max_named: most companions given their own colour and legend entry

Without a bound row the axes are drawn empty with the note No bound companion. Throws an error for an empty rows and an ArgumentError for max_named < 0.

source

Stellar Population

Stellar classes, the population of an epoch on the HR plane (single stars and members of KS-regularised pairs together), and the class census of a run. run_pipeline writes the census as stellar_census.csv.

Nbody6Dynamics.StellarClass — Type
StellarClass

A group of SSE/BSE stellar types K* (Hurley, Pols & Tout 2000, doi:10.1046/j.1365-8711.2000.03426.x) treated as one population: key identifies it, label is its legend entry, kstar the range of types it holds. luminous states whether its members have a photosphere that places them on the optical HR plane; neutron stars, black holes and massless remnants do not (the engine assigns them cooling-curve or placeholder luminosities), so they are counted and never drawn.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.STELLAR_CLASSES — Constant
STELLAR_CLASSES

The stellar classes in evolutionary order. The grouping is the engine's own ("main stellar types" of global_output.F, used for its per-type Lagrangian radii): AGB = K* 5–6, helium stars = 7–9, white dwarfs = 10–12, with the two main-sequence types (K* 0 and 1, split at 0.7 M☉) taken together.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.stellar_class — Function
stellar_class(kstar) -> StellarClass

The StellarClass of stellar type kstar.

stellar_class(6).label      # "AGB"
stellar_class(14).luminous  # false
source
Nbody6Dynamics.stellar_class_index — Function
stellar_class_index(kstar) -> Int

Index into STELLAR_CLASSES of the class holding stellar type kstar. Throws an ArgumentError outside the SSE/BSE range −1…15.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.HRPopulation — Type
HRPopulation

The stars of one epoch that lie on the HR plane, as columns: log_teff (log₁₀ Teff/K), `logluminosity(log₁₀ L/L☉),class(index into [STELLARCLASSES](@ref)) andbinarymember(the star is a member of a KS-regularised pair).time_myr` is the epoch.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.hr_population — Function
hr_population(sev, bev = nothing) -> HRPopulation

The HR-plane population of one epoch: the single stars of sev together with both members of every pair of bev, the latter flagged as binary members. Stars of a non-luminous class (StellarClass) and records carrying placeholder values (log L ≤ −5 or log T_eff ≤ 3) are left out.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.hr_populations — Function
hr_populations(sevs, bevs = BinaryEvolutionSnapshot[]) -> Vector{HRPopulation}

hr_population of every epoch of sevs, each paired with the binary snapshot of the same epoch when bevs holds one.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.StellarCensus — Type
StellarCensus

Number of stars of every class at every stellar-evolution epoch of a run. single[i, k] counts the single stars of class k (STELLAR_CLASSES) at time_myr[i], binary_member[i, k] the members of KS-regularised pairs. Non-luminous classes are included: this is where neutron stars and black holes are accounted for.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.stellar_census — Function
stellar_census(sevs, bevs = BinaryEvolutionSnapshot[]) -> StellarCensus

Class census of a run from its stellar-evolution snapshots and, when given, the binary snapshots of the same epochs.

census = stellar_census(results[:stellar_evo], results[:binary_evo])
class_counts(census)[end, stellar_class_index(14)]   # black holes at the last epoch
source
Nbody6Dynamics.class_counts — Function
class_counts(census) -> Matrix{Int}

Stars per epoch (rows) and class (columns), single stars and binary members together.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.classes_present — Function
classes_present(census) -> Vector{Int}

Indices of the classes with at least one star at any epoch, in the order of STELLAR_CLASSES. Figures of a run take their legend from this set, so every figure of the run lists the same classes whatever its epoch shows.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.write_stellar_census — Function
write_stellar_census(path, census) -> String

Write census as CSV: time_myr, then <class>_single and <class>_binary for every class. An existing file is backed up, never overwritten. Returns path.

Plotting

Nbody6Dynamics.plotting_available — Function
plotting_available() -> Bool

Whether the Makie extension is loaded, i.e. whether the figure routines have methods. false on a headless installation that never loaded a backend.

Example

plotting_available() || @info "Running without figures"
source
Nbody6Dynamics.PlottingUnavailable — Type
PlottingUnavailable(entry_point, hint = "")

No Makie backend is loaded, so the figure routine entry_point has no methods. Thrown by every public plotting entry point of the package and by the pipeline phases that would produce figures; hint carries the context-specific remedy.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.set_publication_theme! — Function
set_publication_theme!()

Activate publication_theme globally, for figures composed by hand from Makie calls. The figure routines of the package do not depend on it.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.publication_theme — Function
publication_theme() -> Theme

The publication theme: Computer Modern fonts from MathTeXEngine, 26-unit labels and legends over 22-unit tick labels, boxed axes with inward ticks and no minor ticks, a faint dashed grid, frameless horizontal legends, 3-unit data lines and 14-unit markers with a 1.5-unit edge. Built on every call so that the font faces are live ones, not faces captured at precompile time. Every figure routine of the package draws inside with_theme(publication_theme()), so the session's own theme is neither needed nor changed; Nbody6Dynamics.set_publication_theme! activates it globally for figures composed by hand.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.plot_snapshot — Function
plot_snapshot(snap::Snapshot, cfg::VisualizationConfig;
              filename::AbstractString = "snapshot",
              projections::Vector{Symbol} = [:xy, :xz])

Generate publication-quality plots of particle positions in the requested projections (:xy, :xz, :yz): a scatter coloured by mass, or the projected surface mass density when cfg.style.snapshot_render selects it for this particle count (see PlotStyle). Positions are shown in pc, the density in M☉ pc⁻² and the time annotation in Myr when cfg.units == "physical" (header AS scaling); N-body units otherwise.

Nbody6Dynamics.plot_snapshot_evolution — Function
plot_snapshot_evolution(snaps::Vector{Snapshot}, cfg::VisualizationConfig;
                       filename::AbstractString = "snapshot_evolution",
                       projections::Vector{Symbol} = [:xy, :xz, :yz],
                       max_panels::Int = 6)

Multi-panel figure showing cluster evolution across snapshots, as mass-coloured scatter panels or as projected surface mass density on one colour scale (cfg.style.snapshot_render, decided once for the figure by its largest snapshot). Positions are shown in pc and time annotations in Myr when cfg.units == "physical".

When the spatial extent varies dramatically across panels (e.g. merger runs), each panel is zoomed on its own data and carries its own tick values. Under global limits (the extent is comparable throughout) only the border panels are labelled. The extent of a density panel is that of the mass fraction cfg.style.density_mass_frac, so escapers do not set the scale.

plot_particle_count(diag::DiagnosticsData, cfg::VisualizationConfig;

Nbody6Dynamics.plot_hr — Function
plot_hr(sevs::Vector{StellarEvolutionSnapshot}, cfg::VisualizationConfig;
        epoch = length(sevs), bevs = BinaryEvolutionSnapshot[],
        filename = "hr_diagram") -> String
plot_hr(sev::StellarEvolutionSnapshot, cfg::VisualizationConfig;
        bev = nothing, filename = "hr_diagram") -> String

Hertzsprung–Russell diagram (log Teff against log L) of epoch epoch of a run, coloured by stellar class ([`STELLARCLASSES](@ref)), above a census strip of the stars per class against time with the epoch marked. Axes and legend are those of the whole run, so the figures of different epochs of one run match. Withbevs`, the members of KS-regularised pairs are part of the population and drawn as open markers.

The single-snapshot method has no run to refer to: it shows the classes of that snapshot alone, without a strip, and notes the neutron stars and black holes it cannot draw.

Returns the output file path.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.plot_hr_evolution — Function
plot_hr_evolution(sevs::Vector{StellarEvolutionSnapshot}, cfg::VisualizationConfig;
                  bevs = BinaryEvolutionSnapshot[], max_panels = 6,
                  epochs = nothing, filename = "hr_evolution") -> String

HR diagrams of up to max_panels epochs spaced evenly through the run (or of the snapshot indices epochs), on shared axes under one legend, above a census strip of the stars per class against time with the panel epochs marked.

The legend lists every class present at any epoch of the run, not only at the epochs drawn: evolved phases are short against the output interval, and a class the panels miss still shows in the strip.

Returns the output file path.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.plot_escapers — Function
plot_escapers(escapers::Vector{EscaperRecord}, cfg::VisualizationConfig;
              filename = "escapers")

Two-panel escaper summary sharing the escape-time axis [Myr]: (top) cumulative escaped mass [M☉] as a step curve with the total escaper count and mass annotated; (bottom) escape velocity [km/s] on a log axis, distinguishing luminous stars (K* < 10) from compact remnants (K* ≥ 10).

Escaper quantities are intrinsically physical (esc.11 tokens 6–13), so the axes are Myr / M☉ / km s⁻¹ regardless of cfg.units. Warns and returns nothing for empty input; otherwise saves the figure and returns nothing.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.plot_escape_anisotropy — Function
plot_escape_anisotropy(escapers::Vector{EscaperRecord}, cfg::VisualizationConfig;
                       filename = "escape_anisotropy")

Sky projection of the escape directions from esc.11: azimuth φ [deg] (from the x-axis, native range [0°, 360°]) against elevation θ [deg] (from the xy-plane, [-90°, 90°]), on plain Cartesian axes with equal data margins. Luminous stars (K* < 10) and compact remnants (K* ≥ 10) are distinguished by colour and marker; the escaper count is annotated.

Warns and returns nothing when no record carries finite direction angles; otherwise saves the figure and returns nothing.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.plot_mass_segregation — Function
plot_mass_segregation(sev::StellarEvolutionSnapshot, cfg::VisualizationConfig;
                      filename = "mass_segregation")

Mass-segregation diagnostic from one stellar evolution snapshot: distance from the density centre RI [pc] (log x) against stellar mass [M☉] (log y), with the snapshot epoch annotated. Records with non-finite or non-positive RI or mass are skipped (both axes are logarithmic).

Warns and returns nothing when no record passes the validity filter; otherwise saves the figure and returns nothing.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.plot_evolutionary_clock — Function
plot_evolutionary_clock(sev::StellarEvolutionSnapshot, cfg::VisualizationConfig;
                        filename = "evolutionary_clock")

Evolutionary-clock histogram for the main-sequence population (K* ≤ 1) of one snapshot: the age fraction t/TMS, where t is the snapshot epoch [Myr] and TMS the per-star main-sequence lifetime TM [Myr] from SSE, clamped to [0, 2]. Stars past turnoff (t/TMS > 1) are counted in an annotation and the TMS boundary is marked. Records with non-finite or non-positive TM are skipped. The count axis turns logarithmic when the largest bin exceeds twenty times the median occupied bin, which otherwise flattens every bin but one.

Warns and returns nothing when no main-sequence record carries a valid TM; otherwise saves the figure and returns nothing.

Nbody6Dynamics.plot_core_mass — Function
plot_core_mass(sevs::Vector{StellarEvolutionSnapshot}, cfg::VisualizationConfig;

Core mass MC [M☉] against total stellar mass [M☉] for the evolved stars (K* ≥ 2) of the last snapshot, log–log, colour/marker-coded by stellar class as in the HR figures, with the epoch annotated and the MC = M identity guide marked. Records with non-finite or non-positive core or total mass are skipped (SSE fields are NaN in pre-v2026.07 data).

Warns and returns nothing when there are no evolved stars with a valid core mass — early snapshots are MS-only; otherwise saves the figure and returns nothing.

Run Telemetry

Nbody6Dynamics.TelemetrySample — Type
TelemetrySample

One sample of the simulation process tree and, when probed, the GPU(s). NaN marks quantities unavailable at sampling time.

Fields

  • elapsed_s: wall-clock time since launch [s]
  • n_processes: processes in the tree rooted at the launched PID
  • rss_mib: resident set size summed over the tree [MiB]
  • hwm_mib: resident high-water mark summed over the tree [MiB]
  • cpu_time_s: cumulative user + system CPU time of the live tree [s]
  • cores_busy: CPU-time rate since the previous sample [cores]
  • load_1min: system 1-minute load average
  • gpu_util_pct, gpu_mem_util_pct: mean over devices [%]
  • gpu_mem_used_mib: sum over devices [MiB]
  • gpu_power_w: sum over devices [W]
  • gpu_temp_c: maximum over devices [°C]
source
Nbody6Dynamics.plot_telemetry — Function
plot_telemetry(samples, cfg; filename = "telemetry") -> Union{Nothing,String}

Run telemetry against wall-clock time as stacked panels sharing the time axis: cores busy, with the host's 1-minute load average dotted on a twin axis (grey tick labels); resident memory (RSS solid, high-water mark dashed); and, when any sample carries GPU data, GPU utilisation with the memory utilisation dashed. The mean cores busy, the peak RSS and the mean GPU utilisation appear as the legend entries' quantitative takeaways. Returns the output path, or nothing with fewer than two samples.

Static Test Models

Nbody6Dynamics.sample_plummer_om — Function
sample_plummer_om(N::Int, a::Float64, r_a::Float64; rng = Random.default_rng()) -> (pos, vel)

Osipkov–Merritt anisotropic Plummer model of N equal-mass particles with scale radius a and anisotropy radius r_a (Merritt 1985, DOI 10.1086/113810). The density is that of the Plummer sphere, ρ = 3/(4π a³) (1 + r²/a²)^{-5/2}; the velocity anisotropy is β(r) = r²/(r² + r_a²), isotropic inside r_a and radially biased outside. r_a is in the same length units as a; r_a = Inf gives the isotropic Plummer model.

The distribution function follows from Eddington inversion in the variable Q = E + L²/(2r_a²): the augmented density ρ_Q = (1 + r²/r_a²) ρ is a polynomial in the relative potential ψ = (1 + r²/a²)^{-1/2}, so with x = a²/r_a² the inversion gives the closed form f(Q) ∝ (128/7)(1 − x) Q^{7/2} + 8x Q^{3/2}, which for x = 0 reduces to the isotropic Plummer distribution function ∝ (−E)^{7/2}. It is non-negative for all bound orbits only when x ≤ 16/9, i.e. r_a ≥ 0.75 a; smaller r_a is rejected.

Positions are drawn by the inverse CDF as in sample_plummer; speed and pitch angle by von Neumann rejection from the distribution function at the particle's radius, with a uniformly random azimuth of the tangential velocity.

Returns (pos::Matrix{Float64}, vel::Matrix{Float64}), each 3 × N, in units where G = M_total = 1.

Example

pos, vel = sample_plummer_om(10_000, 1.0, 1.5; rng = Xoshiro(1))
source
Nbody6Dynamics.lynden_bell_flip! — Function
lynden_bell_flip!(vel, pos, axis, p; rng = Random.default_rng()) -> vel

Lynden-Bell (1960) rotation by sign flips (DOI 10.1093/mnras/120.3.204). For every particle off the rotation axis axis (any non-zero 3-vector), the azimuthal velocity v_φ about that axis is given the positive sign with probability p and the negative sign otherwise, by reflecting the velocity in the meridional plane (v ← v − 2 v_φ φ̂). Particles on the axis are left untouched.

The density, the speeds, the radial velocities and every velocity dispersion are unchanged, so an equilibrium stays an equilibrium; the mean azimuthal velocity in any shell becomes (2p − 1)⟨|v_φ|⟩. p = ½ is the non-rotating null, p = 1 the maximal rotation the given model admits. vel is overwritten and returned.

Example

pos, vel = sample_plummer_om(10_000, 1.0, Inf)
lynden_bell_flip!(vel, pos, [0.0, 0.0, 1.0], 0.8)
source
Nbody6Dynamics.stretch_positions! — Function
stretch_positions!(pos, q, s) -> pos

Scale the y coordinates by q and the z coordinates by s, turning a spherical model into a static one with axis ratios b/a = q and c/a = s (for 1 ≥ q ≥ s) about the x, y, z axes, the known answer for the shape estimator (Zemp et al. 2011, DOI 10.1088/0067-0049/197/2/30). The result is not an equilibrium; velocities are left to the caller. pos (3 × N) is overwritten and returned.

Example

pos, _ = sample_plummer(10_000, 1.0)
stretch_positions!(pos, 0.8, 0.6)
source
Nbody6Dynamics.rank_masses_by_energy! — Function
rank_masses_by_energy!(mass, energy, S; rng = Random.default_rng()) -> mass

Mass segregation of tunable degree S for the estimator calibration, a scheme of its own: it is neither the S of Šubr, Kroupa & Baumgardt (2008, DOI 10.1111/j.1365-2966.2008.12993.x) nor that of Baumgardt, De Marchi & Kroupa (2008, DOI 10.1086/590488), and with positions kept and masses permuted it changes the mass-density profile, so it is not an initial-condition segregation method. A random fraction S of the particles (round(Int, S n) of n) receives the largest masses, ranked so that the most bound of them (lowest energy) carries the largest mass; the remaining masses go to the remaining particles in random order. S = 0 is a random permutation of the masses, S = 1 the full energy ranking.

The mass set is preserved exactly — the result is a permutation of the input — and only its assignment to particles changes; energy (per-particle specific or total energy, any consistent units) is not modified. mass is overwritten and returned.

Example

rank_masses_by_energy!(mass, energy, 0.5; rng = Xoshiro(1))
