Initial Mass Functions

Nbody6Dynamics.IMFSpec — Type

Tag type for stellar mass-function samplers. Each concrete subtype carries its own parameters and dispatches sample_masses.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.KroupaIMF — Type
KroupaIMF(; bodyn = 0.08, body1 = 100.0)

Natural Kroupa (2001) sampling in [bodyn, body1] M☉. The total cluster mass is an output (the sum of the N samples), not a specified parameter.

Use this mode when the scientific intent is a realistic stellar population. Stellar-evolution prescriptions in Nbody6++ are well-defined for this case.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.RescaledKroupaIMF — Type
RescaledKroupaIMF(; bodyn, body1, target_mass)

Super-particle mode: sample N stars from Kroupa in [bodyn, body1], then apply a uniform linear rescale so the sum equals target_mass. The IMF shape (power-law slope) is preserved but the effective mass range shifts by the rescale factor.

A warning is emitted at sample time when the rescale factor is outside ×[0.7, 1.4]: in that regime the individual body masses no longer correspond to real stars and downstream stellar-evolution output is non-physical.

Use this mode deliberately when constructing super-particle simulations.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.PowerLawIMF — Type
PowerLawIMF(; alpha = 2.35, bodyn = 0.08, body1 = 100.0)

Single power-law mass function dN/dm ∝ m^{−alpha} on [bodyn, body1] [M☉] (alpha = 2.35 is the Salpeter 1955 slope, DOI 10.1086/145971), drawn by the inverse cumulative distribution; alpha is any finite real (the logarithmic forms apply at 1 and 2).

source
Nbody6Dynamics.EqualMassIMF — Type
EqualMassIMF(; particle_mass)

Every body has identical mass particle_mass [M☉].

source
Nbody6Dynamics.imf_name — Function

Short label used in metadata TOML and logs.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.expected_mass — Function
expected_mass(imf::IMFSpec, N::Int) -> Float64

Expected total cluster mass [M☉] for N bodies drawn from imf — exact for RescaledKroupaIMF/EqualMassIMF, the analytic expectation for KroupaIMF.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.sample_masses — Function
sample_masses(imf::IMFSpec, N::Int, rng::AbstractRNG) -> Vector{Float64}

Draw N body masses [M☉] according to the IMF specification. Dispatches on the concrete IMFSpec subtype:

  • KroupaIMF — natural Kroupa sampling; no rescale.
  • RescaledKroupaIMF — Kroupa then uniform rescale to target_mass; emits a @warn when the rescale factor is outside ×[0.7, 1.4].
  • EqualMassIMF — every body gets particle_mass.
source
sample_masses(imf::PowerLawIMF, N, rng) -> Vector{Float64}

N masses [M☉] of the power law dN/dm ∝ m^{−alpha} on [bodyn, body1] by the inverse cumulative distribution, m = [m_lo^{1−α} + u (m_hi^{1−α} − m_lo^{1−α})]^{1/(1−α)} (m = m_lo (m_hi/m_lo)^u at α = 1).

source
Nbody6Dynamics.kroupa_mean_mass — Function
kroupa_mean_mass(m_low = 0.08, m_up = 100.0) -> Float64

Analytical mean stellar mass ⟨m⟩ of the Kroupa (2001) IMF restricted to [m_low, m_up] in M☉. Used for:

  • Sanity-checking over-determined configs (M_total / N should lie near this value for a natural stellar population).
  • Estimating the expected cluster mass when only N is specified.

For the standard range [0.08, 100] this returns ≈ 0.58 M☉.

source

Configuration

Nbody6Dynamics.ClusterSpec — Type
ClusterSpec(; N, rbar, profile, imf, position = Float64[], velocity = Float64[])

Specification for a single star cluster.

Fields

  • N::Int: number of bodies to sample.
  • rbar::Float64: target half-mass radius [pc].
  • profile::DensityProfile: spatial sampler (KingProfile, PlummerProfile or RotatingKingProfile).
  • imf::IMFSpec: mass-function sampler (KroupaIMF, RescaledKroupaIMF, or EqualMassIMF).
  • position::Vector{Float64}: centre-of-mass position [pc] (length 3). For orbit_mode = "kepler" and "single" this is left empty.
  • velocity::Vector{Float64}: centre-of-mass velocity [km s⁻¹]. For orbit_mode = "kepler" and "single" this is left empty.
  • binaries::BinarySpec: primordial binary population (none by default).
  • segregation::SegregationSpec: primordial mass segregation of the systems (SegregationSpec; none by default).
  • imbh::Union{Nothing,IMBHSpec}: seeded IMBH (IMBHSpec); nothing by default.

The keyword constructor takes the structured (tag-typed) fields only; the flat TOML schema is the concern of load_merger_config, which translates it into these types. The seven-argument positional form of earlier versions (without segregation) remains valid.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.SegregationSpec — Type
SegregationSpec(; method = "none", degree = 0.0, max_points = 10_000_000)

Primordial mass segregation of a cluster ([merger.clusterN.segregation]).

Fields

  • method::String: "none" (unsegregated sampling) or "bdk08".
  • degree::Float64: degree of segregation S, 0 ≤ S ≤ 1; 0 is a random assignment, 1 complete ordering.
  • max_points::Int: largest admitted number N′ of model points; > 0. N′ ≥ N ⟨m⟩/m_min grows with the mass range of the IMF, and every point costs its phase-space coordinates and energy in memory.

"bdk08" is the scheme of Baumgardt, De Marchi & Kroupa (2008, Appendix A; DOI 10.1086/590488) with the partial ordering of McLuster (Küpper et al. 2011, Eq. A1; DOI 10.1111/j.1365-2966.2011.19412.x). N′ ≥ N ⟨m⟩/m_min phase-space points of the unsegregated model are ranked by specific energy, most bound first; the systems, sorted by mass in descending order and permuted by the McLuster slot order of degree S, take one point each from consecutive energy slices of N′ m/M points. The mass-density profile of the model is preserved, so the cluster stays in virial equilibrium. The scheme acts on system masses: a primordial pair is placed as its centre of mass, before the pairs are expanded.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.BinarySpec — Type
BinarySpec(; fraction = 0.0, pairing = "random", period = "kroupa1995",
             a_min = 0.01, a_max = 100.0, q_min = 0.1, eccentricity = "thermal",
             roche_fill_max = 0.0, massive = MassiveBinarySpec())

Primordial binary population of one cluster ([merger.clusterN.binaries]). With a massive branch (massive.model ≠ "none") the keys fraction to eccentricity describe the stars below massive.m_min only.

Fields

  • fraction: binary fraction by systems, N_b / (N_s + N_b); 0 ≤ fraction < 1
  • pairing: "random" (both components drawn from the IMF; the more massive becomes the primary) or "uniform_q" (the secondary's mass is q m₁ with q uniform in [q_min, 1], replacing the drawn mass of the partner)
  • period: "kroupa1995" (the birth period distribution of Kroupa 1995b, DOI 10.1093/mnras/277.4.1507, Eq. 8, f(log P) ∝ (log P − 1) / (45 + (log P − 1)²) for 1 ≤ log P/d ≤ 8.43, converted to a semi-major axis with Kepler's third law) or "loguniform" (semi-major axis log-uniform between a_min and a_max). Kroupa's birth distribution belongs with his pre-main-sequence eigenevolution, which the generator does not apply: short eccentric orbits are not circularised and the mass ratios are not evolved (Belloni et al. 2017, DOI 10.1093/mnras/stx1763)
  • a_min, a_max: semi-major axis bounds [AU] for "loguniform"; 0 < a_min < a_max
  • q_min: lower mass-ratio bound for "uniform_q"; 0 < q_min < 1
  • eccentricity: "thermal" (f(e) = 2e) or "circular"
  • roche_fill_max: Roche-lobe guard at periastron for every pair, the largest admitted φ_i = R_i / (r_L(q_i) a (1 − e)) of either component; 0 (no guard) or 0 < roche_fill_max ≤ 1. R_i is the ZAMS radius of zams_radius (Tout et al. 1996, DOI 10.1093/mnras/281.1.257) and r_L the Roche-lobe radius of Eggleton (1983, DOI 10.1086/160960); the period and eccentricity laws are truncated, not rejected
  • massive: the branch of the massive primaries, a MassiveBinarySpec
source
Nbody6Dynamics.MassiveBinarySpec — Type
MassiveBinarySpec(; model = "none", m_min = 5.0, fraction = 0.69, pairing = "pool",
                    log_period_min = 0.15, log_period_max = 3.5, period_exponent = -0.55,
                    q_min = 0.1, q_exponent = -0.1, ecc_exponent = -0.45, ecc_max = 0.9,
                    ecc_turnover = true)

Binary population of the massive primaries of one cluster ([merger.clusterN.binaries.massive]). The defaults are the O-star distributions of Sana et al. (2012, DOI 10.1126/science.1223344): f(lP) ∝ lP^π, f(q) ∝ q^κ, f(e) ∝ e^η with lP = log10(P/d). π, κ and f_bin are those of Sana et al. (2012); η and the domains of lP, q and e are as tabulated by Cottaar & Hénault-Brunet (2014, DOI 10.1051/0004-6361/201322445, Table 1) and used by Sana et al. (2013, DOI 10.1051/0004-6361/201219621). fraction is the fraction of primaries with a companion at log(P/d) ≤ 3.5: a wider log_period_max keeps the density in log P only if fraction is raised accordingly. Sana et al. measured O stars (M ≳ 15 M☉); below that mass the branch is an extrapolation (Moe & Di Stefano 2017, DOI 10.3847/1538-4365/aa6fb6).

Fields

  • model: "none" (no massive branch; every star belongs to the population of BinarySpec), "sana2012" or "moe2017" (the joint distribution f(lP, q, e | M₁) of Moe & Di Stefano 2017 with the inner-binary probability of Moe's code; it ignores fraction, period_exponent, q_exponent, ecc_exponent and ecc_max and uses m_min, pairing, log_period_min, log_period_max, q_min and ecc_turnover)
  • m_min: primary-mass threshold [M☉] of the branch; > 0 (moe2017 warns below 0.8)
  • fraction: binary fraction of the massive primaries, one Bernoulli trial per primary reached unpaired; [0, 1] (Sana: f_bin = 0.69 ± 0.09)
  • pairing: "pool" (the partner is the unpaired star of mass ≤ m₁ closest to q m₁; the IMF multiset is kept) or "replace" (a random unpaired star below m_min takes the mass q m₁; this alters the IMF)
  • log_period_min, log_period_max: period range [log10 d]; 0 ≤ log_period_min < log_period_max ≤ 8.43 (Sana: 0.15 and 3.5, secondary); moe2017 requires log_period_min ≥ 0.15 and log_period_max ≤ 8.0
  • period_exponent: π (Sana: −0.55 ± 0.22)
  • q_min: lower mass-ratio bound; 0 < q_min < 1 (Sana: 0.1, secondary)
  • q_exponent: κ (Sana: −0.10 ± 0.58)
  • ecc_exponent: η; > −1 (Sana: −0.45 ± 0.17, secondary)
  • ecc_max: upper eccentricity bound; 0 < ecc_max < 1 (Sana: 0.9, secondary)
  • ecc_turnover: eccentricity law of moe2017. true is the variant of Moe's code: f(e) ∝ e^η below 0.8 e_max, carrying the probability 8/(9 + η), and a linear decline to zero at e_max above it. false is the paper-only law f(e) ∝ e^η on [0, e_max]
source
Nbody6Dynamics.OrbitSpec — Type
OrbitSpec(; apocentre = 15.0, eccentricity = 0.7, apocentre_rh = 0.0, pericentre = 0.0,
            pericentre_rh = 0.0, orbit_normal = [0.0, 0.0, -1.0], apocentre_angle = 0.0)

Orbital parameters for the 2-cluster Kepler convenience mode. Ignored for orbit_mode = "explicit" and "single". The clusters start at apocentre.

Fields

  • apocentre::Float64: apocentre separation [pc]; used when none of the three keys below is positive.
  • eccentricity::Float64: orbital eccentricity, 0 ≤ e < 1.
  • apocentre_rh::Float64: apocentre separation in units of the input half-mass radius r_h,1 of cluster 1 (0 = unused).
  • pericentre::Float64: pericentre separation [pc] (0 = unused); the apocentre is r_p (1 + e) / (1 − e).
  • pericentre_rh::Float64: pericentre separation in units of r_h,1 (0 = unused).
  • orbit_normal::Vector{Float64}: direction of the orbital angular momentum of the pair (three components, any non-zero norm).
  • apocentre_angle::Float64: rotation of the apocentre direction about orbit_normal [degrees].

At most one of the TOML keys apocentre, apocentre_rh, pericentre, pericentre_rh may be given; none falls back to apocentre = 15.0. orbit_apocentre resolves the apocentre in pc.

Orientation: with n the unit orbit_normal, the reference direction u is the projection of x̂ onto the plane normal to n (of ŷ when n ∥ x̂); the separation vector cluster 2 − cluster 1 at the start points along e_apo = u cos φ + (n × u) sin φ, φ = apocentre_angle, and the relative velocity along n × e_apo, so the orbital angular momentum is along n. The default [0, 0, −1] with φ = 0 is the orbit of earlier versions: in the x–y plane, cluster 2 on +x at apocentre and moving towards −y.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.ComplexSpec — Type
ComplexSpec(; n_members = 0, mass_total = 0.0, mass_function = "equal", beta = 2.0,
              mass_lo = 100.0, mass_hi = 1.0e4, size_mass = "constant", rh = 1.0,
              size_scatter_dex = 0.0, centres = "uniform_sphere", radius = 5.0,
              plummer_cutoff = 5.0, fractal_dimension = 1.6, filament_length = 20.0,
              filament_width = 1.0, virial_ratio = 0.5, rotation_fraction = 0.0,
              softening = 0.0, member_n_min = 50, imf_partition = "member",
              control = "none")

Parameters of a cluster complex ([merger.complex], orbit_mode = "complex"). Masses in M☉, lengths in pc, speeds in km s⁻¹.

FieldTypeDefaultMeaning, bounds
n_membersInt0number of members; ≥ 2 when > 0
mass_totalFloat640.0target total mass [M☉]; > 0 when used
mass_functionString"equal""equal" or "power_law"
betaFloat642.0dN/dM ∝ M^−β; finite (power_law)
mass_lo, mass_hiFloat64100.0, 1.0e4bounds [M☉]; 0 < lo < hi (power_law)
size_massString"constant""constant", "marks_kroupa", "frost", "dobbs"
rhFloat641.0half-mass radius for "constant" [pc]; > 0
size_scatter_dexFloat640.0log-normal scatter of r_h [dex]; ≥ 0
centresString"uniform_sphere""uniform_sphere", "plummer", "fractal", "filament"
radiusFloat645.0sphere radius / Plummer scale a / fractal radius [pc]; > 0
plummer_cutoffFloat645.0Plummer truncation in units of a; > 1
fractal_dimensionFloat641.6box-fractal dimension D; 1 ≤ D ≤ 3
filament_lengthFloat6420.0[pc]; > 0
filament_widthFloat641.0Gaussian transverse σ [pc]; > 0
virial_ratioFloat640.5target Q_cc = T/|W| of the centres; ≥ 0
rotation_fractionFloat640.0Trot/T of a solid-body rotation about +z; 0 ≤ f < 1; 0 when virialratio = 0
softeningFloat640.0Plummer softening ε of the point-mass energy [pc]; 0 = the mean member r_h; ≥ 0
member_n_minInt50smallest allowed member star count; ≥ 2
imf_partitionString"member""member" (each member samples its own IMF) or "global" (one star list partitioned)
controlString"none""none", "monolithic" (C-E) or "cold" (C-cold); needs imf_partition = "global"

Member masses

"equal" needs n_members > 0 and mass_total > 0 and gives every member mass_total / n_members. "power_law" takes exactly one of them: n_members draws, or draws until the running sum reaches or exceeds mass_total (the last draw is kept). The inverse CDF of dN/dM ∝ M^−β on [lo, hi] with u uniform in [0, 1) is M = (lo^(1−β) + u (hi^(1−β) − lo^(1−β)))^(1/(1−β)) for β ≠ 1 and M = lo (hi/lo)^u for β = 1.

Size–mass relations

r_h = rh ("constant"); r_h = 0.10 pc (M/M☉)^0.13 ("marks_kroupa", Marks & Kroupa 2012, Eq. 7, DOI 10.1051/0004-6361/201118231); r_h = (2.365/8) pc (M/10⁴ M☉)^0.18 ("frost", Rantala, Naab & Lahén 2024, DOI 10.1093/mnras/stae1413, with fh = 1/8 and R₄ = 2.365 pc; 0.45 pc at 10⁵ M☉); `rh = 2.26 pc (M/10⁴ M☉)^0.27("dobbs", Dobbs et al. 2022, DOI 10.1093/mnras/stac2474). Each radius is multiplied by10^(sizescatterdex g),g` standard normal.

IMF partition

"member": member i has Nᵢ = round(Mᵢ / ⟨m⟩) stars drawn from the template IMF. "global": one star list is drawn from the template IMF in batches of max(1000, ⌈1.1 ΣMᵢ / ⟨m⟩⌉) until its sum reaches ΣMᵢ, shuffled, and dealt out in order: member i takes stars until the running total reaches or exceeds the cumulative target Σ{j≤i} Mⱼ, the last member until it reaches ΣMᵢ, so every member is within one star of its target, and the rest of the list is dropped (`complexpartitionstars). Each member then carries its share as anExplicitMassesIMF`, and its radius follows from its realised mass.

Controls

"monolithic" (C-E) and "cold" (C-cold) replace the complex by one Plummer cluster of the same star list, built from the same seed, whose positions and velocities are rescaled about the centre of mass to the total energy E of the complex's systems (primordial pairs as point masses), keeping Q = 0.5 (C-E) or matching also the global Q = T/|W| of the complex (C-cold). The internal energy of the pairs is not matched (the control draws its own pairs), template pairings that change the stellar masses are refused, and the control is not segregated.

Centre distributions

  • "uniform_sphere": r = radius u^(1/3), isotropic direction; velocities standard normal per component.
  • "plummer": a = radius, r = a (u^(−2/3) − 1)^(−1/2) redrawn while r > plummer_cutoff a; speed q (r² + a²)^(−1/4) with g(q) ∝ q² (1 − q²)^(7/2) by rejection, isotropic velocity direction (Aarseth, Hénon & Wielen 1974, A&A 37, 183).
  • "fractal": the box fractal of Goodwin & Whitworth (2004, DOI 10.1051/0004-6361:20031529) of dimension D: each parent cube of side L spawns 8 children at ± L/4 per coordinate with uniform noise of ± 0.05 L, inheriting the parent velocity plus a Gaussian component of standard deviation 2^(−g) in generation g (halved per generation); a child is a parent of the next generation with probability 2^(D − 3). The construction stops at the first generation with at least n parents inside the unit sphere, of which n are chosen at random and scaled by radius.
  • "filament": x uniform in [−L/2, L/2] (L = filament_length), y and z normal with σ = filament_width; velocities standard normal.

Velocity field

With W = −Σ_{i<j} G mᵢ mⱼ / √(|rᵢ − rⱼ|² + ε²) of the centred members and w = ẑ × r, the raw velocities lose their mass-weighted projection on w and become α v + β w with α = √((1 − f) Q |W| / T_v) and β = √(f Q |W| / T_w), so that T = Q |W| and the solid-body rotation carries the fraction f = rotation_fraction of T (complex_scale_velocities!). The spin parameter λ = |L| √|E| / (G M^(5/2)) of the centres follows Peebles (1969, DOI 10.1086/149876).

source
Nbody6Dynamics.MergerOutputSpec — Type
MergerOutputSpec(; format = "nbody", truncate_jacobi = true,
                   output_dir = "", tcrit = 100.0, dtadj = 1.0, deltat = 1.0,
                   truncate_radius_rh = 0.0, truncate_rule = "roche")

Output and integration parameters for merger ICs.

Fields

  • format::String: "nbody" (KZ(22)=2, N-body units) — the only supported format.
  • truncate_jacobi::Bool: truncate each cluster at its orbit-dependent tidal radius with respect to its nearest neighbour (truncate_rule) before combining.
  • truncate_radius_rh::Float64: with truncate_jacobi = true and a positive value, every cluster is truncated at truncate_radius_rh times its own input half-mass radius instead of the orbit-dependent radius (kepler and explicit modes); 0 keeps the orbit-dependent radius; ≥ 0.
  • truncate_rule::String: the orbit-dependent radius, "roche" (the Roche lobe of Eggleton 1983, roche_truncation_radius, inside the inner Lagrange point at every mass ratio) or "hill" (the Hill radius jacobi_radius, valid for a cluster much lighter than its neighbour; the rule of earlier versions).
  • output_dir::String: directory for generated files; empty (the default) leaves the choice to the caller — run_merger_pipeline then creates a runs/merger_<timestamp>/ under the working directory, and the main pipeline always writes into its run directory.
  • tcrit::Float64: simulation end time (NB units when format = "nbody").
  • dtadj::Float64, deltat::Float64: adjustment and snapshot intervals.
  • tcrit_myr, dtadj_myr, deltat_myr: the same three in Myr; a positive value replaces the NB one, converted at generation with the realised N-body time unit of the configuration (0 = unused). A key may be given in one form only.

dtadj and deltat (and the stellar dtplot) are written as the nearest dyadic rational with an exact decimal expansion (engine_interval): the engine counts the decimal digits of these intervals with a loop that never terminates on other values. For intervals ≥ 0.25 NB the change is below 0.4 %; below that the nine-digit cap binds, the spacing stays 2⁻⁹ NB and the change reaches up to 2⁻¹⁰ NB.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.Nbody6ParameterSpec — Type
Nbody6ParameterSpec(; qe = 2.0e-4, etai = 0.0, etar = 0.0, etau = 0.0,
                      nnbopt = 0, rs0 = 0.0, rmin = 0.0, dtmin = 0.0,
                      eclose = 1.0, gmin = 1.0e-6, gmax = 0.01, smax = 1.0,
                      kz16 = 0, tcomp = 1.0e8, tcrtp0 = 3600.0,
                      isernb = 40, iserreg = 40, iserks = 0,
                      nfix = 1, ncrit = 10, nrun = 1, ncomm = 1, checkpoint = false,
                      preset = "engine", binary_physics = "inherited",
                      gw_recoil = "off", bh_spin = "zero", escapers = "remove",
                      imbh_route = "auto", imbh_output_dt_myr = 1.0,
                      post_newtonian = "off",
                      kz = Dict{Int,Int}())

Every numerical field of the &INNBODY6 and &ININPUT namelists that the merger input writer emits ([merger.nbody6]), in the N-body units of the combined system. A zero for etai, etar, etau, nnbopt, rs0, rmin, or dtmin means "the preset's value or rule, resolved at generation time" (resolve_nbody6_parameters). Meanings follow the Nbody6++GPU input manual.

Fields

  • qe: energy-error tolerance per adjustment interval; > 0
  • etai, etar, etau: irregular, regular, and regularised time-step factors; ≥ 0; 0 = preset value
  • nnbopt: target neighbour number; 0 = clamp(round(√N_total), 20, 300)
  • rs0: initial neighbour-sphere radius; 0 = derived; must not exceed the smallest member half-mass radius
  • rmin, dtmin: KS regularisation distance and time step; 0 = derived
  • eclose: binding energy per unit mass of a hard binary; > 0
  • gmin, gmax: relative perturbation for unperturbed KS motion and the soft-binary termination parameter; 0 < gmin < gmax
  • smax: maximum time step (a power of two commensurate with 1); > 0
  • kz16: KZ(16), engine re-derivation of RMIN, DTMIN, ECLOSE every DTADJ; 0 keeps the written values (recommended for multi-cluster systems); 0–3
  • tcomp: run-time limit [s]; > 0
  • tcrtp0: termination time [Myr]; > 0
  • isernb, iserreg, iserks: MPI block-size thresholds for serial execution of irregular, regular, and KS blocks; ≥ 0
  • nfix: multiplier of DELTAT for conf.3 and binary output; ≥ 1
  • ncrit: minimum particle number, alternative termination criterion; ≥ 1
  • nrun: run identification index; ≥ 1
  • ncomm: the engine writes a restart (COMMON) dump on every ncomm-th dump call, counting the periodic and the terminating calls together; ≥ 1. Keep 1: with a larger value the dump of an END RUN, of a stop request or of an energy halt is written only when the count happens to fit
  • checkpoint: true writes a restart dump at every adjustment (KZ(2) = 1), from which an interrupted run resumes; requires ncomm = 1. false (KZ(2) = −1) leaves the dump at the end of the run or at a stop request as the only one
  • preset: "engine" or "baseline"; the source of every zero entry. "engine": the rules the engine's own example inputs follow (η = 0.02, neighbour number √N, RMIN from the engine's adjust.F rule for the smallest member, DTMIN from the Aarseth close-encounter step). "baseline": the integration baseline of the calibration programme — ETAI 0.01, ETAR 0.02, ETAU 0.1, NNBOPT 100, RMIN three times the 90° deflection scale 4 r_h/N of the smallest member, DTMIN 0.4 of the Aarseth close-encounter step at that RMIN; the engine's automatic re-derivation of these quantities (KZ(16), KZ(17), KZ(32)) is refused. Explicit nonzero keys override the preset in both cases.
  • binary_physics: "inherited" or "full"; the few-body and binary options. "inherited" writes the block the generator has always written: no chain and no stable hierarchies (KZ(15) = 0), no gravitational-wave decay of compact pairs (KZ(27) = 0), no binary event output. "full" writes the complete set adopted for the binary programme (second table below, _FULL_BINARY_KZ) with the Level defaults of &INSSE, &INBSE and &INCOLL; it requires stellar evolution (stellar.kz19 ≥ 3), since the decay, tides and Roche transfer act on the stellar types, and an engine from the fork tag v2026.07.6: from v2026.07-campaign.5 on the decay of compact KS pairs follows the Peters equations, and earlier tags end the run at the first binary–binary chain and can end a run with an unrestartable dump inside a chain. The former names preset = "campaign" and binary_physics = "campaign" are read as "baseline" and "full" with a deprecation warning
  • gw_recoil: recoil of the remnant of a merger of two neutron stars or black holes (&INBSE kicktype, applied by COAL and MIX under stellar.kz19 ≥ 3): "off" (−2, the Level C default, no recoil), "fit_q" (0, the mass-ratio fit), "nr_fits" (1, the numerical-relativity fits with random spin orientations; the recoil direction of the engine is not isotropic) or "test_1e4" (−1, 10⁴ km s⁻¹, a test value); any value but "off" requires stellar.kz19 ≥ 3
  • bh_spin: the dimensionless spin given to a black hole at formation (&INBSE bhspinfl): "zero" (0, the Level C default), "uniform" (1), "gaussian" (2, mean 0.5, width 0.2), "maxwellian" (3, σ = 0.2), "geneva" (4) or "mesa" (5, tracks in Z and the CO-core mass); the draws of 1–3 use an unseeded generator in the engine and are not restart-reproducible (warned)
  • escapers: "remove" (KZ(23) = 2, the escapers beyond twice the tidal radius are removed, the behaviour the generator has always written) or "keep" (KZ(23) = 0, every star stays in the integration with its full phase space, for tails and the escaper catalogues; the cost grows with the escaped population)
  • imbh_route: how the seeded IMBHs (IMBHSpec) reach the engine: "kz24" (KZ(24) = 1, the IMBH as the last body of dat.10 repeated on the IMBHINIT line; one IMBH), "flag" (KZ(24) = 3, engine fork ≥ v2026.07.11: the IMBHs as the last bodies of dat.10, one per cluster and 1 to 16 in all, flagged by their names on the line after NIMBHS DTBH) or "auto" (kz24 for one IMBH, flag for several); see resolved_imbh_route
  • imbh_output_dt_myr: interval of the engine's IMBH record mbh.45 (DTBH) [Myr]; > 0
  • post_newtonian: "off" (KZ(43) = 0) or "full" (KZ(43) = 7, engine fork ≥ v2026.07.14, whose booking of the PN work closes the energy check; v2026.07.12 and .13 halt embedded runs): the 1PN, 2PN and 2.5PN relative accelerations of Blanchet (2024, DOI 10.1007/s41114-024-00050-z) in every perturbed KS step of a bound pair of two neutron stars or black holes (seeded IMBHs included) with a pericentre compactness m/(c² q) ≥ 10⁻⁶; such steps run without slow-down and replace the orbit-averaged Peters decay, which unperturbed pairs keep. "full" requires the Peters path, KZ(27) = 3 (from binary_physics = "full" or a kz override), which also holds the merger test
  • kz: explicit KZ(i) = v overrides applied last ([merger.nbody6.kz], keys 1–50); an override of an index that also has a named key (8, 14, 16,
    1. warns. KZ(18) ≥ 2 is refused: it runs the engine's primordial-triple
    generator on the dat.10 pairs, and the generator writes no triples (NHI0 = 0). KZ(46) > 0 (per-binary custom output, several GB per N-body time unit at 6×10⁵ bodies) warns

Nbody6Dynamics.StellarSpec — Type
StellarSpec(; kz19 = 3, level = "C", zmet = 0.001, epoch0 = 0.0, dtplot = 1.0,
              dtplot_myr = 0.0)

Stellar-evolution settings of merger.inp ([merger.stellar]):

  • kz19: KZ(19), evolution and mass-loss scheme; 0 = off, 1–2 supernova schemes, ≥ 3 Eggleton–Tout–Hurley (SSE); ≥ 0
  • level: SSE/BSE parameter level (Kamlah et al. 2022): "A", "B", "C", or "0" (no level; the engine's independent defaults)
  • zmet: metal abundance; 0.0001 ≤ zmet ≤ 0.03 (the engine's own bounds)
  • epoch0: formation time of the population [Myr]; ≤ 0 (age at start is −epoch0)
  • dtplot: interval of the stellar-evolution diagnostics [NB]; > 0 and, per the manual, ≥ deltat
  • dtplot_myr: the same in Myr; a positive value replaces dtplot at generation, converted with the realised time unit
source
Nbody6Dynamics.TidalSpec — Type
TidalSpec(; kz14 = 0, gmg = 0.0, rg0 = 0.0, rg = zeros(3), vg = zeros(3),
          velocity_frame = "inertial")

External galactic field of merger.inp ([merger.tidal], KZ(14) and the &INXTRNL0 namelist):

  • kz14: 0 isolated; 1 standard solar-neighbourhood linearised tide (no parameters); 2 point-mass galaxy on a circular orbit (gmg, rg0); 5 MWPotential2014 with the cluster on a galactocentric orbit (rg, vg). Options 3 (point mass + disk + halo + bulge) and 4 (Plummer potential) are refused: the engine rescales every velocity to the &INSCALE virial ratio including the external potential on those paths (xtrnl0.F), which would destroy the prescribed orbital kinematics of a multi-cluster configuration.
  • gmg: galaxy mass [M☉] (kz14 = 2); > 0
  • rg0: galactocentric distance of the circular orbit [kpc] (kz14 = 2); > 0
  • rg, vg: galactocentric position [kpc] and velocity [km s⁻¹] of the configuration's centre of mass (kz14 = 5); both non-zero
  • velocity_frame: "rotating" or "inertial". Under kz14 = 1, 2 the engine integrates in the frame that rotates with the circular Galactic orbit, Ω = Ω ẑ (Ω = A − B = 26.4 km s⁻¹ kpc⁻¹ for kz14 = 1, VG/RG for kz14 = 2), and takes the dat.10 velocities as velocities in that frame (xtrnl0.F, xtrnld.f). The generator builds the clusters and the orbit in an inertial frame, so "inertial" (the default) converts them, writing v − Ω × r for every body. "rotating" writes the generated velocities as they are (the behaviour of earlier versions): clusters non-rotating and a Kepler orbit of the pair in the rotating frame. Without a rotating frame (kz14 = 0, 5) the value has no effect; the TOML key is then refused at load, a programmatic TidalSpec may carry either value.

The &INSCALE tidal radius stays 0 so the engine derives it from the field with the generator's RBAR (a non-zero value would override RBAR).

source
Nbody6Dynamics.MergerConfig — Type
MergerConfig(clusters, orbit_mode, orbit, output[, nbody6, stellar, tidal, seed, virial_max_n,
             seed_streams, complex])

Top-level configuration for multi-cluster merger initial conditions.

Orbit modes

  • "kepler" — exactly 2 clusters placed on a Keplerian orbit at apocentre. Requires an OrbitSpec. Cluster positions/velocities are computed automatically.
  • "explicit" — any number of clusters (≥ 2). Each cluster must specify position = [x, y, z] and velocity = [vx, vy, vz] in its TOML block. The orbit section is ignored (with a warning if it appears in TOML).
  • "single" — one cluster at the origin, at rest (n_clusters = 1), without truncation. position and velocity are refused; the orbit section is ignored (with a warning if it appears in TOML).
  • "complex" — a cluster complex built from a ComplexSpec: member masses from a mass function, half-mass radii from a size–mass relation, centres from a distribution and the bulk velocities scaled to a target virial ratio of the members as softened point masses. clusters holds the member template ([merger.complex.member], N and rbar placeholders); the members are assembled as in explicit mode. n_clusters and [merger.clusterN] tables are refused; the orbit section is ignored (with a warning if it appears in TOML).

Fields

  • clusters::Vector{ClusterSpec}
  • orbit_mode::String: "kepler", "explicit", "single" or "complex"
  • orbit::OrbitSpec
  • output::MergerOutputSpec
  • nbody6::Nbody6ParameterSpec: integration and run-control parameters
  • stellar::StellarSpec: stellar-evolution settings
  • tidal::TidalSpec: external galactic field
  • seed::Union{Int, Nothing}: RNG seed. nothing = non-deterministic.
  • virial_max_n::Int: largest N, per cluster and for the combined system, for which the exact O(N²) potential is evaluated (virialisation of each cluster, combined virial ratio); default _VIRIAL_NMAX (200 000)
  • seed_streams::String: "shared" (default) draws every cluster and its pairs from one MersenneTwister(seed) stream; "per_cluster" gives cluster i its own stream seeded by (seed, i), which samples and expands its pairs, so a cluster's realisation does not depend on the other clusters or on the orbit
  • complex::ComplexSpec: parameters of orbit_mode = "complex"; default ComplexSpec(), unused in the other modes
  • mbh::Union{Nothing,MBHSpec}: static massive black hole at the origin ([merger.mbh], MBHSpec); nothing by default
source
Nbody6Dynamics.MergerICResult — Type
MergerICResult

Structured output from generate_merger_ic, carrying everything needed for downstream plotting and inspection without re-reading files.

Fields

  • output_dir::String
  • N_total::Int: total particle count (after optional Jacobi truncation)
  • M_total::Float64: total mass [M☉]
  • rbar::Float64: half-mass radius [pc]
  • zmbar::Float64: mean particle mass [M☉]
  • cluster_ranges::Vector{Vector{Int}}: body indices of every cluster in dat.10 order (a contiguous range without primordial binaries; with them, the cluster's pairs block followed by its singles block)
  • n_pairs::Vector{Int}: primordial pairs per cluster (sum = NBIN0)
  • cluster_specs::Vector{ClusterSpec}
  • orbit_mode::String
  • orbit_spec::OrbitSpec
  • mass_physical::Vector{Float64}: [M☉]
  • pos_physical::Matrix{Float64}: 3×N [pc]
  • vel_physical::Matrix{Float64}: 3×N [km/s]
source
Nbody6Dynamics.load_merger_config — Function
load_merger_config(path::AbstractString) -> MergerConfig

Load a merger IC configuration from a TOML file. Accepts the canonical flat schema and the structured metadata schema interchangeably. Keys the schema does not define are rejected, and parsed values are validated fail-fast (N ≥ 2, rbar > 0, W0 > 0, IMF mass bounds, Kepler orbit parameters, positive integration intervals); violations raise an ArgumentError naming the offending merger.* key.

Kepler mode (2 clusters, flat form)

[merger]
n_clusters = 2
orbit_mode = "kepler"

[merger.cluster1]
model = "king"
N = 50000
rbar = 2.0
W0 = 6.0
imf = "kroupa"
bodyn = 0.08
body1 = 100.0
# mass_total optional: present → super-particle rescale; absent → natural Kroupa

[merger.orbit]
apocentre = 15.0
eccentricity = 0.7

Explicit mode (N clusters)

Each cluster provides position = [x, y, z] and velocity = [vx, vy, vz].

Single mode (1 cluster)

n_clusters = 1, orbit_mode = "single": the cluster is placed at the origin, at rest; position and velocity must be absent.

Structured form (equivalent to flat; used by the merger_ic.toml metadata round-trip)

[merger.cluster1]
N       = 1500
rbar    = 1.0
profile = { type = "king", W0 = 6.0 }
imf     = { type = "kroupa", bounds = [0.08, 100.0] }
source

IC Generation

Nbody6Dynamics.run_merger_pipeline — Function
run_merger_pipeline(config_path::AbstractString; rng=nothing,
                    output_dir="", make_plots=true) -> MergerICResult

One-call entry point for the initial conditions of a single cluster or of a multi-cluster merger.

  1. Loads the merger TOML configuration from config_path
  2. Generates particle ICs (Plummer/King + Kroupa IMF + Kepler orbit)
  3. Writes dat.10 + merger.inp + merger_summary.txt
  4. Generates diagnostic plots (projections, velocity field, IMF, density)

The output directory is output_dir when given, else the TOML's [merger.output] output_dir (resolved against the TOML's own directory when relative), else a fresh runs/merger_<timestamp>/ under the working directory. Returns a MergerICResult for programmatic inspection.

Example

using Nbody6Dynamics
result = run_merger_pipeline("input_files/mergers/merger_equal_mass.toml")
source
Nbody6Dynamics.generate_merger_ic — Function
generate_merger_ic(cfg::MergerConfig; rng=Random.default_rng(),
                   output_dir="") -> MergerICResult

Generate complete merger initial conditions from a MergerConfig. Produces dat.10, merger.inp, and merger_summary.txt.

Supports four orbit modes:

  • "kepler": 2 clusters on a Keplerian orbit (positions/velocities auto-computed)
  • "explicit": N clusters with user-specified CM positions and velocities
  • "single": one cluster at the origin, at rest (no truncation)
  • "complex": the members of a cluster complex (cfg.complex): masses, radii and centres drawn from the complex's own stream _cluster_rng(seed, 0), member i sampled from _cluster_rng(seed, i); after sampling, the centres and bulk velocities are scaled to the target virial ratio of the realised member masses as softened point masses (complex_scale_velocities!, softening cfg.complex.softening or the mean member half-mass radius), and the members are combined as in explicit mode

With cfg.complex.control = "monolithic" or "cold" the complex is built first as without the control (same streams, nothing written) and the energies of its systems are evaluated (pairs as point masses; their internal energy is not matched, the control draws its own pairs); one Plummer cluster of the union of the members' star lists, sampled and virialised from _cluster_rng(seed, 1) at r_h = 1 pc, is then rescaled about its centre of mass to the complex's total energy (C-E, Q = 0.5) or to its total energy and virial ratio (C-cold), and is written instead as a single cluster at the origin, at rest.

With cfg.seed_streams = "per_cluster" (always in complex mode) and no external rng, cluster i is sampled and its pairs expanded from its own stream (_cluster_rng(seed, i)); an external rng replaces every stream.

Every cluster with an IMBHSpec gets its IMBH, in cluster order, on its systems after truncation and before pair expansion: centred on their centre of mass (plus the offset), its velocity from the cluster's stream for velocity = "equipartition", and with response = "jeans" the systems' velocities rescaled. The IMBHs are the last bodies of dat.10, in cluster order (names N_stars + 1, …). The route follows resolved_imbh_route: under :kz24 (one IMBH) its state is repeated on the IMBHINIT line of merger.inp (KZ(24) = 1); under :flag (KZ(24) = 3, up to 16) the lines NIMBHS DTBH and the IMBH names follow &INSCALE. N_total and M_total include the IMBHs, the cluster ranges and RBAR (the stars' half-mass radius) do not. Refused: M•/M_stars > 0.05 and M•/⟨m⟩ < 20 (warned above 0.02, below 50 and above 10⁴).

Returns a MergerICResult for downstream plotting and inspection.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.load_merger_ic_result — Function
load_merger_ic_result(dir::AbstractString) -> MergerICResult

Reconstruct a MergerICResult from the dat.10 and merger_ic.toml files in dir, without re-sampling. Useful when the IC plots need to be regenerated after a code fix — e.g. bug fixes to plot_merger_ic or legend rendering — without changing the underlying particle data.

The particle positions and velocities in the returned result are in physical units (pc and km/s), matching what plot_merger_ic expects. Conversion from the NB-unit dat.10 uses the stored rbar and the mass scaling (G = 1 in the (M☉, pc, km/s) system with the bundled scaling). A cluster recorded with an explicit star list (imf.type = "explicit") gets an ExplicitMassesIMF of its masses read back from dat.10, with N their count.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.plot_merger_ic — Function
plot_merger_ic(result::MergerICResult, vis::VisualizationConfig)

Generate all diagnostic plots for a merger IC:

  • 3× spatial projections (XY, XZ, YZ) with mass colourmap
  • 3-panel overview
  • Velocity field quiver plot coloured by cluster membership
  • Kroupa IMF histogram with reference power-law slopes
  • Radial mass-density profile per cluster (ρ in M☉ pc⁻³)

All plots use the publication theme and are saved to vis.output_dir.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.sample_plummer — Function
sample_plummer(N::Int, a::Float64; rng=Random.default_rng()) -> (pos, vel)

Generate N particles from a Plummer model with scale radius a using inverse CDF sampling (Aarseth, Hénon & Wielen 1974).

Returns (pos::Matrix{Float64}, vel::Matrix{Float64}) each 3 × N, in internal units where G = M_total = 1. Useful Plummer relations: virial radius r_v = 16a/(3π) ≈ 1.70 a, half-mass radius r_hm = a/√(2^{2/3} − 1) ≈ 1.305 a.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.sample_king — Function
sample_king(N::Int, W0::Float64, rt::Float64;
            rng=Random.default_rng()) -> (pos, vel)

Generate N particles from a King (1966) model with central dimensionless potential W0 and tidal radius rt.

Uses the standard recipe:

  1. Integrate the King ODE to get the density-potential pair (ρ̂, Ŵ)(r̂)
  2. Rejection-sample particle positions from the density profile
  3. At each radius, rejection-sample velocities from the lowered Maxwellian

Returns (pos::Matrix{Float64}, vel::Matrix{Float64}) each 3 × N, in units where the tidal radius equals rt.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.model_density — Function
model_density(profile::DensityProfile, M, r_h) -> Function

Density ρ(r) of the generating model with total mass M and half-mass radius r_h (any consistent unit system): Plummer analytically, ρ = 3M/(4π a³) (1 + r²/a²)^{-5/2} with a = r_h / 1.305; King from the solved dimensionless profile scaled so that its half-mass radius equals r_h and its mass equals M (zero beyond the tidal radius).

source
model_density(p::RotatingKingProfile, M, r_h) -> Function

Angle-averaged density ρ̄(r) of the rotating King model (solve_rotating_king, cached per (W0, omega0)), scaled so that its half-mass radius equals r_h and its mass M; zero beyond the model's outermost radius. The flattening of the model is averaged out: the function serves the spherical profile overlays of the figures.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.sample_kroupa — Function
sample_kroupa(N::Int; m_low=0.08, m_up=100.0,
              rng=Random.default_rng()) -> Vector{Float64}

Draw N stellar masses from the Kroupa (2001) broken power-law IMF using inverse-CDF sampling (no rejection, O(N)).

Only the segments of the Kroupa IMF that overlap [m_low, m_up] are active. Masses are returned in M☉, un-normalised — the caller is responsible for any post-sampling rescale.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.virialise! — Function
virialise!(mass, pos, vel; nmax = 200_000) -> (; T, W)

Shift to centre-of-mass frame and scale velocities so that the virial ratio Q = T/|W| = 0.5 (virial equilibrium). Operates in-place and returns the kinetic and potential energies after scaling (G = 1).

Assumes G = 1. Uses the exact N-body potential energy — O(N²), threaded over strided rows. Refuses (with a clear error) above nmax particles rather than silently burning hours; raise nmax deliberately for large ICs.

Determinism note: the threaded pair sum accumulates per-task partials, so the floating-point summation order — and therefore the bit-exact velocity scale — depends on Threads.nthreads(). Seeded IC generation is bit- reproducible for a fixed thread count; across thread counts results agree to floating-point roundoff only.

source

Orbital Mechanics

Nbody6Dynamics.kepler_velocity — Function
kepler_velocity(M1, M2, d_apo, ecc) -> (v1, v2)

Compute centre-of-mass velocities at apocentre for a two-body Keplerian orbit.

Arguments

  • M1, M2: cluster masses (any consistent unit system, G=1 assumed)
  • d_apo: apocentre separation
  • ecc: orbital eccentricity (0 = circular, <1 = bound)

Returns

Tuple (v1, v2) — scalar speeds along the tangential direction at apocentre. The direction is perpendicular to the line connecting the two centres.

At apocentre, the velocity is purely tangential:

\[v_{\rm apo} = \sqrt{\frac{G(M_1+M_2)(1-e)}{a(1+e)}}\]

where $a = d_{\rm apo}/(1+e)$ is the semi-major axis.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.jacobi_radius — Function
jacobi_radius(d, M_self, M_other) -> Float64

Hill (Jacobi) radius at separation d:

\[r_J = d \left(\frac{M_{\rm self}}{3 M_{\rm other}}\right)^{1/3}\]

This is the limit M_self ≪ M_other of the Roche problem; at comparable masses it overestimates the lobe and at equal masses (0.69 d) lies beyond the inner Lagrange point (0.5 d). The cluster truncation uses it under [merger.output] truncate_rule = "hill" only; the default is roche_truncation_radius.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.roche_truncation_radius — Function
roche_truncation_radius(d, M_self, M_other) -> Float64

Volume-equivalent radius of the Roche lobe of a body of mass M_self with a companion of mass M_other at separation d (Eggleton 1983, DOI 10.1086/160960), with q = M_self / M_other:

\[r_L = d \, \frac{0.49\, q^{2/3}}{0.6\, q^{2/3} + \ln(1 + q^{1/3})}\]

accurate to 1 % for every q. Unlike the Hill radius (jacobi_radius), valid for M_self ≪ M_other only, the lobe stays inside the inner Lagrange point at comparable masses (0.38 d at q = 1, against the Lagrange point at 0.5 d), so a truncated pair does not start with stars beyond the saddle of the joint potential. Throws an ArgumentError unless both masses are positive.

Example

roche_truncation_radius(8.0, 1000.0, 1000.0)   # ≈ 3.03
source
Nbody6Dynamics.orbit_apocentre — Function
orbit_apocentre(orbit::OrbitSpec, rh1::Real) -> Float64

Apocentre separation [pc] of a Kepler-mode orbit, with rh1 the input half-mass radius of cluster 1 [pc] and e = orbit.eccentricity: apocentre_rh > 0 gives apocentre_rh rh1; else pericentre > 0 gives pericentre (1 + e) / (1 − e); else pericentre_rh > 0 gives pericentre_rh rh1 (1 + e) / (1 − e); else apocentre. Throws an ArgumentError for rh1 ≤ 0.

Example

orbit_apocentre(OrbitSpec(; pericentre_rh = 2.0, eccentricity = 0.5), 2.0)   # 12.0
source
Nbody6Dynamics.two_cluster_orbit_quantities — Function
two_cluster_orbit_quantities(M1, M2, d_apo, ecc, rh1; sigma1 = NaN, e_total = NaN,
                             r_out = (NaN, NaN)) -> NamedTuple

Derived quantities of a two-cluster Kepler orbit started at apocentre, in pc, km s⁻¹, M☉ and Myr with G = _G_PC_KMS2_MSUN [pc km² s⁻² M☉⁻¹].

Arguments

  • M1, M2: cluster masses [M☉]; > 0
  • d_apo: apocentre separation [pc]; > 0
  • ecc: eccentricity, 0 ≤ e < 1
  • rh1: half-mass radius of cluster 1 [pc]; > 0
  • sigma1: velocity dispersion of cluster 1 [km s⁻¹] (3-D, mass-weighted rms speed about the mean)
  • e_total: total energy of the configuration, internal plus orbital [M☉ km² s⁻²]
  • r_out: outer radii of the two clusters [pc]

Returns

With M = M1 + M2, μ = M1 M2 / M, a = d_apo / (1 + e), r_p = a (1 − e), p = a (1 − e²):

  • a_pc = a, apocentre_pc = d_apo, pericentre_pc = r_p [pc]
  • apocentre_rh = d_apo / rh1, pericentre_rh = r_p / rh1
  • period_myr = 2π √(a³ / (G M)), converted from pc / (km s⁻¹) to Myr
  • e_orb = −G M1 M2 / (2a), orbital energy [M☉ km² s⁻²]
  • l_orb = μ √(G M p), orbital angular momentum [M☉ pc km s⁻¹]
  • j_orb = √(p / rh1), specific orbital angular momentum in units of √(G M rh1)
  • v_peri_kms = √(G M (1 + e) / r_p), relative speed at pericentre [km s⁻¹]
  • v_peri_over_sigma = v_peri_kms / sigma1
  • lambda_peebles = l_orb √|e_total| / (G M^{5/2}), the spin parameter of Peebles (1969, DOI 10.1086/149876) of the orbital angular momentum; with l in M☉ pc km s⁻¹, E in M☉ km² s⁻² and G in pc km² s⁻² M☉⁻¹ the numerator and the denominator both carry M☉^{3/2} pc km² s⁻², so it is dimensionless; NaN when e_total is NaN
  • roche_lobe_pc = (R_L,1, R_L,2), Roche-lobe radii at apocentre after Eggleton (1983, DOI 10.1086/160960), R_L,i = d_apo 0.49 q^{2/3} / (0.6 q^{2/3} + ln(1 + q^{1/3})) with q = M_i / M_other [pc]
  • roche_filling = (r_out[1] / R_L,1, r_out[2] / R_L,2)

Throws an ArgumentError for non-positive masses, d_apo or rh1, and for e outside [0, 1).

Example

q = two_cluster_orbit_quantities(1000.0, 1000.0, 12.0, 0.5, 2.0; sigma1 = 2.0)
q.pericentre_pc   # 4.0
source
Nbody6Dynamics.tidal_frame_rotation — Function
tidal_frame_rotation(tidal::TidalSpec) -> Float64

Angular velocity Ω [km s⁻¹ pc⁻¹] of the frame in which the engine integrates under tidal.kz14, along +z: A − B with the Oort constants of xtrnl0.F (A = 14.4, B = −12.0 km s⁻¹ kpc⁻¹) for kz14 = 1, √(G M_G / R_G³) of the circular orbit in the point-mass galaxy for kz14 = 2 (gmg [M☉], rg0 [kpc]); 0 for the inertial integrations (kz14 = 0, 5). The Coriolis terms of xtrnld.f (Fx += 2Ω ẏ, Fy −= 2Ω ẋ) fix the sense: a pair orbit with its angular momentum along +z is prograde with respect to the Galactic rotation.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.pair_jacobi_radius — Function
pair_jacobi_radius(M, tidal::TidalSpec) -> Float64

Jacobi radius [pc] of a bound system of total mass M [M☉] in the linearised tide of the engine (Hill problem): (G M / (4A(A − B)))^{1/3} for kz14 = 1 (≈ 1.41 (M/M☉)^{1/3} pc) and (G M / (3Ω²))^{1/3} for the point-mass galaxy of kz14 = 2 (A = 3Ω/4, B = −Ω/4); Inf without a rotating-frame tide. A pair whose apocentre reaches it is not bound to itself.

Nbody6Dynamics.to_rotating_frame! — Function
to_rotating_frame!(pos, vel, Ω)

Replace inertial velocities vel [km s⁻¹] of bodies at pos [pc] (3 × N, about the rotation axis through the origin) by the velocities in the frame rotating with Ω ẑ [km s⁻¹ pc⁻¹]: v − Ω × r, i.e. vₓ + Ω y, v_y − Ω x.

Nbody6Dynamics.complex_member_masses — Function
complex_member_masses(spec::ComplexSpec, rng::AbstractRNG) -> Vector{Float64}

Member masses [M☉] of a complex: n_members equal masses mass_total / n_members ("equal"), or draws from dN/dM ∝ M^−β on [mass_lo, mass_hi] ("power_law"), either n_members of them or as many as it takes for the sum to reach mass_total (the last draw kept). Throws an ArgumentError for an unknown mass function or a count/target combination the mass function does not take.

Nbody6Dynamics.complex_centres — Function
complex_centres(spec::ComplexSpec, n::Int, rng::AbstractRNG)
    -> (pos::Matrix{Float64}, vel::Matrix{Float64})

Positions [pc] (3 × n) of the member centres from the distribution spec.centres ("uniform_sphere", "plummer", "fractal", "filament"; ComplexSpec) and their raw velocities (3 × n, arbitrary units, to be scaled by complex_scale_velocities!). Nbody6Dynamics.complex_scale_velocities! — Function
complex_scale_velocities!(vel, pos, mass; virial_ratio, rotation_fraction = 0.0,
                          softening) -> NamedTuple

Scale the bulk velocities vel [km s⁻¹] (3 × n) of point masses mass [M☉] at pos [pc] (3 × n) to the virial ratio Q = virial_ratio of the softened point-mass system, with the fraction f = rotation_fraction of the kinetic energy in a solid-body rotation about +z. In place:

  1. pos and vel are shifted to their mass-weighted means.
  2. W = −Σ_{i<j} G mᵢ mⱼ / √(|rᵢ − rⱼ|² + ε²), ε = softening > 0.
  3. Q = 0 sets vel to zero (f must be 0).
  4. Otherwise, with wᵢ = ẑ × rᵢ, vel loses its mass-weighted projection on w, vel ← vel − (Σ m v·w / Σ m |w|²) w, and becomes α vel + β w with α = √((1 − f) Q |W| / T_v), β = √(f Q |W| / T_w), where T_v = ½ Σ m |v|² and T_w = ½ Σ m |w|².

Returns (virial_ratio = T/|W|, e_total = T + W, l_total = (Lx, Ly, Lz), rotation_fraction = β² T_w / T, kinetic = T, potential = W, softening = ε) of the final velocities, energies in M☉ km² s⁻², L = Σ m r × v in M☉ pc km s⁻¹. Throws an ArgumentError for ε ≤ 0, Q < 0, f outside [0, 1), f > 0 with Q = 0 or with all members on the z axis, fewer than two members, and vanishing velocities to scale (T_v = 0).

Example

out = complex_scale_velocities!(vel, pos, mass; virial_ratio = 0.5, softening = 0.5)
out.virial_ratio   # 0.5
source

Output

Nbody6Dynamics.write_dat10 — Function
write_dat10(path, mass, pos, vel)

Write a dat.10 particle file for Nbody6++ (KZ(22)=2).

Each line: MASS X Y Z VX VY VZ

All values must be in N-body units (G=1, M_total=1).

source
Nbody6Dynamics.generate_merger_inp — Function
generate_merger_inp(path, N_total, rbar, zmbar; nbody6, stellar = StellarSpec(),
                    kz14 = 0, tcrit, dtadj, deltat, nrand, mass_bounds = (0.08, 100.0),
                    imbh = nothing)

Generate an Nbody6++ .inp file configured for external particle input via dat.10 in N-body units (KZ(22) = 2, the only supported input mode).

Follows the Fortran NAMELIST read order expected by nbody6.F → start.F: &INNBODY6 → &ININPUT → &INSSE → &INBSE → &INCOLL → &INDATA → &INSCALE (→ &INXTRNL0 if KZ(14) = 2, 5) (→ the list-directed IMBHINIT line BIMBH XIMBH(1:3) VIMBH(1:3) DTBH if KZ(24) = 1, or the lines NIMBHS DTBH and the NIMBHS IMBH names if KZ(24) = 3).

imbh selects one of two IMBH routes by its route field; either sets KZ(24) with the other fixed options (before the kz overrides), and an unknown route raises an ArgumentError.

(route = :kz24, mass_nb, pos_nb, vel_nb, dtbh_nb), holding the last dat.10 row (the IMBH, N-body units) and the mbh.45 interval DTBH [NB], sets KZ(24) = 1 and writes the IMBHINIT line in the units the engine reads it (M☉, pc, km s⁻¹). The engine overwrites particle N with BODY(N) = BIMBH/ZMBAR, X = XIMBH/RBAR, XDOT = VIMBH/VSTAR, VSTAR = 1e-5 √(GM/PC) √(ZMASS ZMBAR/RBAR), where under KZ(22) = 2 its ZMBAR is already the mass scale ZMBAR N / ZMASS (start.F), not the mean mass written to &ININPUT. With rbar_w, zmbar_w the values of the RBAR=%.6f, ZMBAR=%.6f fields and zmass_w = zmbar_w N_total (ZMASS = 1), the line carries BIMBH = mass_nb zmass_w, XIMBH = pos_nb rbar_w and VIMBH = vel_nb VSTAR(zmass_w, rbar_w), so the engine's conversion returns the dat.10 state to rounding (an identity overwrite).

(route = :flag, names::Vector{Int}, dtbh_nb) sets KZ(24) = 3 (engine fork ≥ v2026.07.11) and writes, at the same position, the line NIMBHS DTBH (length(names) and dtbh_nb as %.15e) and then the names separated by single spaces on one line. The engine keeps the dat.10 mass, position and velocity of these bodies and flags them as IMBHs (type 14). names must hold 1 to 16 distinct names in 1:N_total.

An IMBH under stellar evolution (stellar.kz19 ≥ 3) needs the Peters decay of KZ(27) = 3 on either route; a smaller final KZ(27) is refused.

Key settings

  • nbody6: resolved Nbody6ParameterSpec; obtain it from resolve_nbody6_parameters — unresolved zeros are refused. Its binary_physics = "full" writes the option set of _FULL_BINARY_KZ over the inherited block, then its kz overrides are applied; a resulting KZ(46) > 0 warns. checkpoint = true sets KZ(2) = 1 (a restart dump at every adjustment) and logs the disk space the dumps can take.
  • stellar: StellarSpec (KZ(19), Level, ZMET, EPOCH0, DTPLOT); KZ(12) HR diagnostics are switched off with kz19 = 0
  • tidal: TidalSpec (KZ(14) and the &INXTRNL0 namelist for options 2 and 5)
  • mass_bounds: (BODYN, BODY1) of &INDATA, inert under KZ(22) = 2 but kept consistent with the sampled population
source
Nbody6Dynamics.engine_interval — Function
engine_interval(dt; resolution = 1/128, max_digits = 9) -> Float64

dt rounded to the nearest dyadic rational m 2⁻ᵏ whose spacing 2⁻ᵏ is at most resolution × dt, with k ≤ max_digits. Such a value has an exact decimal expansion of k digits, so the engine's digit counter (_engine_digit_counter_terminates) stops after k steps, and it is commensurate with the engine's power-of-two block time steps. The cap of nine digits is the engine's as well: string_left.f formats a count of at most ten with an I1 descriptor, so exactly ten digits produce an invalid format and a runtime error at the next output. Applied to DTADJ, DELTAT and DTPLOT before they are written to merger.inp; the change is below resolution / 2 unless max_digits binds (intervals below about 2⁻ᵐᵃˣ⁻ᵈⁱᵍⁱᵗˢ × 128). dt ≤ 0 is returned unchanged.

Example

engine_interval(0.6302)   # 0.62890625 = 161/256
engine_interval(0.5)      # 0.5
source
Nbody6Dynamics.sample_binaries — Function
sample_binaries(spec::BinarySpec, masses, rng; zmet = NaN)
    -> (; masses, primary, secondary, m1, m2, a_pc, e, n_guarded)

Pair the stars of masses [M☉]. Without a massive branch (spec.massive.model == "none"), N_b = round(fraction N / (1 + fraction)) of the N stars are paired by spec.pairing, spec.period and spec.eccentricity. With model = "sana2012" the massive primaries are paired first (MassiveBinarySpec), and the same rule then applies to the unpaired stars below massive.m_min, N being their number. With model = "moe2017" each massive primary is a binary with the inner-binary probability of Moe's code and takes its period, mass ratio and eccentricity from the joint distribution of Moe & Di Stefano (2017, DOI 10.3847/1538-4365/aa6fb6) at its mass clamped to 0.8–40 M☉.

With spec.roche_fill_max = φ > 0 every pair, at its final masses, has its period law truncated to a ≥ a_RL = max_i R_i/(φ r_L(q_i)) and its eccentricity law to e ≤ 1 − a_RL/a, so that neither ZAMS component fills more than φ of its Roche lobe at periastron; the radii need the metallicity zmet (finite, 1e-4 ≤ zmet ≤ 0.03). A pair whose a_RL lies beyond the period range raises an ArgumentError.

Returns the (possibly modified, for "uniform_q" and "replace") mass vector, the star indices of the primaries and secondaries (massive pairs first), the component masses [M☉] with m1 ≥ m2, the semi-major axes [pc], the eccentricities, and n_guarded, the number of pairs whose period (or axis) range the guard raised (its eccentricity cap acts on every pair). Every star belongs to at most one pair. Without the massive branch and the guard the draws are those of earlier versions.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.zams_radius — Function
zams_radius(m::Real, z::Real) -> Float64

Zero-age main-sequence radius [R☉] of a star of mass m [M☉] at metallicity z: the rational fit of Tout et al. (1996, DOI 10.1093/mnras/281.1.257) with the coefficients of the engine's SSE package (rzamsf after zcnsts), so that the radii agree with those the engine assigns at t = 0.

Arguments

  • m: stellar mass [M☉]; finite and > 0. The fit is calibrated on 0.1–100 M☉; above 100 M☉ it is an extrapolation.
  • z: metallicity; 1e-4 ≤ z ≤ 0.03, the range of the fit and of the engine.

Throws an ArgumentError outside these bounds.

Example

zams_radius(1.0, 0.02)   # ≈ 0.888 R☉
source
Nbody6Dynamics.expand_binaries — Function
expand_binaries(pos, vel, mass, system_ranges, cluster_binaries, kept; rng)
    -> (; pos, vel, mass, cluster_blocks, n_pairs, hard_fraction)

Turn the combined system arrays (code units, G = 1) into body arrays: every pair becomes two bodies on a Keplerian relative orbit about the system's centre of mass, written first, cluster by cluster, followed by the singles of every cluster (the engine's primordial-pair convention). For cluster i, system_ranges[i] are its systems in the combined arrays, kept[i] their indices in that cluster's sampled system set, and cluster_binaries[i] the sampled pair table (system_binary[j] = pair index of sampled system j, 0 for a single, plus m1, m2, a_pc, e). cluster_blocks[i] are the contiguous body-index blocks of cluster i (its pairs block and its singles block), n_pairs[i] its number of pairs, and hard_fraction[i] the fraction of its pairs with binding energy above ⟨m⟩ σ² of the cluster's systems.

rng is one AbstractRNG for all clusters, drawn from cluster by cluster, or a vector of one RNG per cluster, cluster i drawing from rng[i].

source
Nbody6Dynamics.crossing_time — Function
crossing_time(M, E) -> Float64

Crossing time M^{5/2} / (2|E|)^{3/2} of a self-gravitating system of mass M and total energy E < 0 in G = 1 units (the engine's TCR, scale.F/adjust.F); NaN for E ≥ 0.

Nbody6Dynamics.resolve_nbody6_parameters — Function
resolve_nbody6_parameters(spec, clusters, cluster_ranges, N_total, rbar_pc)
    -> Nbody6ParameterSpec

Fill the zero entries of spec from its preset and the member clusters, in N-body units of the combined system (length unit rbar_pc). With r_h the smallest member half-mass radius in those units, N_min the smallest post-truncation membership, and ρ̂ the central density contrast of that cluster's profile (_central_density_contrast):

Key"engine""baseline"
ETAI0.020.01
ETAR0.020.02
ETAU0.10.1
NNBOPTclamp(round(√N_total), 20, 300)100
RS0min(2 r_h (2 NNBOPT / N_min)^{1/3}, r_h)the same
RMIN4 r_h / (N_min ρ̂^{1/3})12 r_h / N_min
DTMIN0.04 √(ETAI/0.02) √(RMIN³ N_total)0.016 √(ETAI/0.02) √(RMIN³ N_total)
  • RS0 is twice the radius enclosing about NNBOPT stars at the mean density of the half-mass sphere, capped at r_h. The factor 2 matches the engine's own example inputs (RS0 ≈ 0.5 r_h for NNBOPT ≈ √N). The engine regrows an empty neighbour sphere itself (fpoly0.F), so the radius is a matter of start-up cost, not of correctness; the start-up hang once attributed to it was the interval digit counter (engine_interval).
  • RMIN of "engine" is the functional form of the engine's own re-derivation (adjust.F) evaluated for the member cluster instead of the whole configuration; that of "baseline" is three times the 90° deflection scale 4 r_h / N_min.
  • DTMIN is the Aarseth close-encounter step with the mean body mass 1/N_total ("engine"), or 0.4 of it ("baseline"), at the resolved RMIN; it uses the resolved ETAI.

User-supplied (nonzero) values are kept. The result has no zero entries.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.to_nbody_units! — Function
to_nbody_units!(mass, pos, vel, M_total_solar, rbar_pc)

Convert from physical units (M☉, pc, km/s) to N-body units (G = 1, Mtotal = 1, length unit `rbarpc`) in-place.

The callers pass the half-mass radius of the whole initial configuration as rbar_pc, and the engine reads the result with KZ(22) = 2, which skips its rescaling to E = -1/4. The total energy is therefore not -1/4: an isolated King W₀ = 6 cluster starts near -0.20 (rV ≈ 1.25 rh), and a bound pair lower still, its orbital binding energy adding to the total.

N-body scaling

  • Mass: m_nb = m_solar / M_total_solar
  • Length: r_nb = r_pc / rbar_pc (rbar = length unit in pc)
  • Velocity: v_nb = v_kms / vstar where vstar = sqrt(G M_total / rbar) in km/s, i.e. _CODE_VSTAR_KMS × sqrt(M_total / rbar).
source

Nbody6Dynamics.parse_merger_summary — Function
parse_merger_summary(path::AbstractString) -> Vector{Vector{Int}}

Read merger_summary.txt and return the body indices (dat.10 order, 1-based) of every initial cluster from the post-truncation body counts and, when present, the pair counts: pairs of all clusters come first, cluster by cluster, then the singles, so a cluster with binaries owns two contiguous blocks. Returns an empty vector if the file cannot be parsed.

Nbody6Dynamics.per_cluster_virial — Function
per_cluster_virial(snaps, cluster_ranges; bound_only = true)
    -> (Q::Matrix{Float64}, n_mem::Matrix{Int})

Internal virial ratio of every initial cluster at every snapshot, Q[i, k] for cluster i at snapshot k (NaN with fewer than 3 selected members). By default only the members bound to the cluster enter T and W (bound_set, which takes max_passes and min_members); bound_only = false uses every present member and is dominated by kicked remnants after the first supernovae. One O(N_i²) pair sum per cluster and snapshot.

bound_fraction(snap::Snapshot; max_passes = 100, min_members = 10) -> Float64

Mass fraction of the stars bound to the whole system in its own frame (iterative energetic unbinding with regularised pairs at their centres of mass, bound_set): a snapshot-based escaper measure independent of the engine's escape sphere. One O(N²) pair sum plus O(N·N_removed) per pass.

Nbody6Dynamics.plot_cluster_structure — Function
plot_cluster_structure(snaps, cluster_ranges, cfg; lagr = nothing,
                       filename = "merger_cluster_structure")

Two stacked panels sharing the time axis: the half-mass radius of every initial cluster measured about its own centre from its bound members (cluster_structure), and the bound mass fraction. With lagr given, the engine's global 50 % Lagrangian radius about its single density centre is overlaid dashed grey on the radius panel, which makes the difference between the configuration and its members visible. Nbody6Dynamics.ClusterStructure — Type
ClusterStructure

Per-cluster structural quantities at every snapshot, all by original membership (cluster_ranges of merger_ic.toml) and in N-body units. Arrays are n_clusters × n_times; NaN marks clusters with fewer than _MIN_MEMBERS bound members.

Fields

  • time: snapshot times [NB]
  • n_members: members present in the snapshot
  • n_bound: members bound to the cluster (negative energy in its own frame)
  • bound_mass_fraction: bound mass over present member mass
  • centre: 3 × n_clusters × n_times shrinking-sphere centre of the bound members
  • r_lagr: 3 × n_clusters × n_times radii enclosing 10 %, 50 %, 90 % of the bound mass
  • sigma_1d: one-dimensional velocity dispersion of the bound members [NB]
  • q_virial: T/|W| of the bound members (COM velocity subtracted)

Nbody6Dynamics.cluster_structure — Function
cluster_structure(snaps, cluster_ranges; settings = PostprocessConfig(), bound_only = true,
                  max_passes = 100, min_members = 10) -> ClusterStructure

Structure of every initial cluster at every snapshot, measured about the cluster's own shrinking-sphere centre from the members it binds (bound_only = false uses all present members and reproduces the plain membership statistics). The bound selection removes tidally stripped stars and kicked stellar remnants, which otherwise dominate the velocity dispersion and the virial ratio after the first supernovae.

Regularised pairs enter the bound selection, sigma_1d and q_virial as their centres of mass; max_passes and min_members go to bound_set. The centre takes its stop rule and step from the centre keys of settings, a PostprocessConfig. One O(N_i²) pair sum per cluster and snapshot.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.RadialProfile — Type
RadialProfile

Radial structure of one particle set about a centre on two shell sets.

Kinematic shells, of equal membership (counts differing by at most one):

  • r: member-median radius of each shell
  • r_edges: shell boundaries (length(r) + 1), midpoints between consecutive shell members, from 0 to the outer radius
  • rho: shell density ΔM / (4π/3 (r_{k+1}³ − r_k³))
  • sigma_r, sigma_t: radial and one-dimensional tangential velocity dispersions of the shell about its mean flow, with velocities taken relative to the set's centre-of-mass velocity (σ_t² = ½ ⟨|v_t − ⟨v_t⟩|²⟩); NaN in shells with fewer than n_min_sigma members
  • beta: anisotropy 1 − σ_t²/σ_r² (0 isotropic, 1 radial); NaN in shells with fewer than n_min_beta members
  • n: members per shell
  • M, r_h: total mass and half-mass radius of the set
  • sigma_err: relative error of σ_r, (2(n − 1))^{-1/2}; NaN where σ_r is
  • beta_err: standard error of β, (1 − β)(3/n)^{1/2}; NaN where β is

Logarithmic density shells:

  • r_log: mass-weighted mean radius of each shell
  • r_log_edges: shell boundaries (length(r_log) + 1)
  • rho_log: shell density, shells merged outward until each holds at least n_min_density members
  • n_log: members per shell
source
Nbody6Dynamics.radial_profile — Function
radial_profile(pos, vel, mass, centre; nbins = 12, n_min_inner = 20,
               mass_fraction_max = 0.99, n_min_sigma = 50, n_min_beta = 300,
               n_min_density = 100) -> RadialProfile

Shell profile of the particles about centre (N-body or any consistent units), out to the radius r_max enclosing mass_fraction_max of the mass.

Kinematic shells: the particles with d ≤ r_max are split into max(1, min(nbins, N_used ÷ n_min_sigma)) shells of equal membership, so that the statistical error is the same in every shell. Velocity dispersions carry the relative error δσ/σ = (2(n − 1))^{−1/2} and the anisotropy the standard error σ_β = (1 − β)(3/n)^{1/2}; about 300 members give β to ±0.1 near isotropy, hence the default n_min_beta. sigma_r and sigma_t are NaN below n_min_sigma members, beta below n_min_beta.

Logarithmic density shells: nbins shells whose outer edges are log-spaced between the radius enclosing n_min_inner particles and r_max (the innermost shell is the sphere inside the first edge). From the inside out, each shell is merged with its outer neighbour until it holds at least n_min_density members; a short outermost remainder joins the shell inside it, and a set with fewer than n_min_density particles inside r_max has a single shell.

Arguments

  • pos, vel: 3 × N positions and velocities; mass: N masses
  • centre: reference point, e.g. shrinking_sphere_centre
  • nbins ≥ 2: maximum number of kinematic shells, number of logarithmic shells before merging
  • n_min_inner: particles inside the first logarithmic edge (N ≥ 2 n_min_inner)
  • mass_fraction_max: enclosed mass fraction defining r_max
  • n_min_sigma ≥ 2, n_min_beta ≥ n_min_sigma, n_min_density ≥ 1: membership thresholds above

Example

c = shrinking_sphere_centre(pos, mass).centre
p = radial_profile(pos, vel, mass, c)
lines(p.r, p.beta)
source

cluster_profiles(snap, cluster_ranges; settings = PostprocessConfig(), bound_only = true,

                 n_min_beta = settings.profile_n_min_beta,
                 n_min_density = settings.profile_n_min_density,
                 max_passes = 100, min_members = 10)
    -> Vector{Union{Nothing,RadialProfile}}

Radial profile of every initial cluster about its own shrinking-sphere centre from its bound members (cluster_structure conventions; max_passes and min_members go to bound_set, the shell thresholds to radial_profile); nothing for clusters with too few members. The centre takes its stop rule and step from the centre keys of settings, a PostprocessConfig, whose profile keys are also the defaults of the shell thresholds; an explicit keyword overrides them.

Nbody6Dynamics.plot_cluster_separation — Function
plot_cluster_separation(snaps, cluster_ranges, cfg;
                        filename = "merger_cluster_separation")

Plot the separation of the density centres of every pair of initial clusters against time, with the half-mass threshold min(r_h,i, r_h,j) below which the pair counts as merged, both from coalescence_time. For nclusters ≤ 5 every pair is shown individually, its threshold dashed in the same colour; for more clusters the min/max envelope and mean of the separations are drawn with the mean threshold dashed and the number of distinct clusters (`ndistinct) on a twin axis. Separations are shown in pc and times in Myr whencfg.units == "physical"` (header AS scaling).

The coalescence time of coalescence_time is marked when the clusters coalesce within the snapshots. After that epoch the initial cluster decomposition is no longer physically meaningful.

source
Nbody6Dynamics.plot_cluster_virial — Function
plot_cluster_virial(snaps, cluster_ranges, cfg;
                    filename = "merger_cluster_virial")

Plot the internal virial ratio Qi(t) of each initial cluster. For nclusters ≤ 5 every cluster is shown individually; for larger N, a min/max envelope with the mean is drawn. A reference line at Q = 0.5 marks virial equilibrium. The time axis is in Myr when cfg.units == "physical" (Q is dimensionless).

Interpretation note: the metric tracks particles by their original cluster ID and, by default, only those still bound to that group (per_cluster_virial(...; bound_only = true)), so stripped stars and kicked stellar remnants do not enter. It is physically meaningful before coalescence (which clusters are still in virial equilibrium, which undergo violent relaxation). After merger the bound subset of an ID-group is whatever remains self-bound inside the remnant, so the curve loses its meaning as a cluster diagnostic; the departure from Q ≈ 0.5 is a rough proxy for the time of coalescence. bound passes max_passes and min_members to bound_set.

source

Internals referenced from the public docstrings

Nbody6Dynamics._engine_digit_counter_terminates — Function
_engine_digit_counter_terminates(dt) -> Bool

Whether the engine's string_left.f terminates on the interval dt. That routine, called at every output (output.F), COMMON dump (mydump.F) and stellar-evolution record (hrplot.F) with DELTAT, DTADJ and DTPLOT, counts the decimal digits of dt by multiplying by ten until dtmp - int(dtmp) is zero, with a default-kind int. A value whose binary representation never lands on an integer under repeated multiplication (0.6302, 0.1576, …) grows past 2³¹, where the conversion overflows, and the loop never ends: the engine sits at 100 % CPU inside its first output and never prints a second adjustment. Emulated here in the same arithmetic.

source
Nbody6Dynamics._validate — Function
_validate(cfg::Nbody6Config)

Validate a parsed configuration against the constraints documented in config.toml and the manual: enumerated choices, numerical bounds, required non-empty fields, and the combinations that cannot be honoured (simulation.run_test with a postprocess.data_dir, postprocess.binary_spans without postprocess.parse_stdout). Raises an ArgumentError naming the offending section.key and the actual value; returns nothing on success. Called unconditionally at the end of load_config so a pipeline cannot start from a configuration it cannot honor.

File-existence checks (input files, merger config paths) remain at runtime: those paths may be created by earlier pipeline phases.

source
Nbody6Dynamics._kinetic_and_potential — Function
_kinetic_and_potential(mass, pos, vel) -> (T, W)

Kinetic energy T = Σ ½ m v² and exact pairwise potential energy W = −Σ_{i<j} m_i m_j / r_ij with G = 1. The O(N²) pair sum is threaded over strided rows so each task sees a balanced mix of long (small i) and short (large i) rows; the summation order, and hence the last bits of W, depend on Threads.nthreads().

source
Nbody6Dynamics._central_density_contrast — Function
_central_density_contrast(profile::DensityProfile) -> Float64

Central density over the mean density inside the half-mass radius, ρ₀ / ρ̄(< r_h). Plummer: 2 (r_h/a)³ ≈ 4.45 analytically. King: from the solved model, with r_h located on the cumulative-mass profile. Rotating King: from the solved axisymmetric model (ρ₀ = 1 in its units, M̂ and the spherical half-mass radius r_h of RotatingKingModel).

source
Nbody6Dynamics._sweep_point_outcome — Function

Outcome fields of one finished point read from its run directory: elapsed time, exit status and completion (END RUN seen, whether or not the engine exited by itself) from RUN_INFO.toml, final time, star and pair counts, energy error and virial ratio from the last ADJUST record. Missing files leave NaN/-1/false entries.

Nbody6Dynamics._run_series — Function
_run_series(run_dir, quantity; fraction = 0.5) -> Union{Nothing, Tuple}

Time series (t_myr, y) of one run for quantity: :lagrangian (radius of mass fraction fraction [pc]), :energy (|ΔE/E| per adjustment, zeros dropped), :n_stars and :n_pairs (from the ADJUST records). nothing when the source file is absent or empty.

source
Nbody6Dynamics._control_time_factor — Function
_control_time_factor(m::AbstractDict, Ns, rbars, rbar_control) -> Float64

Ratio of the estimated N-body time units of the merger configuration and of its control, (RBAR_est / rbar_control)^{3/2}: the total masses are equal, so only the length units differ. RBAR_est adds the N-weighted mean member radius to the half-mass radius of the centres of mass — for a Kepler orbit apocentre × N_min / N_total, for explicit positions their N-weighted RMS distance from the N-weighted centre.

source
Nbody6Dynamics._local_density — Function
_local_density(pos, mass; k = _DENSITY_NEIGHBOURS, weighting = :mass) -> Vector{Float64}

Casertano & Hut (1985, doi:10.1086/163485) density estimate at every particle: the mass of its k − 1 nearest neighbours divided by the volume of the sphere reaching the k-th (weighting = :mass), or the number density (k − 1) / (4π/3 r_k³) (weighting = :number, the proxy behind the engine's XNDBL). O(N²).
